### [1] [Learning Low-Order Approximations of the Quark Propagator in Lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06612)
*Simon Pfahler,Maren Käfferlein,Daniel Knüttel,Christoph Lehner,Tilo Wettig*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces a gauge-equivariant neural network in the Clifford basis to discover interpretable, convergent quark propagator approximations that outperform and extend beyond the divergent hopping expansion in lattice QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The hopping expansion—a standard quark propagator approximation in lattice QCD—diverges for light quarks, limiting strong-coupling and finite-density simulations.

Method: Uses a gauge-equivariant neural network operating in the Clifford algebra basis to learn physically interpretable, low-order approximations of the quark propagator directly from gauge-field data.

Result: Yields improved low-order approximations that outperform the hopping expansion at equal computational cost; identifies convergent behavior where hopping expansion fails; generalizes across unseen gauge configurations and lattice volumes.

Conclusion: Provides plug-in-replaceable, theoretically insightful approximations and suggests a novel convergent series expansion for the quark propagator, advancing both computational and analytical approaches in lattice QCD.

Abstract: The hopping expansion is an important approximation of the quark propagator in lattice quantum chromodynamics, but it diverges for light quarks, which limits the scope of strong-coupling and finite-density simulations. We present an artificial scientific discovery framework using gauge-equivariant neural networks in the Clifford basis to extract interpretable approximations. Our approach yields improved low-order approximations that outperform the hopping expansion at identical computational cost. In the parameter regime where the hopping expansion fails, our approach identifies convergent behavior, thus suggesting a novel series expansion. The discovered approximations generalize to unseen gauge fields and volumes, enabling immediate implementation as plug-in replacements and a new theoretical understanding of quark-propagator expansions.

</details>

### [2] [Signature Failure and Minimum Spatial Quanta: Shear-Induced Quantum Boundaries for Gravitational Path Integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03732)
*Edward J. Shaya*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether quantum metric fluctuations eliminate the smooth Lorentzian structure of spacetime, proposing instead a single, causally connected 4D geometry. Using a graviton-based metric ensemble with UV cutoff and lattice simulations, it identifies a minimal scale q_min ≈ 1.9 ℓ_P. It then computes quantum boundary radii for Schwarzschild (r_qb ∼ 10⁻²² m) and radiation-dominated (ρ_qb/ρ_P ∼ 0.013/C_R) geometries, suggesting finite-curvature quantum boundaries may replace classical singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the incompatibility between smooth Lorentzian spacetime geometry (required by general relativity) and quantum metric fluctuations (expected near Planck scale), by testing whether differentiability and causal structure can survive quantum gravity effects.

Method: Extrapolates Gaussian transverse-traceless graviton vacuum to an additive metric ensemble with physical Fourier cutoff k_max = 1/q; employs a lattice model with local repair and selection of differentiable-state components; computes curvature-induced deformations using Weyl (Schwarzschild) and Ricci (radiation) responses under linearized de Donder gauge; determines quantum boundary scales via numerical simulation and analytic estimation.

Result: Simulated minimum surviving differentiable scale: q_min ≈ 1.9 ℓ_P; quantum black hole boundary radius r_qb ≈ 2.62 C_W^{1/3} (r_g ℓ_P²)^{1/3} ≈ 1.91×10⁻²² C_W^{1/3} (M/M_⊙)^{1/3} m at q ≈ 2.5 ℓ_P; quantum radiation energy density ratio ρ_qb/ρ_P ≈ 1.31×10⁻² / C_R at same q; local homogeneity criterion is time-reversal symmetric.

Conclusion: Quantum metric fluctuations do not destroy global differentiability and causal connectivity if restricted to a single continuing 4D geometry; instead, they induce finite-curvature quantum boundaries—replacing classical singularities—with model-dependent thresholds near ~2–2.5 ℓ_P.

Abstract: We investigate whether quantum metric fluctuations can destroy the differentiable Lorentzian support required for spacetime. We conjecture that non-Lorentzian metric elements are absent from the physical manifold and restrict admissible histories to a single continuing, causally connected four-dimensional geometry. To explore this proposal, we extrapolate the Gaussian transverse-traceless graviton vacuum to an additive metric ensemble with physical Fourier cutoff $k_{\max}=1/q$. A lattice model with local repair and selection of periodically winding differentiable-state components gives a minimum surviving scale $q_{\min}\simeq1.9\,\ell_{\rm P}$ over the simulated duration. We then examine two complementary curvature-induced deformations. For the Schwarzschild electric Weyl field, the smallest boundary radius at the center of a black hole, occurring when $q\simeq2.5\,\ell_{\rm P}$, is $r_{\rm qb}\simeq2.62\,C_W^{1/3}(r_g\ell_{\rm P}^2)^{1/3} \simeq1.91\times10^{-22}\, C_W^{1/3}\left(\rm M/\rm M_\odot\right)^{1/3}\mathrm{m}$, where $r_g=GM/c^2$ and $C_W$ parameterizes the finite-$q$ Weyl response. For homogeneous radiation, a supplementary Gaussian four-metric ensemble subject to linearized de Donder conditions gives $ρ_{\rm qb}/ρ_{\rm P}\simeq1.31\times10^{-2}/C_R$ at $q=2.5\,\ell_{\rm P}$, where $C_R$ is the corresponding Ricci-response coefficient. The local homogeneous criterion is unchanged under reversal of the expansion rate. These model-dependent thresholds motivate a possible replacement of classical singular continuations by finite-curvature quantum boundaries.

</details>


### [3] [Scalar decoupling and critical-curve invariance in a reduced MOG black-hole theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03751)
*Nikko John Leo S. Lobos,Emmanuel T. Rodulfo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes linear perturbations of a Schwarzschild-MOG black hole, showing decoupling into scalar and gravito-vector sectors via a field redefinition; the scalar sector is stable and yields conformal metric perturbations with vanishing critical-curve transfer coefficients (except for a charged axial dipole), while the vector sector produces a charge-dependent, low-frequency metric response distinguishable from stationary rotation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether scalar excitations in Modified Gravity (MOG) can produce observable breathing-mode deformations of black hole photon spheres or critical curves—specifically testing consistency with strong-field observational signatures (e.g., EHT)—and to isolate genuine vector (gravito-electromagnetic) contributions from spurious gauge or conformal effects.

Method: Uses linearized field equations in reduced strong-field MOG; applies field redefinition $\widehat h_{\mu\nu}=h_{\mu\nu}-\psi \bar g_{\mu\nu}$ to decouple scalar ($\psi$) and gravito-vector sectors; imposes spectral boundary conditions for stability; computes transfer coefficients between scalar perturbations and critical curve deformations; constructs horizon-regular metric perturbations via first-order radial integrals; employs matched asymptotics and compares against Kerr–Newman benchmark for stationary shifts.

Result: Scalar-only perturbations induce purely conformal metric changes with null Hamiltonian contribution and vanish identically on angular critical curves (including monopole breathing) under smooth positive conformal factors preserving the capture boundary; only the charged axial dipole generates a nontrivial, constrained metric perturbation; its low-frequency vector-induced response scales as $\omega^2$ and is explicitly charge-dependent; the neutral limit recovers known Kerr shift $\delta C_b = -2a_{\rm rot}$, confirming separation from pure rotation.

Conclusion: Linear scalar breathing modes of the photon sphere/critical curve are rigorously excluded in Schwarzschild-MOG under physically admissible conformal mappings; observed low-frequency metric responses must arise from gravito-vector (charge- or current-related) degrees of freedom—not scalar field dynamics—thus providing a clean theoretical discriminant for MOG vs. GR+scalar extensions in strong-field tests.

Abstract: Linear perturbations of a Schwarzschild-MOG black hole separate into scalar and gravito-vector sectors in the reduced strong-field theory at leading order in $μ\bar G M\ll1$. The field redefinition $\widehat h_{μν}=h_{μν}-ψ\bar g_{μν}$ gives a decoupled Klein--Gordon equation for the scalar and the Reissner--Nordström equations for the gravito-vector sector. The scalar is stable under the stated spectral boundary conditions for $m_G^2\ge0$. A scalar-only excitation produces a conformal metric perturbation with null Hamiltonian contribution $H_1^{(s)}=-ψH_0$. For fixed observer worldlines and conformally corresponding orthonormal frames, every linear scalar-to-critical-curve transfer coefficient vanishes, including monopole breathing, provided the conformal factor is smooth, positive, and preserves the capture boundary. The exceptional charged axial dipole instead induces a constrained metric perturbation. A first-order radial integral reconstructs this perturbation in a gauge regular at the future horizon and on the sphere, separating the radiative amplitude from stationary angular momentum without division by frequency. For a unit-incident canonical vector amplitude with zero angular momentum, matched asymptotic solutions give an explicit charge-dependent local metric response that vanishes as $ω^2$ at fixed radius and fixed subextremal background. A separate infinitesimal Kerr--Newman benchmark yields the stationary impact-parameter midpoint shift and recovers $δC_b=-2a_{\rm rot}$ in the neutral limit. These results exclude linear scalar breathing of the specified angular critical curve and distinguish the low-frequency vector-induced metric response from a stationary rotation effect.

</details>


### [4] [On-shell Homogeneous Curvature Modes in Quadratic $F(R,\mathcal G)$ Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03752)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz,B. Mishra,Y. Gómez-Leyton,Jean Báez Cuevas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes homogeneous cosmological perturbations in a quadratic $F(R,\mathcal{G}) = R + \\alpha R^2 + \\beta \\mathcal{G}^2$ gravity model, deriving perturbation equations, identifying key background solutions (dust-like, de Sitter, and big-rip), and assessing their stability—particularly showing de Sitter stability requires $\\alpha < -1/(6H_{\\rm dS}^2)$ and big-rip perturbations contain a divergent mode.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability and dynamical behavior of cosmological backgrounds in higher-curvature gravity beyond general relativity, specifically in the $F(R,\\mathcal{G})$ class where both Ricci scalar $R$ and Gauss–Bonnet term $\\mathcal{G}$ are squared.

Method: Linearization of the modified Friedmann equation coupled with dust energy conservation; asymptotic analysis of background and perturbation equations; stability assessment via mode analysis of the resulting ODEs for Hubble perturbation and matter contrast.

Result: Three distinct vacuum/dust background branches identified: (i) low-curvature dust branch ($H \\sim 2/(3t)$), (ii) stable de Sitter solution at $\\beta H_{\\rm dS}^6 = 1/96$ only if $\\alpha < -1/(6H_{\\rm dS}^2)$, and (iii) high-curvature big-rip branch ($H \\sim 7/(t_s - t)$) with one divergent homogeneous $\\mathcal{G}^2$-dominated perturbation mode. Einstein limit recovers standard GR dust evolution.

Conclusion: The quadratic $F(R,\\mathcal{G})$ model supports rich cosmological phenomenology but imposes strict constraints on parameter space for stability—especially for the de Sitter vacuum—and exhibits pathological (divergent) perturbations near the big-rip singularity, signaling potential breakdown of linear perturbation theory there.

Abstract: 5We study homogeneous cosmological perturbations in the quadratic model $F(R,\mathcal G)=R+αR^2+β\mathcal G^2$. Linearization of the modified Friedmann equation together with dust conservation yields a second-order equation for the homogeneous Hubble perturbation, or equivalently a third-order equation for the matter contrast. We then restrict the asymptotic analysis to backgrounds that satisfy the zeroth-order equations of the same quadratic theory. Besides the low-curvature dust branch, which approaches $H=2/(3t)$, the model admits a nontrivial vacuum de Sitter solution with $βH_{\rm dS}^6=1/96$. Its homogeneous curvature modes are stable only when $α<-1/(6H_{\rm dS}^2)$. At high curvature the background equation also admits a vacuum big-rip asymptotic branch beginning with $H=7/(t_s-t)$; the first corrections are fixed by the quadratic couplings. The corresponding $\mathcal G^2$-dominated perturbation has one divergent homogeneous mode. The simultaneous Einstein limit is taken only as a consistency check and recovers the standard dust behavior.

</details>


### [5] [Electromagnetic quasinormal modes of a Schwarzschild black hole in a superconducting plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03761)
*Bobur Turimov,Akbar Davlataliev,Bobomurat Ahmedov,Chengxun Yuan,Chen Zhou,Sulton Usanov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends London electrodynamics to curved spacetime, deriving covariant electromagnetic wave equations for superconducting media around Schwarzschild black holes; it shows how the London penetration depth modifies quasinormal modes and enables long-lived quasibound states via an effective photon mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model electromagnetic response in superconducting media in strong gravitational fields—particularly near black holes—requiring a generally covariant formulation that respects gauge invariance and incorporates the condensate phase dynamics.

Method: Formulates a gauge-invariant, generally covariant constitutive relation generalizing the London equation; couples it to covariant Maxwell equations; adopts the unitary (London) gauge to recover a Proca-type theory with position-dependent effective photon mass; derives decoupled (axial) and coupled (polar) Schrödinger-like wave equations in Schwarzschild geometry using Regge–Wheeler–Zerilli formalism; imposes quasinormal-mode boundary conditions.

Result: Axial perturbations obey a modified Regge–Wheeler equation with London-mass–dependent potential; polar sector yields two coupled degrees of freedom; London penetration depth redshifts quasinormal frequencies, alters damping rates, and supports quasibound states when the effective photon mass exceeds a threshold.

Conclusion: The framework consistently unifies superconductivity, general relativity, and massive vector field dynamics, enabling analysis of electromagnetic relaxation, compact object interiors with superconducting phases, and black hole environments with effective massive photons.

Abstract: We formulate a generally covariant extension of London electrodynamics in four-dimensional curved spacetime and investigate the propagation of electromagnetic perturbations in a superconducting medium surrounding a black hole. The ordinary London relation is promoted to a gauge-invariant four-dimensional constitutive equation involving the electromagnetic four-potential and the phase of the superconducting condensate. When combined with the covariant Maxwell equations, the resulting system is equivalent, in the London (unitary) gauge, to a Proca theory with an effective photon mass set by the inverse London penetration depth. We derive the corresponding Schrödinger-like wave equations for electromagnetic perturbations in the axial and polar sectors of the Schwarzschild spacetime in the presence of the superconducting plasma. The axial sector is governed by a single Regge--Wheeler-type equation whose effective potential contains the London mass term, whereas the polar sector contains two coupled physical degrees of freedom. Quasinormal-mode boundary conditions are formulated for the Schwarzschild geometry. The London penetration depth shifts the oscillation frequencies, modifies the damping rates, and can generate long-lived quasibound configurations when the effective field mass is sufficiently large. This formalism provides a framework for studying electromagnetic relaxation in curved superconducting media, compact objects containing superconducting phases, and effective massive-vector fields around black holes.

</details>


### [6] [Influence of quantum decoherence on black hole shadow visibility and time domain echoes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03762)
*Sen Guo,Xin Li,Yu Liang,Kai Lin,Li-Fang Li*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using quantum-decoherence-induced effective reflectivity at black hole horizons to break the observational degeneracy between black holes and horizonless exotic compact objects, enabling detection of electromagnetic echoes via spatial (shadow illumination, visibility changes) and temporal (ACF-based time-domain flare echo detection) signatures with next-generation EHT instruments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the observational degeneracy between black holes and horizonless exotic compact objects — which share identical geometric shadows — by leveraging the fundamental quantum decoherence of the horizon (Danielson-Satishchandran-Wald mechanism) as a physically grounded, testable deviation from classical black hole predictions.

Method: 1) Maps quantum horizon decoherence to an effective macroscopic reflectivity R; 2) Performs Kerr ray tracing to model electromagnetic echoes from partially reflective boundaries under both specular and Lambertian (diffuse) reflection; 3) Analyzes spatial-domain effects on shadow morphology and interferometric visibilities; 4) Derives scaling of echo amplitude with membrane compactness ε and reflection type (Γ = 0 for specular, Γ → 1 for diffuse); 5) Develops an Autocorrelation Function (ACF) pipeline to isolate single-flare echoes from accretion-driven red noise in time-domain data for Sgr A*.

Result: Echoes illuminate the inner shadow and suppress low-frequency visibilities while preserving photon subrings; echo amplitude scales as ε^Γ, enabling detection of R ≳ 0.12 (ngEHT) and R ≳ 0.04 (BHEX) in static imaging; for Sgr A*, the ACF pipeline achieves 3σ echo detection for flare amplitudes A ≳ 0.3 within 15–33 hr of continuous monitoring, robust against severe red noise.

Conclusion: The quantum-decoherence-induced effective reflectivity provides a theoretically grounded, observationally accessible probe of horizon structure; combined spatial and time-domain signatures offer a directly deployable, instrument-ready test of black hole unitarity and the quantum nature of horizons.

Abstract: The geometric shadow of a black hole cannot observationally distinguish horizonless exotic compact objects. We break this degeneracy by mapping the fundamental quantum decoherence of the horizon (the Danielson-Satishchandran-Wald mechanism) into a macroscopic effective reflectivity $\mathcal{R}$. Utilizing Kerr ray tracing, we show that a partially reflective boundary ($\mathcal{R}>0$) generates delayed electromagnetic echoes under both specular and diffuse (Lambertian) conditions. In the spatial domain, these echoes illuminate the inner shadow and alter low-frequency visibilities, while leaving metric-governed photon subrings unchanged. The signal strength scales with the membrane compactness $ε$ as $ε^Γ$, where $Γ=0$ for specular reflection and $Γ\to1$ for diffuse re-emission due to escape-cone collapse. This brings specular reflectivities down to $\mathcal{R}\simeq0.12$ (ngEHT) and $0.04$ (BHEX) within reach of static imaging. For Sagittarius A$^\ast$, we translate these echoes into a robust time-domain signature. Using an Autocorrelation Function (ACF) pipeline, we demonstrate that single-flare echoes can be cleanly extracted from severe accretion red noise, achieving a $3σ$ detection for amplitudes $\mathcal{A}\gtrsim0.3$ within 15 to 33 hours of continuous monitoring. This framework provides a directly deployable pathway to test black hole unitarity.

</details>


### [7] [Coherent quantum state corrections in RN--black holes: Photon dynamics, shadow casts, and energy backreaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03777)
*A. Errehymy,Y. Khedif,M. Daoud,B. Turimov,S. Usanov,Z. Yasakov,F. Turaev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a quantum-corrected Reissner-Nordström black hole model using Gaussian regularization to eliminate unphysical oscillations and tame the central singularity—yielding either Ricci-regular or integrable singular cores—while preserving classical behavior asymptotically; it modifies horizon structure, photon orbits, and shadow properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the pathological central singularity and unphysical oscillations in classical Reissner-Nordström geometry within a coherent quantum black hole framework, while maintaining large-scale classical consistency.

Method: Introduces a smooth Gaussian regularization (replacing sharp cutoffs) to construct a short-distance well-behaved RN metric; analyzes curvature invariants (Ricci scalar, Kretschmann scalar), derives effective anisotropic stress-energy tensor, computes Misner-Sharp mass, classifies horizon configurations via dimensionless ratios α = R_Q/R_M and β = R_s/R_M, and studies null geodesics (photon sphere, light deflection, Shapiro delay, shadow).

Result: A regularized RN geometry with core radius R_s; Ricci-regular center only at critical R_s^* = √π R_Q²/R_M, though Kretschmann scalar still diverges as 1/r there; generic cases feature integrable singularities; effective stress-energy behaves as smooth anisotropic fluid; Misner-Sharp mass interpolates from zero to ADM mass; horizon existence depends on α and β; photon sphere shifts inward, light bending and Shapiro delay decrease, shadow becomes more compact and smoother.

Conclusion: Gaussian regularization yields a physically viable quantum-corrected RN black hole that tames singularities without breaking classical limits, alters causal and optical structure predictably, and provides a concrete geometric realization of quantum black hole phenomenology.

Abstract: We revisit the Reissner-Nordström (RN) geometry within the coherent quantum black hole framework, introducing a version that remains well-behaved at short distances while preserving the expected classical behavior at large scales. The key ingredient is a smooth Gaussian regularization, which replaces the usual sharp cutoff and {suppresses} the unphysical oscillations. This construction introduces a characteristic core radius $R_{\rm s}$, constrained relative to the mass $M$ and charge $Q$ to ensure either a {Ricci-regular} center or a softened integrable singularity. Near the origin, the spacetime exhibits distinct behaviors depending on the choice of parameters. When $R_{\rm s}$ satisfies the critical relation $R_{\rm s}^* = \sqrtπ\,R_{\rm Q}^2 / R_{\rm M}$, the geometry is {Ricci-regulFar}, while deviations from this value produce a milder, integrable singularity. {We clarify that for generic parameters, the Kretschmann scalar retains a $1/r$ divergence even at $R_{\rm s}^*$, so the center is not fully regular in the sense of finite curvature invariants.} The effective stress-energy tensor can be interpreted as an anisotropic fluid, whose density and pressure smoothly interpolate between the quantum-dominated core and the classical RN region at large radii. The Misner-Sharp mass accumulates gradually from the center, recovering the ADM mass asymptotically. The quantum scale also modifies the horizon structure, giving rise to standard, extremal, or horizonless configurations depending on the ratios $α=R_{\rm Q}/R_{\rm M}$ and $β=R_{\rm s}/R_{\rm M}$. Photon motion is correspondingly affected: the photon sphere moves inward, light deflection and Shapiro delay are slightly reduced, and the resulting shadow appears more compact and smoother, reflecting the quantum-corrected geometry.

</details>


### [8] [Nonlinear completions of the Ricci coupling in black-hole scalarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03779)
*Subrat Parida*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates spontaneous scalarization of static, spherically symmetric black holes in scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity with a quartic Ricci coupling completion; it derives near-horizon regularity conditions, determines bifurcation behavior via weakly nonlinear expansion, and confirms stability exchange via radial perturbations—showing that higher-order couplings (e.g., γ) shrink the domain of radially stable black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-order (quartic and beyond) scalar self-interactions affect spontaneous scalarization, stability, and bifurcation structure in scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity—beyond the standard quadratic coupling.

Method: Analytical derivation of near-horizon regularity conditions and curvature invariants; weakly nonlinear expansion for bifurcation analysis; construction and analysis of a radial-perturbation master equation for arbitrary couplings.

Result: Near-horizon regularity condition degenerates at finite horizon curvature, terminating scalarized branches; bifurcation direction is exactly fixed and sign-changing in β at β ≈ 1.1156; mass difference between completions scales linearly with γ; radial stability is exchanged at bifurcation and turning points; increasing γ shrinks the radially stable black hole domain—exponential completion is less stable than quadratic truncation.

Conclusion: Higher-order scalar self-interactions (controlled by γ) quantitatively and qualitatively alter scalarization phenomenology: they reduce stability domains and introduce nontrivial dependence on coupling parameters, implying that effective field theory completions must be carefully constrained by stability requirements.

Abstract: We study spontaneous scalarization of static, spherically symmetric black holes in scalar--Gauss--Bonnet gravity with a Ricci coupling $F=1 βφ^{2}/4+γβ^{2}φ^{4}+O(φ^{6})$ that nonlinearly completes the quadratic one. For arbitrary couplings and potential, we derive the near-horizon regularity condition and horizon curvature invariants in closed form; scalarized branches terminate where this condition degenerates, at finite horizon curvature. A weakly nonlinear expansion fixes the bifurcation direction exactly: for a massless scalar, its governing coefficient is quadratic in $β$, changes sign at $β=1.1156$, and depends linearly on a quartic self-interaction. At equal horizon values of the scalar, completions differ in mass, to leading order, in proportion to $γ$. A radial-perturbation master equation for arbitrary couplings confirms the exchange of stability at the bifurcation and turning points. Consequently, the domain of radially stable black holes shrinks as $γ$ increases over the range studied; it is smaller for the exponential completion than for the quadratic truncation.

</details>


### [9] [Dynamical thin shells in black hole ringdown](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03798)
*Han-Wen Hu,Chen Lan,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a radially oscillating thin shell near a black hole modifies gravitational wave ringdown, finding Floquet sidebands, enhanced late-time echoes for nearby shells, and an exceptional point with a second-order pole contribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how matter dynamics (e.g., post-merger debris or exotic compact objects) near black holes affect gravitational wave ringdown signatures, particularly echoes and spectral features beyond standard QNMs.

Method: Linearized perturbation theory with time-dependent boundary conditions; derivation of matching conditions for polar and axial modes using the Israel junction conditions on a moving worldtube; Floquet analysis for periodic shell motion; identification of exceptional points via mode coalescence in the complex frequency plane.

Result: Periodic shell oscillation generates Floquet sidebands; late-time echo amplitude increases ~100× when shell is close enough for its long-lived mode to couple into the incident band; existence and waveform imprint of an exceptional point with a second-order pole in the Green's function.

Conclusion: Shell dynamics significantly alter ringdown phenomenology beyond simple QNM superposition—enabling new probes of near-horizon structure, including non-Hermitian spectral features like exceptional points.

Abstract: We study how a radially oscillating thin shell outside a black hole (BH) affects gravitational wave ringdown and derive matching conditions for polar and axial perturbations from the linearized Israel junction conditions on the moving worldtube. Periodic shell motion produces Floquet sidebands in the scattered wave. For a distant shell, the motion has little effect on the prompt ringdown and only weakly modifies the late time echoes. When the shell is closer to the BH, a replica of a long lived shell mode enters the incident frequency band, increasing the late time echo amplitude by roughly two orders of magnitude. We also identify an exceptional point where two polar modes coalesce and find the contribution of its second order pole in the late time waveform.

</details>


### [10] [Thermodynamics, thermodynamic geometry, and the shadow of a Bardeen black hole surrounded by a Kiselev fluid: the role of regularity, asymptotics, and observational self-consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03801)
*Hoang Van Quyet*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the Bardeen black hole immersed in Kiselev quintessence, correcting prior claims about its regularity and asymptotics; it provides exact horizon classification, derives corrected thermodynamics using Ma-Zhao prescription, identifies physical vs. spurious singularities in thermodynamic geometry, and critically reassesses EHT-based bounds on the quintessence parameter c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To correct erroneous literature claims that the Kiselev-modified Bardeen black hole is regular at the origin and asymptotically flat for arbitrary c; to rigorously characterize its horizon structure, thermodynamics, and observational constraints—especially in light of EHT imaging of Sgr A*.

Method: Analytical derivation of curvature invariants and asymptotic behavior; closed-form mapping of the (q,c) horizon-structure domain; application of the Ma-Zhao prescription to define mass-dependent nonlinear-electrodynamics source; computer-algebra verification of the extended first law and Smarr relation; analytic and numerical analysis of heat capacity and thermodynamic curvatures (Quevedo and Ruppeiner); computation of photon sphere and shadow critical impact parameter b_c(q,c,w).

Result: The solution is regular only for c = 0; for c ≠ 0, curvature diverges at r → 0 and spacetime is asymptotically de Sitter with cosmological horizon r_c; full (q,c) horizon taxonomy obtained analytically; corrected entropy satisfies extended first law and Smarr relation to machine precision; heat capacity exhibits one physical Davies-type divergence (quartic in parameters); Quevedo curvature contains a spurious singularity from entropy integration constant; Ruppeiner curvature vanishes for pure Bardeen but activates for c ≠ 0; EHT-derived bound on c is shown to be self-inconsistent and reinterpreted as near-horizon, not cosmological.

Conclusion: The Kiselev-Bardeen black hole is fundamentally non-regular and non-asymptotically-flat for c ≠ 0; its thermodynamic and geometric structure is precisely characterized; current EHT constraints on c must be understood as effective near-horizon parameters, not global quintessence density bounds.

Abstract: We reconsider the Bardeen black hole surrounded by a Kiselev fluid, obtained by adding a Kiselev term to the Ayon-Beato-Garcia-Bardeen metric. Contrary to claims in the literature, the solution is regular at the origin only for $c=0$: for $c\neq0$ curvature invariants diverge as $r\to0$, and the spacetime is asymptotically de Sitter-like rather than flat, with a cosmological horizon $r_c$ for $0<c<c_N(q)$. We map the full $(q,c)$ domain of horizon structures in closed form, locating the extremal and Nariai-type boundaries and their cusp. Using the Ma-Zhao prescription for the mass-dependent nonlinear-electrodynamics source, we derive the corrected entropy and verify the extended first law and Smarr relation to machine precision by computer-algebra differentiation, not through the entropy's own definition. The heat capacity shows a single Davies-type divergence, located as a closed-form quartic and expanded analytically in $c$. In thermodynamic geometry, the Quevedo-metric curvature has a spurious second singularity generated by the entropy's integration constant and the non-uniqueness of its conformal factor; we isolate this artefact and show only the Davies-point divergence is physical. The Ruppeiner curvature, identically zero for pure Bardeen, becomes non-zero once $c\neq0$. For the shadow, we compute the critical impact parameter $b_c(q,c,w)$ and show the nominal bound $c\lesssim10^{-2}/M$ from the EHT image of Sagittarius A* is not self-consistent: the same $c$ places the cosmological horizon nine orders of magnitude closer than Earth's distance to Sgr A*, and the bound is fourteen orders of magnitude larger than $H_0/c$, the true cosmological dark-energy scale. We reinterpret the EHT constraint as bounding $c$ only near the horizon, not as an astrophysical or cosmological bound on the quintessence parameter.

</details>


### [11] [Four-derivative corrections to the neutral-scalar Christodoulou threshold in charged de Sitter black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03805)
*Reggie C. Pantig*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how four-derivative quantum gravity corrections affect the Christodoulou regularity criterion for a neutral scalar field near the Cauchy horizon of charged de Sitter black holes, finding that the correction to the regularity threshold depends non-universally on cosmological scale and quasinormal mode competition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how higher-derivative corrections in effective gravity influence Cauchy-horizon regularity — specifically whether and how the Christodoulou criterion is modified near cold extremality in charged de Sitter black holes — given its role in strong cosmic censorship.

Method: Analytic derivation of corrected static black hole solutions, cold extremality relation, near-horizon geometry, and Cauchy-horizon surface gravity within Einstein-Maxwell effective theory with leading parity-even four-derivative terms; reduction of the scalar wave equation to an AdS₂ throat problem near cold extremality; spectral analysis of neutral scalar quasinormal modes; comparison of coefficient dependencies across extremality, throat geometry, and regularity threshold.

Result: The Christodoulou regularity threshold shifts non-monotonically with cosmological scale: it moves away from cold extremality at small/intermediate scales but reverses direction toward larger scales, coinciding with a quasinormal family transition; the coefficient controlling extremal mass differs generically from those governing the throat geometry and regularity boundary, implying no universal correction direction.

Conclusion: Cosmological scale and spectral-family competition are essential in determining how effective gravity corrections impact Cauchy-horizon regularity for neutral scalars; extremality alone does not universally fix the direction of the Christodoulou-threshold correction.

Abstract: We study leading parity-even four-derivative corrections to the linear Christodoulou regularity criterion for a neutral, massless, minimally coupled spectator scalar in four-dimensional charged de Sitter black holes. We derive the corrected static solution, cold extremality relation, near-horizon geometry, Cauchy-horizon surface gravity, and neutral scalar quasinormal spectrum within Einstein-Maxwell effective theory. Near cold extremality, the scalar equation reduces analytically to the finite-temperature anti-de Sitter two-dimensional throat problem. We find that the dominant nonstationary spherically symmetric mode retains its cold-limit regularity ratio through first order, while higher angular sectors respond to the deformation of the relative throat radii. The finite-distance neutral-scalar Christodoulou boundary nevertheless shifts because the relevant exterior scalar spectrum and the unequal horizon surface gravities enter the regularity ratio. At smaller and intermediate cosmological scales, positive effective coefficients move the Christodoulou threshold to larger normalized separation of the Cauchy and event horizons. Toward the larger cosmological-scale part of the cold branch, the same coefficient directions move that threshold back toward the cold endpoint. The reversal accompanies a transition in the quasinormal family that supplies the spectral gap. We further show that the coefficient combination governing the fixed-charge extremal mass parameter differs generically from those governing the cold throat and the finite-distance neutral-scalar boundary. Extremality therefore does not determine a universal direction for the neutral-scalar Christodoulou-threshold correction. Our results identify cosmological scale and spectral-family competition as essential elements in relating effective gravity, black-hole extremality, and Cauchy-horizon regularity in this spectator sector.

</details>


### [12] [Optical Signatures of Black Holes Surrounded by a Generalized Cloud of Strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04173)
*Marcos V. de S. Silva,Ednaldo L. B. Junior,Carlos F. S. Pereira,Diego Sáez-Chillón Gómez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies black hole optical signatures in the Letelier--Alencar spacetime—a Schwarzschild black hole embedded in a generalized cloud of strings—analyzing null geodesics, shadow radius constraints from Sgr A* and M87*, weak-field light deflection (via Gauss–Bonnet and geodesic perturbation), Shapiro delay with asymptotic conical contribution, and ray-traced images of thin accretion disks; it shows that string parameters nonuniformly distort photon mapping, decoupling shadow size from Einstein ring position.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe how a generalized cloud of cosmic strings modifies black hole optical observables—especially given the non-asymptotically-flat, conical asymptotics of the Letelier--Alencar spacetime—and to constrain string parameters using EHT shadow measurements of Sgr A* and M87*.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of null geodesics; derivation of photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter; shadow radius mapping accounting for asymptotic conical deficit; weak-field light deflection computed via both Gauss--Bonnet theorem and perturbative integration of geodesic equations; Shapiro time delay decomposition; backward ray tracing for thin-disk imaging; construction of transfer functions and celestial-sphere lensing maps.

Result: String parameters can increase or decrease photon sphere radius and critical impact parameter relative to Schwarzschild; shadow radius bounds from Sgr A* and M87* yield concrete parameter constraints; weak-field deflection includes an additive conical term beyond standard GR; Shapiro delay acquires a distance-dependent background contribution; lensing images show nonuniform, non-rescaling distortion—e.g., smaller shadows can coexist with outward-shifted Einstein rings.

Conclusion: The generalized cloud of strings induces qualitatively distinct optical signatures compared to pure Schwarzschild geometry—not reducible to simple scaling—and different observables (shadow radius, Einstein ring position, time delay) encode complementary information about the asymptotic conical structure and local curvature, enabling multimessenger constraints on string cloud models.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the optical signatures of black holes surrounded by a generalized cloud of strings, described by the Letelier--Alencar spacetime. We first analyze null geodesics, showing that the string parameters can either increase or decrease the photon sphere radius and the critical impact parameter relative to their Schwarzschild values. Since the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, the critical impact parameter does not directly coincide with the physical shadow radius. We use this distinction to derive constraints on the model parameters from the shadow radius bounds of Sgr A* and M87*. In the weak-field regime, we calculate the light deflection angle using both the Gauss--Bonnet method and a perturbative expansion of the null geodesic equations, obtaining consistent results that contain a contribution from the asymptotically conical geometry in addition to the local gravitational deflection. We also examine the Shapiro time delay and identify a distance dependent contribution associated with the non Minkowskian asymptotic background. Finally, we study the optical appearance of geometrically and optically thin accretion disks and construct celestial sphere images through backward ray tracing. The transfer functions and lensing images show that the generalized cloud of strings modifies the null geodesic mapping in a nonuniform manner rather than producing a simple rescaling of the Schwarzschild image. In particular, configurations with a smaller shadow may still produce an outward displacement of the main Einstein ring image, demonstrating that different optical observables probe distinct aspects of photon propagation in this geometry.

</details>


### [13] [Exact Solutions for Colliding Neutrino Waves and the Absence of Mutual Scattering for a Single Grassmann Mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04321)
*Yorgo Senikoglu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper exactly solves the head-on collision of two plane neutrino waves in the Einstein-Weyl system, revealing that due to nilpotency of the Grassmann-mode bilinear ε = ηη*, the waves pass through each other without scattering—no metric cross-terms, vanishing Ψ₂, and decoupled Weyl scalars Ψ₀, Ψ₄ proportional to individual energy fluxes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether and how neutrino waves scatter gravitationally during head-on collision in a fully coupled general relativistic quantum field setting, specifically within the Einstein-Weyl system with fermionic (Grassmann) spinor fields.

Method: Treats massless Weyl spinors as odd Grassmann-valued fields; imposes the mixed-component Einstein equations to constrain wave structure; constructs exact solution in interaction region using single Grassmann mode; matches to single-wave regions via non-impulsive O'Brien-Synge junction conditions; analyzes Weyl scalars and metric decomposition.

Result: Exact solution shows no metric cross-terms between waves; Ψ₂ ≡ 0; Ψ₀ and Ψ₄ are individually proportional to each wave’s energy flux; waves propagate through each other unchanged—no mutual scattering—due to nilpotency of ε = ηη*.

Conclusion: Gravitational 'transparency' of colliding neutrino waves arises intrinsically from Grassmann algebra and nilpotent bilinear structure in the Einstein-Weyl system; scattering requires breaking this structure—e.g., via massive spinors, multiple modes, or higher-order couplings.

Abstract: We study the head-on collision of two plane neutrino waves in the coupled Einstein-Weyl system, treating the massless Weyl spinor as an odd Grassmann valued field. We show that the mixed components of the Einstein equations force the two counter propagating waves to be built on a single Grassmann mode, whose bilinear $\varepsilon=ηη^{*}$ is nilpotent. With this structure the full coupled system is solved exactly in the interaction region for arbitrary amplitude and phase profiles, and the metric and spinor fields are matched to the single wave regions by the non-impulsive O'Brien-Synge junction conditions. The metric functions contain no terms coupling the two waves; the Weyl scalar $Ψ_2$ vanishes, and $Ψ_0$, $Ψ_4$ are proportional to the neutrino energy fluxes. The waves therefore traverse each other without mutual scattering; we trace this to the nilpotency of the single mode bilinear. We discuss the limits of this result and indicate where cross couplings can arise.

</details>


### [14] [Bouncing Dust Collapse and Black-to-White Hole Transition on the Brane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04353)
*Rikpratik Sengupta,Chiranjeeb Singha*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spherical collapse of a dust cloud on a timelike-extra-dimension braneworld (Shtanov–Sahni model), deriving exact interior dynamics, matching to a charged Vaidya exterior with positive tidal charge, proving geodesic completeness, bounce existence conditions, trapped region formation, and recovery of Oppenheimer–Snyder in the GR limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand gravitational collapse and singularity avoidance in braneworld cosmologies with timelike extra dimensions, particularly whether bounce behavior and nonsingular evolution are generic features distinct from standard GR.

Method: Exact integration of the brane Friedmann equation for a closed homogeneous dust cloud; matching via Israel junction conditions to a spherically symmetric exterior with trace-free effective stress-energy; explicit construction of advanced/retarded charged Vaidya metrics; analysis of curvature invariants, geodesic completeness, trapped surfaces, and energy conditions.

Result: Interior exhibits nonsingular periodic evolution with bounded curvature invariants and geodesically complete worldlines; bounce occurs iff comoving density satisfies a simple condition; exterior is necessarily nonstatic charged Vaidya with positive tidal charge; trapped region forms above critical mass; full contraction–bounce–expansion solution constructed via C^{1,1} matching of Vaidya patches; white-hole emergence into second asymptotic region; GR limit recovers Oppenheimer–Snyder.

Conclusion: Gravitational collapse in the Shtanov–Sahni timelike-braneworld generically avoids singularities via regular bounces, supports geodesically complete spacetimes, and admits physically consistent global solutions with white-hole re-emergence — features absent in standard GR, highlighting the role of brane tension λ as a fundamental regulator of spacetime structure.

Abstract: We study the spherical collapse of a homogeneous dust cloud on a three-brane in the Shtanov--Sahni braneworld, in which the extra dimension is timelike. In the high-energy regime the brane Friedmann equation acquires a negative quadratic term in the matter density, controlled by a critical density $\rhoc=2λ$, where $λ$ is the magnitude of the negative brane tension. For a spatially closed interior we integrate the dynamics exactly. Every turning point of the scale factor lies above $\astar=\sqrt{3/4πλ}$, a bounce exists if and only if a simple condition on the comoving density holds, and all curvature invariants stay below bounds fixed by $λ$ alone along the entire cycle. Comoving worldlines are geodesically complete. We then match the cloud across a comoving boundary to a spherically symmetric exterior with a trace-free effective source and solve the Israel conditions in closed form. The exterior is a charged Vaidya geometry whose mass function and tidal charge are fixed by the boundary radius. The tidal charge is positive, opposite in sign to the Randall--Sundrum case, and the exterior cannot be static. A trapped region forms on the boundary if and only if the mass exceeds $2/(3\sqrt{πλ})$, and in that case the bounce still takes place in an untrapped neighbourhood of the boundary. For trapped clouds we construct an exterior over the full contraction, bounce and re-expansion by joining an advanced and a retarded charged Vaidya patch through a static wedge. The matched metric is of class $C^{1,1}$, the effective energy conditions hold, and the cloud re-emerges through a white hole region into a second asymptotic region. In the general relativistic limit $λ\to\infty$ the Oppenheimer--Snyder solution is recovered.

</details>


### [15] [On the stability of spherically symmetric relativistic configurations with scalar field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04360)
*Anatolii Tugay,Valery Zhdanov,Yurii Taistra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates radial (monopole) linear stability of static, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat scalar field configurations in General Relativity with power-law potentials, finding instability for small scalar charges using a novel shooting method applied to a regularized master equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether static, spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat scalar field solutions—typically afflicted by naked singularities—are physically viable, the authors examine their linear stability against radial perturbations.

Method: A new shooting method is developed to compute eigenvalues associated with exponentially growing (unstable) radial perturbation modes; the perturbation problem is reduced to a single second-order 'master' equation in divergent form, with a regularized effective potential ensuring numerical robustness.

Result: Instability modes are confirmed to exist for sufficiently small scalar charges of the background static configurations.

Conclusion: The presence of unstable radial modes implies that these naked-singularity-bearing scalar field configurations are dynamically non-viable for small scalar charge, reinforcing concerns about their physical acceptability in GR.

Abstract: Static spherically symmetric asymptotically flat configurations in General Relativity with a minimally coupled scalar field typically possess naked singularities. We study a linear stability of these configurations against radial (monopole) perturbations in case of power-law scalar field potentials. We employ a new shooting method that extracts the eigenvalues corresponding to the exponentially growing modes of the perturbation. The problem is reduced to one second order ``master'' equation in a divergent form with a regularized effective potential. We confirm our previous results showing that instability modes exist for sufficiently small scalar charges of the initial static configurations.

</details>


### [16] [Bias-aware Fisher forecasts for stochastic gravitational-wave background](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04459)
*Bo-Qiang Lu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Whittle-likelihood forecasting for stochastic gravitational-wave background (SGWB) detection in mHz interferometers (e.g., LISA), showing that a commonly used two-stage composite estimator significantly inflates amplitude uncertainty—up to 10×—due to its implementation, not the underlying gauss_D likelihood approximation; jointly maximizing gauss_D yields unbiased estimates, and the authors provide calibrated bias and MSE corrections for power-law and broken-power-law templates under strong-signal, stationary assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify and correct the substantial amplitude uncertainty inflation caused by the standard two-stage composite estimator in Whittle-likelihood SGWB analysis—particularly critical for upcoming mHz space-based interferometers—and to establish a reliable, unbiased baseline using jointly maximized gauss_D or exact Whittle likelihood.

Method: Frequentist error analysis comparing three estimators: (i) exact Whittle (control), (ii) determinant-approximate gauss_D (working model), and (iii) composite two-stage estimator (per-bin amplitude estimation + unweighted log-space template fitting); calibration of bias vector and mean-squared-error matrix for power-law and smooth broken-power-law spectral templates; evaluation across segment durations (30 min to 11.5 d), signal strengths (via SNR), and spectral features (e.g., sharp sound-shell signals).

Result: The composite estimator inflates marginal-Fisher amplitude error bars by up to R=10.2 at 11.5-d baseline (median ~3); inflation scales as R²−1 ∝ T_seg² for shorter segments and exhibits a non-removable efficiency gap C_b up to 18% in outer spectral index α_out; single-parameter Whittle-bias diagnostics fail to capture multi-parameter inflation; inflation is negligible (<0.15%) on 30-min segments and vanishes below sensitivity floor; ln(R−1) correlates strongly with ln(SNR) (corr=0.91); calibration is valid only under strong-signal, stationary, matching noise-model assumptions.

Conclusion: The composite two-stage estimator introduces severe, avoidable amplitude uncertainty inflation due to its decoupled structure—not the gauss_D approximation itself; jointly optimized gauss_D is unbiased and recommended as baseline; calibrated bias and MSE corrections enable robust inference for power-law and broken-power-law SGWB templates, but applicability is restricted to strong-signal, stationary regimes with matched noise models.

Abstract: Whittle-likelihood forecasting of the stochastic gravitational-wave background underpins planned mHz interferometers (LISA, Taiji, TianQin). A composite two-stage estimator, per-bin amplitude estimation then unweighted log-space template fitting, inflates the marginal-Fisher amplitude error bar on resolved-bin count by up to an order of magnitude at \(11.5\)-d baseline. This inflation arises from the composite implementation, not the determinant-approximate \texttt{gauss\_D} likelihood it approximates. Jointly maximised, \texttt{gauss\_D} is unbiased (\(R=1.000\) within \(10^{-4}\)) and is recommended baseline with exact-Whittle likelihood. With exact Whittle as control and \texttt{gauss\_D} as working model, we calibrate bias vector and mean-squared-error matrix for power-law and smooth broken-power-law templates. First, for sharp signals of a \(32\)-point sound-shell scan, the composite estimator inflates amplitude error bar by \(R=10.2\) at \(11.5\)-d baseline (median \(\simeq3\)), follows \(R^2-1\propto T_{\mathrm{seg}}^2\) at shorter segments, and has a non-removable efficiency gap \(\mathbf{C}_b\) up to \(18\%\) in \(α_{\mathrm{out}}\). Second, the usual single-parameter Whittle-bias diagnostic does not track multi-parameter inflation and errs both ways. Third, inflation is negligible on \(30\)-min segments, \(R-1\le1.5\times10^{-3}\) over measured bias-exponent range, vanishes below sensitivity floor, and tracks resolved signal strength with \(\mathrm{corr}[\ln \mathrm{SNR},\ln(R-1)]=0.91\). Results are frequentist; interval diagnostics transfer to flat-prior credible intervals only in the locally Gaussian regime. The calibration is a strong-signal, stationary statement transferable only to a matching noise model.

</details>


### [17] [Magnetic-field-induced chaos of charged particles around a rotating black hole in a King-type dark matter halo](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04521)
*Yu Wang,Meilin Liu,Haiguang Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies orbital dynamics of charged particles around a rotating black hole embedded in a King-type dark matter halo and immersed in a uniform magnetic field; it finds that magnetic coupling can induce chaos, but regular orbits persist depending on initial conditions and halo density.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how dark matter halos and external magnetic fields jointly influence the chaotic versus regular orbital behavior of charged test particles near rotating black holes.

Method: Uses an effective rotating spacetime geometry derived via a modified Newman--Janis algorithm from a static King-type dark matter halo; employs Hamilton–Jacobi analysis (with Carter constant for the unmagnetized case); applies numerical diagnostics including Poincaré sections, fast Lyapunov indicators, and finite-time Lyapunov exponents with mass-shell monitoring and convergence checks.

Result: Magnetic coupling induces transition from regular to chaotic motion for some initial conditions; higher-density halos enhance finite-time Lyapunov exponents for chaotic families; weak-halo effects are numerically unresolved; certain initial conditions retain regular (closed Poincaré) orbits even with nonzero magnetic coupling.

Conclusion: The King-type dark matter halo modulates magnetic-field-induced orbital instability in an initial-condition- and halo-density-dependent way, and regular motion remains possible in the magnetized system.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of charged test particles around a rotating black hole embedded in a King-type dark matter halo and subjected to a prescribed, asymptotically uniform magnetic field. We adopt an effective rotating geometry constructed from a static halo seed through a modified Newman--Janis prescription and a test electromagnetic field that generally requires supporting currents in the nonvacuum background. In the absence of magnetic coupling, the timelike Hamilton--Jacobi equation separates and admits a Carter-type constant, providing an integrable reference system. We characterize the orbital dynamics using Poincaré sections and tangent-vector growth diagnostics, including fast Lyapunov indicators and finite-time Lyapunov exponents, with mass-shell monitoring and numerical refinement checks. For the sampled initial conditions, increasing the magnetic coupling can produce a transition from regular motion to scattered Poincaré sections with sustained positive finite-time growth rates. At fixed magnetic coupling, the two higher-density halo cases yield larger finite-time exponents for a representative chaotic orbit family, whereas the weak-halo correction is not resolved consistently under numerical refinement. A different initial condition yields a closed Poincaré curve and a decreasing finite-time exponent over an extended integration, supporting regular motion despite the nonzero magnetic coupling. These results show that the King-type halo modulates magnetic-field-induced orbital instability in an initial-condition-dependent manner, while regular motion persists within the magnetized system.

</details>


### [18] [Exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spaces with solvable motions groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04522)
*V. V. Obukhov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies null homogeneous Petrov spaces V*_4(N) by leveraging the geometry of associated 3D homogeneous Riemannian spaces V_3(N), classified via Bianchi types I–VII; it constructs metrics and electromagnetic vector potentials in a canonical frame, derives reduced Maxwell vacuum equations solely in terms of group structure constants and wave-variable-dependent functions, and obtains all non-equivalent exact solutions—explicitly parameterized by arbitrary functions of the wave variable—thereby simplifying subsequent Einstein-Maxwell integration to ODE systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and construct exact solutions of the Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spacetimes V*_4(N), exploiting the underlying 3D spatial homogeneity and Bianchi group structure to reduce complexity and enable full integration.

Method: Uses the canonical (non-holonomic) frame of 3D homogeneous Riemannian spaces V_3(N) (Bianchi classification N = I–VII), constructs the 4D metric and invariant electromagnetic vector potential for V*_4(N), derives Maxwell vacuum equations expressed purely in terms of group structure constants, non-holonomic metric components, vector potential components, and their derivatives with respect to the wave variable; solves these PDEs completely for solvable G_3(N) groups.

Result: All non-equivalent exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations in V*_4(N) for Bianchi types I–VII are obtained explicitly in closed form, parameterized by arbitrary smooth functions of the wave variable; the Einstein-Maxwell system thereby decouples, leaving only ODEs for the remaining Einstein equations.

Conclusion: The geometric framework based on Bianchi-classified 3D homogeneous spaces enables complete integration of Maxwell vacuum equations in null homogeneous Petrov spacetimes; the resulting solutions provide a foundational ansatz for solving the full Einstein-Maxwell system via reduction to ODEs.

Abstract: Null homogeneous Petrov spaces $V^*_4(N)$ are considered. The geometry of $V^*_4(N)$ is determined by the geometry of the three-dimensional homogeneous Riemannian space $V_3(N)$, which is invariant under the action of the three-parameter motions group $G_3(N)$ ($N$ corresponds to the group number in the Bianchi classification). The canonical frame of the space $V_3(N)$ is used to construct the metric tensor of the space $V^*_4(N)$ and the components of the vector potential of the invariant electromagnetic field. Using this reper, we obtain Maxwell vacuum equations containing only the structure constants of the groups, the non-holonomic components of the contravariant metric tensor and vector potential, and their derivatives with respect to the wave variable. All non-equivalent exact solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations are found for the case of solvable motions groups $G_3(I)-G_3(VII)$.
  All solutions of Maxwell vacuum equations obtained in this article are written explicitly in terms of arbitrary independent functions of wave variable. Therefore, when integrating the Einstein-Maxwell equations, it has to consider only the remaining systems of Einstein equations which reduce to systems of ordinary differential equations that include only these independent functions.

</details>


### [19] [Quasinormal-Mode Collider Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04595)
*Mian Zhu,Jun Tao,Peng Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Introduces quasinormal-mode collider spectroscopy (QCS) to study nonlinear interactions among black-hole quasinormal modes, establishing factorization and reconstruction properties of cubic strain residues for consistency tests and waveform prediction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To move beyond linear BH spectroscopy and probe how quasinormal modes interact nonlinearly during ringdown—revealing intermediate states and interaction structure, analogous to cosmological collider physics.

Method: Develops QCS framework; proves two structural theorems: (1) factorization of cubic strain residue at isolated nondegenerate intermediate poles into excitation amplitude and quadratic conversion coefficient; (2) reconstruction of intermediate pole residue from horizon and infinity radiation, enabling full cubic waveform difference prediction under fixed first- and third-order data.

Result: Demonstrates rank constraints across output channels and initial data choices as consistency tests; shows exact reconstruction of cubic waveform differences from lower-order radiation, linking first- and third-order fields to second-order dynamics.

Conclusion: QCS provides a rigorous, predictive framework for nonlinear BH ringdown, enabling observational tests of mode coupling and intermediate-state physics in gravitational-wave signals.

Abstract: Black-hole (BH) quasinormal modes encode the properties of merger remnants and provide powerful tests of strong-field gravity. The BH spectroscopy has predominantly treated them as independent linear oscillations. Nonlinear ringdown can additionally reveal how these modes interact and which intermediate states mediate their interactions. Inspired by cosmological collider physics, we introduce quasinormal-mode collider spectroscopy (QCS), a framework for organizing and predicting nonlinear BH responses. We establish two structural results. First, at an isolated, nondegenerate intermediate pole, a cubic strain residue factorizes into an excitation amplitude and a quadratic conversion coefficient. This factorization imposes rank constraints across output channels and choices of second-order initial data, providing consistency tests of the intermediate-mode description. Second, the intermediate pole residue can be reconstructed from its horizon and infinity radiation. With the first-order field and third-order data held fixed, this reconstruction extends to the full cubic waveform difference generated by changing the second-order initial data. QCS thus connects lower-order radiation to higher-order observables and provides a framework for probing the interaction structure of nonlinear ringdown.

</details>


### [20] [Covariance scaling and a dual-mode torque diagnostic in measurements of the gravitational constant](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04707)
*Vineet Kumar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes inconsistencies in historical measurements of the gravitational constant G, using a proportional covariance-scale model on the CODATA-2018 dataset; it finds that the 2026 NIST torsion-balance result—though anomalous under nominal uncertainties—is consistent once empirically inferred extra dispersion (λ ≈ 2.97) is applied; it also identifies a common absolute torque offset (1.92 ± 0.22 pN·m) across NIST configurations, rejecting a simple fractional scaling model (Δχ² = 11.04), and provides diagnostic tools for torsion-balance consistency without recommending a new G value.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve persistent discrepancies among laboratory determinations of G—exceeding quoted uncertainties—by rigorously auditing the CODATA-2018 archive’s covariance structure and accounting for apparatus lineage–induced correlations, especially between BIPM-14 and the 2026 NIST redetermination.

Method: Applies a proportional covariance-scale model (scaling full reported covariance matrices by λ) to the CODATA-2018 G determinations; excludes BIPM-14 due to shared apparatus lineage with NIST’s 2026 measurement; performs χ²-based consistency tests (Q_λ, finite-sample F-test), compares absolute vs. fractional torque-offset models for NIST’s four free-minus-servo differences using Δχ², and conducts covariance audits and lineage-qualified fits.

Result: Historical fit yields λ̂ = 2.967 for 15 measurements; NIST’s correlated result becomes statistically consistent (p = 0.382) after λ-scaling; mean-only scaling fails to resolve discrepancy; inclusion of all 16 data yields comparable conclusion; NIST torque differences support a common absolute offset ΔN̂ = 1.918 ± 0.215 pN·m over fractional scaling (Δχ² = 11.041).

Conclusion: Discrepancies in G are attributable to underestimated Type-B uncertainties and apparatus-specific systematic effects—not statistical outliers; the analysis validates a covariance-scaling framework and introduces experimentally testable diagnostics for dual-mode torsion balances, but deliberately refrains from proposing a revised G value.

Abstract: Laboratory determinations of the Newtonian gravitational constant $G$ disagree by more than their quoted uncertainties allow. We examine the sixteen determinations comprising the CODATA-2018 archive using a proportional covariance-scale model that retains the full reported covariance matrix. Because the 2026 NIST torsion-balance redetermination shares apparatus lineage with the BIPM Mark II balance, our primary historical fit excludes the BIPM-14 determination, giving $\hatλ = 2.967$ for the remaining fifteen measurements. Evaluated against this historical model under the declared uniform covariance-scaling convention, the correlated four-configuration NIST result is highly unlikely under the archive's reported, unscaled covariance but is statistically unremarkable once the historically inferred extra dispersion is applied ($Q_λ= 4.840$; finite-sample $F$ tail $p = 0.382$); a mean-only scaling sensitivity leaves the vector discrepant. Retaining the complete archive also gives a comparable result. We further examine four matched free-minus-servo torque differences reported by NIST. They are consistent with a single common absolute torque offset across the four tested configurations, $\widehat{ΔN} = 1.918 \pm 0.215$ pN m, and poorly described by an equally simple fractional-scale alternative under the stated independent-Type-A treatment, with a chi-square contrast of $Δχ^{2} = 11.041$ between the two torque models. The paper provides a covariance audit of the historical archive, a lineage-qualified consistency check, and an experimentally testable diagnostic for dual-mode torsion balances, without proposing a revised value of $G$.

</details>


### [21] [Polyakov backreaction in large D discretely self-similar collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04960)
*Sheng-Hong Lai,Wen-Yu Wen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum backreaction effects on self-similar black hole solutions in high-dimensional Einstein–Klein–Gordon theory using the Polyakov anomaly, revealing that quantum corrections prevent generic smooth, exactly self-similar completions of the classical singularity—instead yielding either singular crossings or a narrow, high-curvature throat under strict regularity conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum anomalies—specifically the trace anomaly via the Polyakov action—affect the near-singularity structure of discretely self-similar black hole solutions, particularly whether semiclassical backreaction can yield regular, self-similar extensions of classical singularities.

Method: Analyzes an analytic family of discretely self-similar solutions in large-D Einstein–Klein–Gordon theory; imposes horizon regularity to fix a Floquet exponent and select a unique quantum state; uses spherical reduction and auxiliary-field (Polyakov) formalism; constructs nonlinear inner expansion due to nonuniform quantum coupling; identifies critical dilaton surface and matches to zero-mass exterior.

Result: The Polyakov auxiliary field exhibits affine-periodicity with fixed monodromy; quantum coupling diverges toward the center, invalidating uniform first-order perturbation; a critical dilaton surface emerges whose generic crossing is singular; only fine-tuned matching yields a narrow, high-curvature throat; anomaly shifts effective null-energy boundaries away from classical curves.

Conclusion: The minimally coupled Polyakov model does not support a generic smooth, exactly self-similar semiclassical completion of the classical central singularity—regularity requires extreme fine-tuning, implying inherent obstruction to self-similarity at the quantum level.

Abstract: We study anomaly-induced semiclassical backreaction on an analytic family of discretely self-similar solutions of Einstein--Klein--Gordon theory at large spacetime dimension. Horizon regularity fixes a null-coordinate Floquet exponent and selects a unique regular quantum state. The Polyakov auxiliary field is affine-periodic rather than periodic, with a monodromy that is determined independently by a global zero-mode condition and by the conformal-coordinate scaling. Spherical reduction introduces a quantum coupling that grows toward the center, so the periodic first-order expansion is nonuniform. We construct the resulting nonlinear inner problem and find a critical dilaton surface. Generic crossings of this surface are singular, whereas matching to the regular zero-mass exterior selects a narrow, high-curvature throat. The anomaly also separates the effective null-energy boundaries from the classical matter flux and zero-curvature curves. These results show that the minimally coupled Polyakov model does not yield a generic smooth, exactly self-similar completion of the classical center.

</details>


### [22] [Thermodynamic Volumes of Extended Black Hole Thermodynamics with Auxiliary Gauge Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05022)
*Zili Mou,Yu Tian,Hongbao Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper derives the first law of extended black hole thermodynamics using the Iyer-Wald formalism applied to a modified gravitational Lagrangian with coupling constants promoted to dynamical scalar fields via auxiliary gauge fields; it confirms that thermodynamic volumes conjugate to these couplings are independent of the auxiliary fields, match prior results, and can alternatively be expressed in terms of those fields evaluated at the horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously derive the first law of extended black hole thermodynamics—particularly thermodynamic volumes conjugate to coupling constants (e.g., cosmological constant)—within a covariant, field-theoretic framework, avoiding ad hoc parameter variation.

Method: Applies the Iyer-Wald formalism to a modified gravitational Lagrangian where coupling constants are promoted to dynamical scalar fields by introducing auxiliary gauge fields; computes the Noether charge and Wald entropy; identifies conjugate thermodynamic variables via boundary integrals and on-shell variations.

Result: Thermodynamic volumes conjugate to coupling constants are shown to be independent of the auxiliary gauge fields and identical to those obtained by direct parameter variation; additionally, these volumes admit an equivalent expression solely in terms of the auxiliary gauge fields, evaluated on the black hole horizon.

Conclusion: The Iyer-Wald approach consistently reproduces extended black hole thermodynamics without relying on brute-force parameter variation; the horizon-based expression of thermodynamic volumes in terms of auxiliary fields validates prior geometric proposals and strengthens the physical interpretation of thermodynamic volume.

Abstract: By applying the Iyer-Wald formalism to the modified Lagrangian form for the gravitational system, where the coupling constants appearing in the original Lagrangian form, such as the cosmological constant, are promoted to the dynamical scalar fields by introducing the corresponding auxiliary gauge fields, we derive the first law of the extended black hole thermodynamics, where the resulting thermodynamic volumes as the conjugate thermodynamic variables to the coupling constants are nevertheless independent of the auxiliary gauge fields and exactly the same as those obtained before via varying the coupling constants by brute force. In addition, it is further shown that such volumes can also be expressed in terms of the auxiliary gauge fields and evaluated on the black hole horizon, validating the proposal made in the previous literature.

</details>


### [23] [Thermodynamic Stability of a Viscous Variable Generalised Chaplygin Gas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05361)
*D. Panigrahi,K. Kanjilal,Saibal Ray*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a viscous variable generalized Chaplygin gas (VVGCG) model with volume-dependent parameter B and bulk viscosity ξ ∝ ρ^{1/2}, showing it yields a physically viable cosmic evolution—early deceleration, late-time phantom-like acceleration—and satisfies full thermodynamic consistency (positive heat capacity, entropy vanishing at T→0, stability, fundamental identity) for n<0 and ξ₀<0; Hubble-57 data constrain α≈0.0024, n≈−0.0182, ξ₀≈−0.0015.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a cosmologically viable and thermodynamically consistent dark energy/dark fluid model that unifies early deceleration and late-time phantom-like acceleration, while generalizing existing Chaplygin gas models and incorporating realistic bulk viscosity and variable equation-of-state parameters.

Method: Analytical and phenomenological modeling in a flat FLRW universe: (i) adopt VVGCG equation of state P = −B/ρ^α with B = B₀V^{n/3}; (ii) include bulk-viscous pressure Π = −3ξH, where ξ = ξ₀ρ^{1/2}; (iii) solve Friedmann and continuity equations to derive cosmological dynamics (expansion history, transition volumes V_c, V_f); (iv) perform full thermodynamic analysis—derive thermal/caloric equations of state, entropy S(T,V), heat capacity c_V, stability criteria (isothermal/adiabatic compressibility, c_V > 0), and verify the fundamental thermodynamic identity dU = TdS − PdV.

Result: A physically viable branch exists for ξ₀ < 0: early positive effective pressure → deceleration; sign flip of effective pressure at finite V_c; acceleration onset at larger V_f; asymptotic phantom-like w < −1 for n < 0; thermodynamic consistency achieved only for n < 0 and ξ₀ < 0 — satisfying c_V > 0, entropy → 0 as T → 0, finite minimum volume V_min, maximum temperature T_max, and stability; Hubble-57 constraints confirm compatibility with stable region.

Conclusion: The VVGCG model successfully describes the full cosmic expansion history—from radiation/matter-dominated deceleration to late-time phantom-like acceleration—while maintaining strict thermodynamic consistency across all epochs, and subsumes several known Chaplygin gas models as limiting cases.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological dynamics and thermodynamic properties of a viscous variable generalized Chaplygin gas (VVGCG) model in a spatially flat Friedmann--Lema^ıtre--Robertson--Walker universe, described by $P=-B/ρ^α$, where $B=B_0V^{n/3}$. The model includes a bulk-viscous pressure $Π=-3ξH$, with $ξ=ξ_0ρ^{1/2}$. We find that the physically viable branch corresponds to $ξ_0<0$, for which the positive viscous pressure dominates at early times, producing decelerated expansion. As the volume increases, the effective pressure changes sign at a finite volume $V_c$, while the transition to acceleration occurs at a larger volume $V_f$. At large volume, the equation-of-state parameter approaches a phantom-like regime for $n<0$. A detailed thermodynamic analysis of the thermal and caloric equations of state, entropy, heat capacity, and isothermal and adiabatic stability shows that the stability conditions and $c_V>0$ are simultaneously satisfied for $n<0$ and $ξ_0<0$. The entropy vanishes as $T\rightarrow0$, consistent with the third law, while the model admits a finite minimum volume and corresponding maximum temperature. Thermodynamic consistency is further verified through the fundamental thermodynamic identity. The framework also contains several existing Chaplygin-gas models as special cases. Constraints from the Hubble-57 data yield $α=0.0024$, $n=-0.0182$, and $ξ_0=-0.0015$, consistent with the thermodynamically stable parameter region. Thus, the VVGCG model provides a consistent description of cosmic evolution from an early decelerating phase to late-time phantom-like acceleration while remaining thermodynamically stable throughout the evolution.

</details>


### [24] [Ellis-Bronnikov wormholes with NUT charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05656)
*Masato Nozawa,Tomohiro Harada,Keisuke Nakashi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs exact NUT-charged wormhole solutions in Einstein-phantom-scalar gravity—both asymptotically locally flat and AdS variants—showing regularity, geodesic completeness, finite conserved charges, and an emergent ambi-Kähler structure upon Euclideanization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
