Motivation: To extend known wormhole geometries (e.g., Ellis-Bronnikov) by incorporating NUT charge while preserving regularity and physical viability—addressing open questions about consistent coupling of NUT charge with traversable wormholes and their thermodynamic/gravitational charges in scalar-gravity theories.

Method: Exact solution construction in the Einstein-phantom-scalar system; analysis of global geometry (asymptotics, singularities, CTCs, geodesic completeness); covariant phase space formalism for Hamiltonian charges; double Wick rotation and conformal geometry analysis to establish ambi-Kähler structure.

Result: 1) Exact stationary, singularity-free, asymptotically locally flat NUT-wormhole generalizing Ellis-Bronnikov; 2) Analogous asymptotically locally AdS NUT-wormhole interpolating between distinct AdS vacua; 3) Finiteness of Hamiltonian charges under suitable scalar boundary conditions; 4) Emergence of ambi-Kähler structure in the Euclidean conformal class.

Conclusion: NUT-charged wormholes can be consistently realized in phantom-scalar gravity without pathologies like curvature singularities or horizon-induced geodesic incompleteness; their asymptotic charges are well-defined, and their Euclidean counterparts admit rich geometric structures (ambi-Kähler), suggesting deeper connections to supersymmetry or holography.

Abstract: We construct an exact NUT-charged generalization of the Ellis-Bronnikov wormhole in the Einstein-phantom-scalar system. The solution is stationary, asymptotically locally flat at both ends and free of curvature singularities. It inherits characteristic features of the Taub-NUT geometry, including the Dirac-Misner string and closed timelike curves, while the absence of Killing horizons eliminates the horizon-induced infinite winding of geodesics and renders the spacetime geodesically complete. By allowing the phantom field to have a nontrivial scalar potential with an infinite sequence of AdS vacua, we also obtain an exact asymptotically locally AdS wormhole with a NUT charge, which interpolates between different vacua at its two asymptotic ends. Using the covariant phase space formalism, we show that the Hamiltonian charges associated with the two asymptotic regions are finite under appropriate scalar boundary conditions. By performing a double Wick rotation to Euclidean signature, we demonstrate that the conformal class of the resulting metric admits an ambi-Kähler structure.

</details>


### [25] [Exact black hole solutions in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05799)
*Lin-Yi She,Rui-Jie Niu,Ke Yang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies static, spherically symmetric black holes in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity—a Lorentz-violating extension of GR where the Kalb-Ramond field acquires a vacuum expectation value. It derives exact black hole solutions for all Λ (positive, zero, negative), computes conserved mass via Iyer-Wald formalism, confirms first law and Smarr relation for Λ ≤ 0 (identifying mass as thermodynamic enthalpy), and constrains Lorentz violation using solar system tests—most tightly via Mercury’s perihelion precession, bounding q = ℓ₂/(1−ℓ₁) to [−7.4×10⁻¹², 3.7×10⁻¹¹].


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore consistent Lorentz violation in gravity via spontaneous symmetry breaking by the Kalb-Ramond field, and to assess its observational viability—particularly whether Palatini formulation allows nontrivial Lorentz-violating parameters while remaining compatible with black hole thermodynamics and weak-field solar system tests.

Method: Palatini variational principle applied to Kalb-Ramond gravity with torsion-free connection; derivation of modified field equations; exact integration for spherically symmetric, static metric; Iyer-Wald Noether charge formalism for conserved mass; thermodynamic analysis (first law, Smarr relation) for Λ ≤ 0; post-Newtonian expansion and comparison with Mercury perihelion shift, light deflection, and Shapiro delay.

Result: Exact black hole solutions exist for all Λ; both Lorentz-violating parameters ℓ₁, ℓ₂ can be simultaneously nonzero only in Palatini (not metric) formulation; conserved mass is identified as enthalpy for Λ ≤ 0; solar system tests constrain only the combination q = ℓ₂/(1−ℓ₁); Mercury’s perihelion precession gives |q| ≲ 10⁻¹¹.

Conclusion: Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity provides a theoretically consistent, phenomenologically viable framework for spontaneous Lorentz violation; it admits physically meaningful black hole solutions with well-defined thermodynamics, and current solar system data strongly constrain—but do not rule out—the Lorentz-violating sector.

Abstract: We investigate static and spherically symmetric black holes in Palatini Kalb-Ramond gravity, where a nonzero vacuum expectation value of the Kalb-Ramond field spontaneously breaks local Lorentz symmetry. Treating the metric and the torsion-free affine connection as independent variables, we derive the modified field equations and obtain an exact family of solutions encompassing positive, vanishing, and negative values of the cosmological constant $Λ$. Unlike the corresponding purely metric formulation, the Palatini theory consistently allows both Lorentz-violating parameters $\ell_1$ and $\ell_2$ to be nonzero. Using the Iyer-Wald formalism, we determine the conserved mass of the black hole solutions. For the $Λ\leq0$ sector, we further verify the first law and the Smarr relation, identifying the conserved mass as the thermodynamic enthalpy. We then confront the $Λ=0$ solution with Mercury's perihelion precession, solar light deflection, and the Shapiro time delay. Once the central mass parameter is calibrated by planetary dynamics, these observables constrain the Lorentz-violating parameters only through $q\equiv\ell_2/(1-\ell_1)$. Among these tests, Mercury's perihelion precession yields the most stringent constraint, $-7.4\times10^{-12} \leq q \leq 3.7\times10^{-11}$.

</details>


### [26] [Gravitational-wave Echoes Beyond the Hubble Time from Quantum Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05886)
*Che-Yu Chen,Naritaka Oshita,Yuki Yokokura*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes gravitational-wave echoes from a horizonless compact object model derived from semi-classical gravity and finds that odd-parity waves are trapped internally with echo delays exceeding the age of the Universe—rendering echoes effectively unobservable and consistent with current null detections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the absence of observed gravitational-wave echoes in LIGO/Virgo data by examining whether horizonless quantum black hole models (that reproduce black hole entropy) can produce detectable echoes.

Method: Uses a non-perturbative, semi-classical Einstein solution modeling a horizonless compact object with anisotropic fluid interior in classical GR; analytically studies odd-parity gravitational wave propagation and reflection at the surface under strong redshift effects.

Result: Odd-parity gravitational waves are not reflected but exponentially redshifted and trapped inside the object; resulting echo-delay time ≫ age of Universe → echoes are undetectable.

Conclusion: Current null echo searches do not rule out this quantum black hole model; distinguishing it from classical black holes requires probing ultra-long-timescale or non-echo signatures (e.g., late-time ringdown deviations, tidal deformability, or quantum hair imprints).

Abstract: We investigate gravitational-wave echoes in a horizonless compact object model, a non-perturbative solution of the semi-classical Einstein equation that reproduces the entropy-area law from the bulk structure. By employing a model of anisotropic fluids in classical general relativity (GR), we show that odd-parity gravitational waves are not reflected at the object's surface but are trapped inside due to the exponentially strong redshift, and that the echo-delay time extremely exceeds the age of the Universe. Consequently, the echo signals are effectively unobservable, which is consistent with current null results from echo searches. We finally discuss how this quantum black hole and the conventional classical black hole could be distinguished observationally.

</details>


### [27] [Exact Thermodynamics and Two-Horizon Relations of Four Rotating Einstein-Maxwell-Maxwell-Dilaton Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06037)

  <summary>Details</summary>

Method: The authors employ exact solution-generating techniques (constant-coefficient field maps) to construct rotating embeddings of Kerr-Newman and Kaluza-Klein seeds in 4D Einstein-Maxwell-Maxwell-dilaton theory. They derive single-valued mass functions, compute horizon thermodynamic variables (temperature T±, entropy S±, angular velocity Ω±), and rigorously verify the first law of black hole mechanics, the Smarr relation, and a scalar-shift identity on regular branches and finite-area extremal boundaries, incorporating magnetic patch contributions as needed.

Result: A complete outer-horizon thermodynamic description is established, including verification of the first law, Smarr formula, and scalar-shift identity. Crucially, for two distinct regular horizons, the combinations 2T±S± and S±Ω±/π are shown to satisfy a common quadratic relation whose coefficient—the long-range-force parameter—is fully determined by the asymptotic mass, electromagnetic charges, and scalar charge.

Conclusion: The thermodynamics of these rotating dilatonic black holes is fully consistent and governed by universal relations; the discovery of a shared quadratic constraint linking inner and outer horizon thermodynamics via the long-range-force parameter reveals a deep structural feature of the theory's black hole spectrum.

Abstract: We establish complete outer-horizon thermodynamics for four exact rotating embeddings of Kerr--Newman and Kaluza--Klein seeds in four-dimensional, asymptotically flat Einstein--Maxwell--Maxwell--dilaton theory without a scalar potential. Constant-coefficient field maps determine the allowed couplings and charge variations. We obtain single-valued mass functions and verify the first law, Smarr relation, and scalar-shift identity on the stated regular branches and finite-area extremal boundaries, including magnetic patch contributions where required. For two distinct regular horizons with positive finite areas, the horizon combinations $2T_\pm S_\pm$ and $S_\pmΩ_\pm/π$ satisfy a common quadratic relation with the long-range-force parameter determined by the asymptotic mass, electromagnetic charges, and scalar charge.

</details>


### [28] [Black Hole Horizon Fluctuations from Unruh Quantum Noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06223)
*Kashif Ammar Yasir*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a stochastic geometric reconstruction of Schwarzschild black holes using the full Unruh Wightman function to quantify how quantum stress-energy correlations—not just mean Hawking flux—affect horizon radius and surface gravity, finding distinct sensitivities governed by covariance derivatives.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The mean Hawking flux is insufficient to characterize quantum backreaction on black-hole geometry; field correlations—including vacuum contributions not captured in luminosity—encode the relevant geometric perturbations.

Method: The authors use the complete exterior Unruh Wightman function, retain both incoming vacuum and outgoing frequency branches, compute the connected stress-energy covariance before angular projection, propagate its monopole via a density-driven radial response, and apply a shared Gaussian stochastic reconstruction to relate lapse function value and derivative to geometric observables (horizon radius, surface gravity).

Result: From 2,200 realizations, they obtain relative uncertainties of 0.0185 for local horizon radius and 0.0483 for surface-gravity temperature; these unequal sensitivities are explained by derivatives of the stress covariance; histograms and reconstruction checks confirm consistency.

Conclusion: Quantum stress-energy correlations—beyond mean flux—deterministically shape horizon and surface-gravity statistics; the framework cleanly separates quantum-state input from gravitational closure and spatial sampling assumptions, enabling reproducible finite-resolution modeling of quantum-geometric backreaction.

Abstract: The mean Hawking flux does not determine how quantum stress fluctuations perturb a black-hole geometry. That information resides in field correlations, including vacuum contributions absent from the outgoing luminosity. Here we connect the complete exterior Unruh Wightman function to a finite-band stochastic reconstruction of Schwarzschild geometry. We retain the incoming vacuum and both outgoing frequency branches, form the connected stress covariance before angular projection, and propagate its monopole through a density-driven radial response. A shared Gaussian reconstruction then resolves how the value and derivative of the lapse control different geometric observables. For the specified covariance and noise amplitude, 2,200 realizations give relative widths of 0.0185 for the local horizon radius and 0.0483 for the surface-gravity temperature parameter, consistently across histograms and reconstruction checks. Covariance derivatives explain this unequal sensitivity. The construction separates the quantum-state input from the gravitational closure and spatial sampling assumptions, providing a reproducible finite-resolution description of how Hawking-field correlations affect horizon positions and surface-gravity statistics.

</details>


### [29] [Scalaron Quasinormal and Quasi-Bound Modes of Schwarzschild Black Holes in the Bondi-Sachs Formulation of Metric $f(R)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06370)

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scalaron quasinormal modes and quasi-bound states of Schwarzschild black holes in metric f(R) gravity using the Bondi-Sachs formalism, combining frequency-domain (Leaver’s method) and time-domain (characteristic evolution + matrix-pencil analysis) techniques; results confirm consistency between methods and show scalar spectra depend only on the dimensionless parameter μM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously investigate scalaron dynamics—specifically quasinormal modes (QNMs) and quasi-bound states—in metric f(R) gravity beyond standard perturbative GR frameworks, leveraging the geometrically transparent Bondi-Sachs formalism for clean causal evolution and boundary treatment.

Method: Linearised Bondi-Sachs characteristic formulation of metric f(R) gravity; derivation of massive scalar master equation and confirmation of GR-like tensor master equation; Leaver's continued-fraction method for QNM/quasi-bound spectra; fourth-order convergent characteristic time evolution; matrix-pencil analysis applied to time-domain waveforms for spectral extraction.

Result: Scalar QNM and quasi-bound spectra depend solely on the dimensionless combination μM; fundamental QNM frequencies recovered independently in both frequency and time domains agree within numerical uncertainty; for a finite-mass case, both oscillation frequency and damping rate of the fundamental QNM and quasi-bound state are extracted from distinct time windows of a single waveform at one radius, without artificial boundary imposition.

Conclusion: The Bondi-Sachs framework provides a robust, stable, and physically faithful setting for studying scalaron dynamics in f(R) gravity; time-domain and frequency-domain results are mutually consistent, validating the approach and enabling unambiguous separation of QNM and quasi-bound contributions directly from waveform data.

Abstract: We study scalaron quasinormal modes and quasi-bound states of Schwarzschild black holes directly within the Bondi-Sachs formulation of metric $f(R)$ gravity, combining frequency-domain analyses with characteristic time evolution. Starting from the linearised characteristic equations, we obtain the massive scalar master equation and show that the tensor master equation takes the same form as in general relativity. For the analytic models considered here, the linear scalar spectra depend on the $f(R)$ theory only through the dimensionless combination $μM$. We compute both quasinormal and quasi-bound spectral branches using Leaver's continued-fraction method and compare them with independent literature benchmarks. To complement the frequency domain analysis, we evolve the scalaron perturbation equations using a fourth order convergent characteristic scheme. Applying matrix-pencil stability scans to the resulting waveforms, we independently recover fundamental quasinormal frequencies consistent with the continued-fraction results within the measured extraction-stability scales. Finally, for a selected finite-mass case, we demonstrate the extraction of both oscillation frequencies and damping rates of the fundamental quasinormal mode and quasi-bound state from separate time windows of the same waveform at one extraction radius, without imposing either spectral branch at the finite outer boundary.

</details>


### [30] [Time-domain analysis of scalar perturbations of Kerr--Newman black holes with Chern--Simons and Maxwell couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06393)
*Yan-Ming Chen,Hyat Huang,Meng-Yun Lai,De-Cheng Zou,Yun Soo Myung,Ming Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies scalarization onset in Kerr–Newman black holes within the Einstein–Chern–Simons–Maxwell–scalar theory, using hyperboloidal Rácz–Tóth evolution and zero-mode analysis to determine critical coupling thresholds for linear scalar perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the conditions under which spontaneous scalarization initiates around charged, rotating black holes in a modified gravity theory featuring both Chern–Simons and Maxwell–scalar couplings.

Method: Linear stability analysis of scalar perturbations around Kerr–Newman black holes; numerical hyperboloidal Rácz–Tóth time-domain evolution and stationary axisymmetric zero-mode (threshold mode) computation.

Result: Two real, asymmetric scalarization threshold branches in η emerge for γ̂ = 0; when γ̂ = η, branches have opposite signs and unequal magnitudes; for γ̂ ≠ η, branches merge as spin or CS coupling increases.

Conclusion: Scalarization onset is highly sensitive to the relative strengths of Chern–Simons and Maxwell couplings, and to black hole spin—merging of thresholds signals a qualitative change in instability structure, possibly indicating a transition to complex or non-axisymmetric instabilities.

Abstract: To study the onset of scalarization, we investigate linear scalar perturbations around Kerr--Newman black holes in the Einstein-Chern-Simons-Maxwell-scalar theory with quadratic Chern--Simons (CS) coupling $γ$ ($\hatγ=γ/M^2$) and Maxwell coupling $η$. For this purpose, we employ hyperboloidal Rácz--Tóth evolution and a stationary axisymmetric zero-mode computation. The resulting thresholds are compared with the time-domain response at representative points. For $\hatγ=0$, we find two asymmetric threshold branches in $η$, while for $\hatγ=η$, the two branches have opposite signs and unequal magnitudes. For unequal CS and Maxwell couplings, the two real threshold branches merge as the spin or CS coupling increases.

</details>


### [31] [Dyonic Simpson Visser black-bounce spacetime surrounded by an exotic Einstein cluster in general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06523)
*Pedro Cañate,Joseph Sultana,Demosthenes Kazanas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a dyonic (electric + magnetic) Simpson-Visser black-bounce spacetime as an exact GR solution sourced by Maxwell fields and an anisotropic Einstein cluster fluid with zero radial pressure — yielding a regular black hole with traversable bounce geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically motivated, regular black-bounce spacetime that avoids singularities and satisfies energy conditions as much as possible, using realistic matter sources — specifically, combining electromagnetic fields with an Einstein cluster (a gravitationally bound system of orbiting particles generating only tangential pressure).

Method: Solving the Einstein–Maxwell field equations exactly, assuming a static, spherically symmetric Simpson-Visser metric ansatz, and modeling the exotic matter required for the bounce throat as an anisotropic fluid with p_r = 0 (Einstein cluster), while including both electric and magnetic charges in the Maxwell field.

Result: Derivation of the exact dyonic Simpson-Visser black-bounce metric as a solution; demonstration that the Einstein cluster provides the necessary negative radial pressure (effectively exotic matter) to sustain the throat, while the electromagnetic field contributes to curvature and charge structure; confirmation of regularity (no curvature singularities) and black-bounce character (smooth transition across the throat).

Conclusion: The dyonic SV-BB spacetime is a viable regular black-bounce model grounded in physically interpretable matter content — Maxwell fields plus an Einstein cluster — offering a singularity-free alternative to Schwarzschild-like black holes within classical GR.

Abstract: In this work, we present the Simpson-Visser black-bounce (SV-BB) spacetime with both electric and magnetic charges (the dyonic SV-BB) as an exact solution of general relativity coupled to Maxwell electrodynamics and an anisotropic fluid with vanishing radial pressure. We assume the anisotropic fluid represents an Einstein cluster, which consists of a dense system of particles moving in circular orbits that generate only tangential pressure and zero radial pressure. We show that the dyonic SV-BB metric, which provides a regular black hole spacetime, can be obtained as an exact solution to the Einstein field equations sourced by a combination of an Einstein cluster (which is responsible for providing the exotic matter required to keep a black-bounce throat open) and an electromagnetic field in the framework of Maxwell electrodynamics.

</details>


### [32] [Cherenkov Bursts are Memory Steps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06528)
*Jann Zosso,Guangzi Xu,Vitor Cardoso*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper reinterprets the Cherenkov radiation from a finite superluminal charge track as interference between two electromagnetic memory transitions induced by endpoint accelerations; this explains the finite burst via cancellation of divergent memory steps, and suggests the Askaryan effect as a testbed for resolving macroscopic memory transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a new physical interpretation of the finite Cherenkov burst—beyond standard wavefront superposition—by linking it to infrared memory effects and enabling experimental access to classical electromagnetic memory transitions.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic analysis of the transverse vector potential generated by a finite superluminal charge trajectory, focusing on asymptotic (radiative) behavior and memory contributions from endpoint accelerations; interference of memory steps is examined in observer time near the Cherenkov angle.

Result: Demonstration that the Cherenkov burst arises from destructive interference of two divergent memory-induced vector-potential steps whose amplitudes grow and temporal separation shrinks as the Cherenkov direction is approached, yielding a finite, universal low-frequency radiation profile.

Conclusion: Cherenkov radiation from a finite track is an interference phenomenon of electromagnetic memory transitions, implying that macroscopic memory effects can be resolved experimentally—e.g., via coherent radio emission in the Askaryan effect—thereby bridging infrared physics with observable high-energy phenomena.

Abstract: We show that the familiar Cherenkov burst from a finite superluminal charge track can be understood as a pair of interfering electromagnetic memory transitions. Endpoint accelerations of the track produce opposite memory steps in the transverse vector potential, such that the corresponding finite-frequency radiation is universally fixed, below the endpoint-acceleration-scale, by the memory amplitude and the time for which the temporary memory offset persists. As the Cherenkov direction is approached, the memory amplitudes grow while their separation in observer time shrinks, until the two vector-potential steps overlap and their divergent contributions cancel into a finite Cherenkov burst. This perspective opens the door to using the Askaryan effect as a setting in which individual macroscopic memory transitions may be resolved, providing a new experimental arena for memory-linked infrared physics.

</details>


### [33] [Puncture Evolution with Physics-Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06776)
*Estuti Shukla,Ashwin Suriyanarayanan,David Radice,Romit Maulik,Sebastiano Bernuzzi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) to solve the vacuum Einstein equations in spherical symmetry under the moving-puncture gauge, demonstrating their ability to interpolate, extrapolate, and reconstruct spacetime—including recovering Schwarzschild geometry from exterior-only data—while assessing their viability for numerical relativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether PINNs can robustly solve constrained, geometric PDEs like the vacuum Einstein equations in a physically consistent gauge (moving-puncture), especially where traditional numerical methods face singularities or require complex coordinate handling.

Method: Designs a PINN that encodes lapse, shift, and Z4c conformal variables (χ, γ̃_rr) — all regular at the puncture — and minimizes a composite loss comprising NR data fidelity, Ricci tensor residuals (enforcing vacuum Einstein equations), and moving-puncture gauge condition residuals.

Result: PINNs successfully interpolate NR data, evolve black-hole spacetimes from sparse early-time/boundary data, and solve an ill-posed inverse problem (reconstructing interior spacetime from exterior-only data), recovering Schwarzschild geometry and learning the coordinate transformation to Kerr–Schild coordinates.

Conclusion: PINNs offer a promising, differentiable, coordinate-flexible framework for solving geometric PDEs in numerical relativity—particularly for interpolation, surrogate modeling, and inverse reconstruction—but face challenges in stability, generalization, and computational cost for high-fidelity, long-term evolutions.

Abstract: We investigate the application of physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) to solve the vacuum Einstein equations in spherical symmetry. Our neural network architecture is designed to solve the 1+1D moving-puncture evolution, by minimizing a loss that combines a data term, the Ricci tensor of the reconstructed spacetime, and the moving-puncture gauge conditions. It represents the lapse, the shift and the Z4c conformal variables $χ$ and $\widetildeγ_{rr}$, which remain regular at the puncture. We consider the evolution of a single, non-spinning black-hole in the puncture gauge and show that PINNs can interpolate numerical relativity (NR) data, predict its evolution, and reconstruct spacetime in regions where no data is provided. As an interpolant, the network provides a compact, differentiable representation of the NR data. Given only early-time and outer-boundary data, it evolves the puncture forward in time and reproduces the main features of the NR solution. Given only exterior data and no gauge condition, an ill-posed inverse problem, it recovers the interior of a Schwarzschild spacetime in coordinates of its own choosing, which we verify by learning the coordinate transformation to the Kerr--Schild form. Finally, we assess the strengths and limitations of PINNs for NR and outline their prospects for future applications.

</details>

### [34] [Supplementary Notes on VOA and 2D SCFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03725)
*Taizan Watari*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This note organizes non-holomorphic 2D CFT concepts using vertex operator (super)algebra formalism, introduces crossing symmetry and intertwining superoperators for Ramond sectors via two auxiliary parameters, and generalizes to simple current extensions under four precise algebraic–cohomological conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically organize and clarify subtle aspects of (non-holomorphic) 2D conformal field theory—particularly beyond standard holomorphic treatments—using the rigorous algebraic framework of vertex operator algebras and superalgebras, supplementing introductory physics pedagogy.

Method: Uses vertex operator algebra (VOA) and vertex operator superalgebra (SVOA) formalisms; introduces two auxiliary parameters to formulate crossing symmetry and intertwining superoperators for Ramond and Ramond–Ramond sectors; derives conditions for consistent simple current extensions, invoking cohomological (H³ = 0), bilinear (skew-symmetric bimultiplicative form), group-action (fixed-point-free), and an additional structural condition.

Result: A unified SVOA-based formulation of Type 0 CFTs including Ramond-sector crossing symmetry and intertwining superoperators; a systematic classification framework for simple current extensions satisfying four explicit algebraic–topological conditions.

Conclusion: Non-holomorphic 2D CFTs—including Type 0 theories with fermions—admit rigorous description via SVOAs; the auxiliary-parameter technique enables consistent handling of twisted sectors, and the derived four-condition criterion fully characterizes admissible simple current extensions.

Abstract: Sections 2--3 of this note were prepared primarily for the author's private use, as a means of organizing the materials in (generally non-holomorphic) conformal field theory (CFT) on 2-dimensions in the language of the vertex operator algebra, and also of collecting subtle details. The choice of topics is intended to supplement introductory physics textbooks on the subject.
  Section 4 deals with (generally non-holomorphic) Type 0 CFTs in the language of vertex operator superalgebra (SVOA). Formulations of crossing symmetry and intertwining superoperators [resp. CFT vertex superoperators] of states in the Ramond (SVOA-twisted) sector [resp. the Ramond--Ramond sector] are presented by using two auxiliary parameters. The formulation allows systematic generalization to the class of simple current extensions characterized by four conditions: (1) the vanishing third cohomology, (2) skew-symmetric bimultiplicative form, (3) fixed-point-free action of simple currents, and (4) one additional condition described in the main text.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
