Abstract: We demonstrate that Maxwell electrodynamics interacting with a planar Chern-Simons interface does not have plane wave solutions localized on the interface. We re-examine the derivation of such waves presented by N. Kaloper in [Phys.Rev.D114(2026)L051903] and show that at an intermediate step certain terms were missed in an equation therein. Taking these terms into account changes the final conclusion of [Phys.Rev.D114(2026)L051903]. We also show that the method used in [arXiv:2606.12513 [hep-th]] to reproduce the same result is not consistent.

</details>


### [36] [Complex Worldline Instantons in (Anti-)de Sitter Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03862)
*Alberto Castellano,Jinwei Chu,Eliseu Kloster,Jamie A. P. Law-Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes complex saddle points of the worldline path integral for charged particles in 2D maximally symmetric spacetimes (AdS, dS, Minkowski) with homogeneous electric fields, showing that semiclassical solutions require complexification of both spacetime and proper time; it classifies instanton sectors topologically, identifies regime-dependent behavior in AdS (governed by R|eE|/m), and provides a residue-based reformulation of the action.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand non-perturbative particle production (e.g., Schwinger effect) in curved spacetimes using the worldline formalism, where real-time semiclassical approximations fail and complex saddles become essential.

Method: Worldline path integral formalism; complex saddle-point analysis with simultaneous complexification of target spacetime coordinates and proper-time parameter; topological classification via winding numbers around compact directions/punctures; residue calculus applied to non-conserved conjugate momentum; global lifting constraints from local patches to (A)dS.

Result: 1) Complex saddles form a unified family split into real- and imaginary-proper-time branches governing interference and suppression respectively; 2) Instanton actions depend solely on topological data (winding numbers); 3) In AdS, three qualitatively distinct regimes (sub/extremal/super-extremal) emerge governed by R|eE|/m, absent in dS and Minkowski; 4) Action admits residue-sum representation; 5) Global consistency imposes additional selection rules on contributing saddles.

Conclusion: The worldline semiclassical analysis of charged particle dynamics in curved backgrounds necessitates a fully complex geometric framework; topological and analytic structure—rather than local geometry alone—determines non-perturbative physics, with AdS exhibiting unique critical behavior tied to curvature-to-field strength scaling.

Abstract: We study complex saddle points of the worldline path integral for massive charged particles in two-dimensional maximally symmetric backgrounds threaded by homogeneous electric fields. Within the worldline formalism, we show that the semiclassical saddles generically require complexifying both the target spacetime and the proper-time parameter. The resulting solutions comprise two slices of a single complex family, distinguished by the direction of proper-time evolution. The one evolving in imaginary proper time carries an action that typically controls the exponential suppression of non-perturbative effects, while that evolving in real proper time may encode additional complex phases responsible for quantum interference between saddles. Treating this family as a whole, we give a prescription for constructing and classifying all topologically inequivalent instanton sectors, and we find that the worldline action depends only on their topological data, namely winding numbers around the compact directions and certain isolated punctures. Moreover, in Anti-de Sitter spacetime, the qualitative structure of the solutions is governed by the dimensionless ratio $R |eE|/m$ ($R$ being the curvature radius), which distinguishes sub-extremal, extremal, and super-extremal regimes. By contrast, no analogous distinction occurs in de Sitter---where the instantons are insensitive to this ratio---or in Minkowski spacetime, which lacks a curvature scale altogether. We also recast the action as a sum over residues of the relevant (non-conserved) conjugate momentum, making transparent which analytic data control each contribution. Finally, we discuss the subtleties that arise when solutions built in various local patches are lifted to global (Anti-)de Sitter space. These impose further necessary conditions on which saddles can in principle contribute to the one-loop path integral.

</details>


### [37] [Magnetic Higher-Form Symmetries as Dual Fundamental $\infty$-Groupoids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03865)
*Alonso Perez-Lona*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a higher-geometric framework for magnetic higher-form symmetries in pre-quantum field theories, using stacky targets and cohesive homotopy theory; it establishes duality theorems (fibration and factorization) linking magnetic symmetries to electric ones, homology, and gauge structure, illustrated via Pin±(2) gauge theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically formalize magnetic higher-form symmetries—especially their topological charges, backgrounds, and action on field theories—beyond standard Pontryagin duality, by leveraging higher geometry, cohesion, and stacky sigma-models.

Method: Uses higher topos theory and cohesive homotopy type theory: encodes magnetic charges via the shape modality of stacky targets; constructs magnetic symmetry backgrounds via op-dualization against moduli stacks of topological field theories; applies homotopy-fixed-point spectral sequences for gauged theories; specializes to E∞-grouplike dualizing stacks and U(1)-gerbe moduli.

Result: Proves two main theorems: (i) a fibration theorem expressing magnetic symmetries of an electrically gauged theory as a fiber sequence involving those of the ungauged theory and gauge group, computable via a spectral sequence; (ii) a factorization theorem showing magnetic backgrounds for connected targets split non-canonically into Pontryagin duals of integral homology groups and are necessarily flat when dualizing discrete charges against connection-enhanced targets. Applies both to Pin±(2) gauge theory, revealing distinct Z₂ magnetic symmetries in degrees d−3, d−4, d−5.

Conclusion: Magnetic higher-form symmetries are intrinsically geometric and cohomological, governed by shape, cohesion, and duality in higher stacks; their classification and behavior under gauging are rigorously captured by homotopical fibrations and homological factorizations—providing a foundational framework for anomaly matching, duality webs, and topological phases.

Abstract: We develop a higher-geometric framework for magnetic higher-form symmetries of pre-quantum field theories. The magnetic (topological) charges of a sigma-model with stacky target are encoded by the shape of the target, equivalently of its stack of fields, which is op-dual to the flat automorphism group controlling electric symmetries. Magnetic symmetry backgrounds are obtained by dualizing these charges against a chosen moduli stack of topological field theories, generalizing Pontryagin duality, and act on the theory by stacking. Cohesive modalities allow for targets with non-finite and non-discrete data. When the dualizing stack is a grouplike $E_\infty$-object, we prove a fibration theorem expressing the magnetic symmetries of an electrically gauged theory in terms of those of the ungauged theory and of the gauge group, computed by a homotopy-fixed-point spectral sequence with an explicit first page. For moduli stacks of higher $U(1)$-gerbes with connection, encoding bulk topological local Lagrangians, we prove a factorization theorem: the magnetic symmetry backgrounds of any connected target split, non-canonically, into Pontryagin duals of its integral homology groups, and are necessarily flat, as a consequence of dualizing discrete charges against a target with connections. We illustrate both theorems with pure $\mathrm{Pin}^\pm(2)$ gauge theory: $O(2)$ carries invertible magnetic $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetries in form degrees $d-3$, $d-4$ and $d-5$ that are absent for $\mathrm{Pin}^-(2)$, the first two distinguished by a single differential of the spectral sequence. Gauging and physical applications are developed in upcoming work.

</details>


### [38] [SU($N$) QCD, four-manifolds and Segre numbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03874)
*Elias Furrer,Ranveer Kumar Singh*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes topological correlators of twisted N=2 SU(N) QCD on compact 4-manifolds using Coulomb branch integrals with background flavor fluxes; it links instanton contributions to virtual Segre numbers of rank-N sheaves, establishes a universal dictionary between Segre series and Coulomb couplings at N=1 vacua, and verifies it for SU(3) with N_f=1–5 using advanced tools including Siegel modular forms, Z_N symmetry, curve factorization, and blowup formula.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify physical computation of topological correlators in twisted N=2 SQCD with algebro-geometric invariants—specifically virtual Segre numbers—and to extend the Coulomb branch formalism to arbitrary N and flavor backgrounds, resolving the role of instanton contributions in the N=1 vacuum decomposition.

Method: Derives general Coulomb branch integral for twisted SU(N) N=2 QCD on X with b_2^+ > 1 and simple type; incorporates background flavor fluxes; identifies contributions from N=1 vacua (instanton branch) via adjoint mass deformation; constructs dictionary between universal Segre generating series and Coulomb couplings at those vacua; employs higher-rank techniques: Siegel modular forms, exact Z_N and charge-conjugation symmetries, Seiberg–Witten curve factorization, and blowup formula.

Result: A complete dictionary for all N linking universal Segre series to Coulomb branch couplings at N=1 vacua; closed-form expressions for all 13 couplings in SU(3) QCD for N_f = 1,…,5; exact agreement with Göttsche–Kool’s virtual rank-3 Segre numbers; recovery of SU(N) Donaldson invariants for pure theory (N_f = 0), consistent with Mariño–Moore.

Conclusion: Topological correlators of twisted N=2 SU(N) QCD encode virtual Segre numbers of rank-N sheaves; the instanton branch contribution is fully captured by Coulomb couplings at N=1 vacua; this provides a physical derivation and generalization of algebro-geometric invariants, validated for SU(3) and consistent with prior results for pure gauge theory.

Abstract: We study correlation functions of topologically twisted $\mathcal N=2$ supersymmetric QCD with gauge group $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ on a compact four-manifold $X$. We derive the Coulomb branch integral for all $N$, including new couplings to background fluxes for the flavor symmetry. For $X$ of simple type with $b_2^+>1$, the correlators become sums over a finite number of strong-coupling singularities on the Coulomb branch, and can be expressed using a number of Coulomb branch couplings. The sum contains the $\mathcal N=1$ vacua, which become the supersymmetric vacua when the $\mathcal N=2$ theory is broken to $\mathcal N=1$ by an adjoint mass deformation, and whose contribution we attribute to the instanton branch. We focus on asymptotically free $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ QCD with $N_f<2N$ equal mass hypermultiplets, and conjecture that its instanton contribution to topological correlators agrees with the generating function of virtual Segre numbers of rank $N$, which are intersection numbers on the moduli space of semistable sheaves on an algebraic surface. We present a precise dictionary for all $N$ between universal series for Segre numbers and Coulomb branch couplings at the $\mathcal N=1$ vacua. The calculation of these couplings requires new higher-rank techniques, involving Siegel modular forms, an exact $\mathbb Z_N$ symmetry, charge conjugation symmetry, factorisation of the Seiberg--Witten curve, and the blowup formula. For pure $\mathrm{SU}(N)$, our result agrees with earlier work by Mariño and Moore, and the twisted correlators give the $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ Donaldson invariants of $X$. For $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ QCD, we determine closed-form expressions for all 13 Coulomb branch couplings for each $N_f=1,\dots, 5$, and find precise agreement with results of Göttsche and Kool for virtual rank 3 Segre numbers.

</details>


### [39] [Quantum covariant bit threads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03929)
*Matthew Headrick,Sreeman Reddy Kasireddy,Andrew Rolph*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces quantum covariant bit threads as an alternative to the quantum extremal surface (QES) formula for computing holographic entanglement entropy, reformulating it in terms of bulk flows—spacelike V-flows and timelike U-flows—with endpoints governed by bulk quantum entanglement; the QES emerges as a 'quantum bottleneck' where flow lines either pass through (contributing area) or jump across (contributing entropy), offering new geometric intuition for islands, evaporation, and baby universes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a flow-based, geometrically intuitive reformulation of the quantum extremal surface (QES) formula that accounts for both gravitational dynamics and bulk quantum corrections—especially in non-static, time-dependent settings like black hole evaporation—while complementing and clarifying the physical meaning of generalized entropy.

Method: Derives two QES-equivalent prescriptions—quantum covariant bit threads—based on minimal/maximal bulk vector field flows: spacelike V-flows and timelike U-flows. Introduces bulk source/sink terms whose densities are determined by bulk entanglement entropies, enabling incorporation of quantum corrections. Demonstrates equivalence to the QES formula via max-flow/min-cut theorems generalized to Lorentzian signature and quantum backreaction.

Result: Establishes exact equivalence between quantum bit thread prescriptions and the QES formula; identifies the QES as a 'quantum bottleneck' where flow lines bifurcate—some threading through (encoding area contribution) and others jumping across (encoding bulk entropy contribution); recovers island rules and resolves ambiguities in time-dependent entanglement evolution.

Conclusion: Quantum covariant bit threads provide a physically transparent, flow-theoretic dual to the QES formalism, unifying geometric and information-theoretic aspects of holographic entanglement. They deepen conceptual understanding of quantum gravity effects—particularly in evaporating black holes—and open avenues for studying dynamical entanglement structure beyond static or perturbative regimes.

Abstract: Entanglement entropy plays a central role in holography. The quantum extremal surface (QES) formula is the standard tool in settings where both dynamics and bulk quantum corrections are important, such as in black hole evaporation. We derive and explore QES-equivalent prescriptions --- quantum covariant bit threads --- that formulate holographic entanglement entropy in terms of minimal or maximal bulk flows rather than extremal surfaces. Like classical covariant bit threads, our prescriptions come in two types: (typically) spacelike V-flows, and timelike U-flows. To account for bulk quantum corrections, the flow lines can start and end at points in the bulk, with the distribution of endpoints controlled by bulk entanglement entropies. In our bit thread language, the QES is a quantum bottleneck to the flow, with some threads passing through it (accounting for the area term in the generalized entropy) and others jumping across it (accounting for the entropy term). Our prescriptions provide a complementary perspective to the QES formula and offer new insights on important phenomena such as black hole evaporation, islands, and baby universes.

</details>


### [40] [Exact effective central charges for a family of 3d superconformal field theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03950)
*Griffen Adams*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes an exact formula for the effective central charge (c_eff) of a 3D superconformal field theory family linked to Chern-Simons theory via the 3d-3d correspondence, expressing c_eff in terms of the dimension of a specific space of modular forms; validated using resurgent continuation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the effective central charge governing the asymptotic growth of protected states in a family of 3D SCFTs arising from the 3d-3d correspondence with Chern-Simons theory on 3-manifolds.

Method: Conjectures an exact formula relating c_eff to the dimension of a particular space of modular forms; verification performed via comparison with known results obtained by resurgent continuation.

Result: A conjectured exact expression for c_eff in terms of modular form dimensions, corroborated by consistency with existing resurgent computations.

Conclusion: The proposed modular-form–based formula provides a precise and testable characterization of the effective central charge for this class of 3D SCFTs, strengthening the quantitative understanding of the 3d-3d correspondence.

Abstract: We conjecture an exact formula for the effective central charge, ceff, of a family of three- dimensional superconformal field theories related to Chern-Simons theory on a three-manifold by the 3d-3d correspondence. The formula determines ceff , which governs the growth of a protected sector of states in the conformal theory, in terms of the dimension of a particular space of modular forms. We confirm our approach by checking against known results computed through resurgent continuation.

</details>


### [41] [Wilson line defect indices for M2-brane SCFTs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04065)
*Hirotaka Hayashi,Tomoki Nosaka,Tadashi Okazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes line defect Witten indices in 3d N≥4 supersymmetric gauge theories, deriving general formulae for Coulomb and Higgs limits and uncovering recursion relations for Wilson line defect indices across ADHM- and ABJ(M)-type theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize line defects in 3d N≥4 SCFTs via protected indices, especially to understand their enumerative meaning (invariant states under residual gauge symmetry) and uncover structural recursion patterns across gauge groups.

Method: Computes normalized line defect Coulomb and Higgs indices as ratios of decorated defect indices to ordinary partition functions; takes unit R-charge fugacity limit to obtain integer Witten indices; identifies residual gauge symmetry from vacuum equations; derives algebraic recursion relations for Wilson line indices using supersymmetric localization and representation-theoretic structure of moduli spaces.

Result: 1) Line defect Witten indices are integers counting gauge-invariant states under the residual unbroken gauge group on each branch; 2) General closed-form formulae for these indices in both Coulomb and Higgs limits for CS-free theories; 3) Novel algebraic recursion relations linking Wilson line indices across U(N), USp(2N), SO(2N), SO(2N+1) ADHM and ABJ(M) theories.

Conclusion: Line defect indices in 3d N≥4 SCFTs are deeply tied to residual gauge symmetry and admit universal recursive structures, revealing hidden algebraic consistency across diverse brane constructions and enabling systematic classification of line operators.

Abstract: We investigate line defect Coulomb and Higgs indices in 3d $\mathcal{N}\ge 4$ supersymmetric gauge theories. The normalized line defect indices defined by ratios of the decorated defect indices to the ordinary indices yield well-defined integer values, the line defect Witten indices in the unit R-charge fugacity limit. We propose general formulae for these Witten indices in both Coulomb and Higgs limits for the theories without Chern-Simons terms, and we show that both count the states invariant under the residual gauge group left unbroken at a generic point of the branch, which we read off from the vacuum equations. Furthermore, we find powerful algebraic recursion relations for Wilson line defect Coulomb indices in the 3d SCFTs describing M2-branes, including ADHM-type theories and ABJ(M) theories. These relations imply that higher rank line defect indices are recursively determined by lower rank data across $U(N)$, $USp(2N)$, $SO(2N)$ and $SO(2N+1)$ ADHM-type theories and ABJ(M) theories.

</details>


### [42] [Anomaly-free six-dimensional $Sp(1)_R$-gauged supergravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04131)
*Katrin Becker,Ergin Sezgin,Qi You*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper classifies consistent 6D N=(1,0) supergravity theories with gauged Sp(1)_R symmetry and one tensor multiplet, using anomaly cancellation and lattice embedding constraints; it finds only two possible numbers of vector multiplets—n_V = 12 or 96—and enumerates all anomaly-free spectra for these cases, including drone-free and reductive (A₂×U(1)×Sp(1)_R) branches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify consistent low-energy effective theories of 6D N=(1,0) supergravity coupled to gravity and matter, subject to quantum consistency (anomaly cancellation) and string-theoretic constraints (unimodular charge lattice embedding), thereby constraining viable UV completions and identifying swampland boundaries.

Method: Imposes local gravitational, gauge, and mixed anomaly cancellation conditions on the 6D N=(1,0) supergravity spectrum; embeds anomaly coefficients into a unimodular rank-two string charge lattice; classifies gauge algebras and matter representations satisfying these constraints; performs exhaustive spectra-assembly computations for semisimple and reductive gauge groups, including drone analysis and U(1) charge enumeration.

Result: Only n_V = 12 and n_V = 96 are allowed for consistent theories; n_V = 96 admits no drone-free spectrum within the specified semisimple non-R algebra class; for n_V = 12, the only semisimple non-R branch is A₁³, yielding 4,337,331 models grouped into 102 anomaly-vector classes; the reductive branch A₂×U(1)×Sp(1)_R yields 178,538 solutions across 21 anomaly-vector sets under bounded U(1) charges.

Conclusion: Anomaly cancellation combined with lattice embedding strongly restricts the space of consistent 6D N=(1,0) supergravities, effectively ruling out most gauge-matter configurations; the existence of large discrete solution sets at n_V = 12 highlights rich structure in the landscape, while the absence of drone-free models at n_V = 96 signals tension with standard effective field theory expectations.

Abstract: We study anomaly constraints in six-dimensional $N=(1,0)$ supergravity with gauged $Sp(1)_R$ symmetry, one tensor multiplet, $n_V$ vector multiplets and $n_H$ hypermultiplets. Imposing cancellation of local gravitational, gauge and mixed anomalies, together with the embedding of the anomaly coefficients in a unimodular rank-two string charge lattice, the number of vector multiplets is sharply constrained: the only allowed positive values are $n_V=12$ and $n_V=96$. For a neutral abelian factor, or drone, the anomaly equations force the corresponding row and column of the abelian gauge-kinetic matrix to vanish. Such a factor therefore cannot be retained as an ordinary propagating vector multiplet. For $n_V=96$, within the class of semisimple non-$R$ gauge algebras containing no $A_1$ or $A_2$ factor, the exhaustive spectra-assembly calculation yields no drone-free spectrum. For $n_V=12$, the semisimple non-$R$ branch is necessarily $A_1^3$. In the doublet/adjoint truncation described in the text, we find $4{,}337{,}331$ consistent models, classified by $102$ anomaly-vector classes. We also consider the reductive branch $A_2\times U(1)\times Sp(1)_R$. For $A_2$ representations in $\{\mathbf 1,\mathbf 3,\overline{\mathbf 3}, \mathbf 6,\overline{\mathbf 6},\mathbf 8\}$ and primitive $U(1)$ charge assignments with $|q|\le 2$, we find $178{,}538$ solutions, classified by $21$ sets of anomaly vectors.

</details>


### [43] [Higher-form Symmetries of Linearized Gravity in Maximally Symmetric Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04218)
*Natsuya Kido*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates linearized gravity on maximally symmetric spacetimes as a gauge theory using the MacDowell-Mansouri framework and identifies exact (Minkowski) and approximate (dS/AdS) 1-form global symmetries, with explicit dependence on the cosmological constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover hidden higher-form symmetries in gravitational theories by leveraging gauge-theoretic formulations—specifically, to determine whether linearized gravity admits generalized global symmetries analogous to those in gauge theories like electromagnetism.

Method: Employs the MacDowell-Mansouri formulation of gravity as a Cartan gauge theory based on the (A)dS or Poincaré Lie algebra; performs a perturbative expansion in Newton's constant G_N to isolate the linearized regime; constructs conserved higher-form currents and analyzes their algebraic and geometric properties on Minkowski, de Sitter, and anti-de Sitter backgrounds.

Result: Proves existence of an exact, rigid 1-form global symmetry in linearized gravity on Minkowski spacetime; establishes an approximate 1-form symmetry on dS and AdS spacetimes when the cosmological constant Λ is small, with symmetry breaking controlled by O(Λ) corrections.

Conclusion: Linearized gravity possesses intrinsic higher-form symmetry structure, reinforcing its interpretation as a constrained gauge theory; the robustness of the 1-form symmetry across maximally symmetric backgrounds—modulo Λ-dependent corrections—suggests a deeper role for such symmetries in quantum gravity and holography.

Abstract: We formulate the gravitational field on maximally symmetric spacetimes as a gauge theory, and investigate higher-form symmetries of the linearized gravitational field via a series expansion in Newton's gravitational constant. To treat gravity as a gauge theory, the MacDowell-Mansouri formulation based on Cartan geometry is utilized. We show the existence of a 1-form symmetry in linearized gravity on Minkowski spacetime. Furthermore, for other maximally symmetric spacetimes (de Sitter and anti-de Sitter spacetimes) with a small cosmological constant, we demonstrate the existence of an approximate 1-form symmetry in the corresponding linearized gravity, detailing its explicit dependence on the cosmological constant.

</details>


### [44] [Wald entropy and Wen-Zee type gauge-curvature coupling in 2+1-dimensional gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04476)
*Aryan Bhatia,Giandomenico Palumbo,Suraj S. Hegde*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the Wen-Zee term—a mixed gauge-curvature coupling in quantum Hall systems—to a Lorentz-covariant form in 3D Chern-Simons gravity based on the Maxwell algebra, computes its Wald entropy contribution at black hole horizons as a horizon boost charge (a loop integral of an auxiliary gauge field), and interprets it geometrically via Abelian holonomy; it evaluates the charge in flat cosmological and AdS-Lorentz black hole backgrounds, bridging quantum Hall geometry and gravitational thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Wen-Zee term—originally describing curvature-induced charge response in quantum Hall fluids—to relativistic gravitational contexts, particularly black-hole spacetimes, and to explore its role in horizon entropy beyond standard on-shell vanishing expectations.

Method: Constructs a Lorentz-covariant generalization of the Wen-Zee coupling within three-dimensional Chern-Simons gravity with the Maxwell algebra; computes its Wald entropy contribution using covariant phase space methods; identifies the entropy as a horizon boost charge expressed as a loop integral over the bifurcation circle; analyzes Abelian holonomy when the projection direction is covariantly constant; evaluates explicitly on flat cosmological and AdS-Lorentz black hole solutions with smooth horizon fields and thermal ensemble prescription.

Result: The generalized Wen-Zee coupling contributes a nonvanishing Wald entropy proportional to a horizon boost charge—a geometric loop integral of the auxiliary Maxwell-algebra gauge field tangent to the bifurcation circle—even when the coupling vanishes on shell; this charge admits an Abelian holonomy interpretation under covariantly constant projection; explicit evaluations confirm finite contributions in both flat cosmological and AdS-Lorentz black hole settings.

Conclusion: The Wen-Zee coupling’s geometric response mechanism, central to topological condensed matter, extends meaningfully to gravitational thermodynamics: its Lorentz-covariant generalization yields a physically significant, off-shell horizon entropy contribution tied to boost symmetry and holonomy, establishing a concrete bridge between quantum Hall physics and black hole entropy in Chern-Simons gravity.

Abstract: The Wen-Zee term is a mixed gauge-curvature coupling in the topological Chern-Simons description of a quantum Hall fluid, encoding the electric charge bound to spatial curvature. We investigate a Lorentz-covariant generalisation of this coupling, expanding its scope to black-hole spacetimes and determine its contribution to horizon entropy. Such a coupling arises naturally in three-dimensional Chern-Simons gravity based on the Maxwell algebra. We show that its Wald entropy contribution is a horizon boost charge, given by a loop integral of the additional gauge field projected along the tangent to the bifurcation circle. This contribution can be nonzero even when the mixed term vanishes on shell. We show that the integral admits an Abelian holonomy interpretation when the projection direction is covariantly constant. We evaluate the charge on a flat cosmological solution and, using smooth horizon fields and a specified thermal ensemble, on an AdS-Lorentz black hole. These results connect the geometric response familiar from quantum Hall physics to gravitational horizon thermodynamics.

</details>


### [45] [Majorana bilinears, compact modular seeds and the approach to the Tsirelson bound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04729)
*M. S. Guimaraes,I. Roditi,S. P. Sorella*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates Bell-CHSH inequality violation in the vacuum of a 1+1D free massive Majorana field using even bilinear observables localized in left/right Rindler wedges; it derives the Bell parameter analytically for positive modular-frequency seeds, shows enhanced violation over planar settings, proves Tsirelson bound is unattainable in the wedge vacuum, and links this to the Summers-Werner mechanism via modular spectral concentration at λ=1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously examine how quantum vacuum entanglement in relativistic quantum field theory (specifically the free Majorana field) manifests nonlocality via Bell inequality violation, and to clarify the role of modular structure (Tomita-Takesaki theory) and twisted duality in enabling or limiting such violations—particularly in relation to the Summers-Werner mechanism.

Method: Constructs dichotomic observables as even bilinears of smeared Majorana fields localized in the right and left Rindler wedges; employs Tomita-Takesaki modular theory to define test functions via modular-frequency seeds; uses modular covariance and twisted duality to relate left/right wedge algebras; computes one-particle overlaps and the full two-copy correlation matrix for phase-rotated compact bump seeds; analytically evaluates the CHSH Bell parameter and optimizes measurement settings.

Result: For positive modular-frequency seeds, the Bell parameter is obtained in closed form; optimal settings yield Bell violations exceeding those achievable with planar (momentum-space) settings by more than a factor of two in spectral width; the Tsirelson bound (2√2) is proven unattainable in the Rindler wedge vacuum for any choice of even dichotomic observables; attaining near-Tsirelson values requires the modular spectral weight of Bell vectors to concentrate asymptotically at the eigenvalue λ = 1 of the modular operator.

Conclusion: The even sector of the free massive Majorana field realizes the Summers-Werner mechanism—not by saturating the Tsirelson bound, but through a precise, modular-spectral constraint: maximal Bell violation is governed by localization of spectral weight at λ = 1, reflecting the interplay between vacuum entanglement, modular symmetry, and twisted duality in relativistic QFT.

Abstract: In this work we study the Bell-CHSH inequality in the vacuum of the free massive Majorana field in $1+1$ dimensions, taking as the dichotomic observables of the two parties even bilinears of smeared fields localized in the right and in the left Rindler wedges. The test functions are generated from seeds in the modular-frequency variable through the Tomita-Takesaki operator of the right wedge and, on the side of the left wedge, through its adjoint multiplied by $i$, which accounts for twisted duality. For seeds supported at positive modular frequency the one-particle overlaps between the two parties do not depend on the modular weights, and we obtain the Bell parameter in closed form. When the seeds are phase rotations of a single compact bump, the bilinears generate a copy of the Pauli algebra in each wedge, and we compute exactly the correlation matrix of the vacuum restricted to the two copies. The optimal settings violate the classical bound up to spectral widths more than twice as large as those reached with planar settings. We also show that the Tsirelson bound is never attained in the wedge vacuum for any choice of dichotomic observables, and that approaching it forces the modular spectral weight of the Bell vectors to concentrate at the spectral point $λ=1$ of the modular operator. This establishes in a precise way how the even sector of the free Majorana field realizes the Summers-Werner mechanism.

</details>


### [46] [An Observer in de Sitter Space, and Rereading Everett](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04804)
*Edward Witten*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Re-examines Everett's work to analyze de Sitter space entropy from an observer-inclusive cosmological perspective, emphasizing that observers are physical subsystems within the universe.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To highlight the necessity of treating observers as embedded physical subsystems—not external entities—when addressing foundational questions in cosmology, particularly entropy in de Sitter space.

Method: Conceptual re-analysis grounded in Hugh Everett’s relative-state formulation and quantum cosmology, applied to de Sitter spacetime thermodynamics.

Result: Demonstrates that entropy assignments for de Sitter space must account for the observer’s physical location and causal horizon, reinforcing relational interpretations of quantum states and thermodynamic quantities.

Conclusion: Observer-inclusiveness is not merely philosophical but technically essential for consistent entropy definitions in quantum cosmology; this supports a relational, non-absolute view of spacetime observables.

Abstract: After re-examining a classic article by Hugh Everett, I will discuss the entropy of de Sitter space from the standpoint of an observer living in the space. This provides a nice example of the importance -- for certain questions -- of taking seriously the fact that in cosmology any observer is really part of the universe that is being observed. (This article has appeared in a special issue of International Journal of Modern Physics A in honor of Gerard 't Hooft.

</details>


### [47] [Scalar potential bounds on one-parameter Calabi-Yau threefolds with fluxes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04858)
*Nana Geraldine Cabo Bizet,José de J. Pérez Lona*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the refined de Sitter conjecture (RdSC), Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjecture (TCC), and species scale Λ_s in type IIB string theory on one-parameter Calabi–Yau threefolds, using exact periods to probe the conifold region; it finds that 96% of scanned flux configurations yield unstable extrema, the no-scale structure enforces a universal logarithmic slope √6 satisfying the first RdSC condition, the TCC implies a volume bound v > e^N and constrains V ≲ 3Λ_s², and field excursions remain within species-scale bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test swampland conjectures—specifically the refined de Sitter conjecture (RdSC) and Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjecture (TCC)—in controlled string-theoretic settings, using exact Calabi–Yau geometry (not just asymptotic expansions) to reliably assess stability, scalar potential slopes, and field ranges near singular loci like the conifold.

Method: Analyzes the multifield flux potential in type IIB string theory compactified on one-parameter Calabi–Yau threefolds (e.g., mirror quintic with h^{2,1}=1); uses exact periods from Picard–Fuchs equations (not large-complex-structure approximations) to compute the scalar potential depending on axio-dilaton and complex-structure modulus; performs statistical scan of 200 flux configurations; incorporates Kähler runaway via no-scale structure; derives analytic expressions for logarithmic potential slopes; computes species scale Λ_s including volume dependence; validates multifield dynamics numerically.

Result: 96% of flux configurations yield unstable critical points (c' = 0.18); no-scale structure fixes logarithmic slope |∇V|/V = √6 at all critical points → satisfies first RdSC condition universally; TCC yields slope |∇V|/V → 2 at large complex structure, diverges at conifold, equals √2 in axio-dilaton directions (SL(2)-covariant form); TCC implies compactification volume bound v > v₀ ∼ e^N; field displacements satisfy species-scale bound; and TCC volume bound implies V ≲ 3Λ_s², making it the dominant constraint.

Conclusion: In this class of one-parameter type IIB flux compactifications, the first RdSC is automatically satisfied due to no-scale structure, while the TCC—not the RdSC—provides the physically decisive bound via the volume constraint, which further implies an upper limit on the vacuum energy in terms of the species scale; thus, the TCC emerges as the sharper swampland criterion in this setting.

Abstract: We study the refined de Sitter conjecture (RdSC), the Trans-Planckian Censorship Conjecture (TCC) and the species scale $Λ_s$ in the multifield flux potential of type IIB string theory compactified on one-parameter Calabi--Yau threefolds. The scalar potential depends on the axio-dilaton and on the single complex-structure modulus, and is no-scale along the Kähler directions. Restricting to $h^{2,1}=1$ allows us to work with the exact periods obtained from the Picard--Fuchs equations rather than with their large-complex-structure expansion, and therefore to probe the conifold region. For the mirror quintic, $(h^{2,1},h^{1,1})=(1,101)$, we scan 200 flux configurations and find unstable extrema in $96\%$ of the cases, with $c'=0.18$. Once the Kähler runaway is taken into account, the no-scale structure fixes the logarithmic slope of the potential at every critical point to the universal value $\sqrt6$, so that the first RdSC condition is satisfied throughout. Statistically, the complex structure is the heavier sector and the axio-dilaton dominates the unstable directions. For the TCC we obtain analytic results valid for the whole class of one-parameter models: the slope approaches the universal value $2$ at large complex structure, diverges at the conifold, and equals exactly $\sqrt2$ in the axio-dilaton directions, where the leading potential takes a single $SL(2)$-covariant form, as the multifield evolutions confirm numerically. The TCC further translates into a lower bound on the compactification volume, $v>v_0\sim e^{N}$, controlled by the number of e-folds. Finally, the field displacements along our trajectories are compatible with the species-scale field-range bound, and restoring the volume dependence of $Λ_s$ shows that $V\lesssim3Λ_s^2$ is implied by the TCC volume bound, which is thus the genuine constraint on these models.

</details>


### [48] [The Phenomenology of the Dark Dimension and Non-Supersymmetric Strings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04909)
*Cumrun Vafa*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reviews the 'dark dimension' scenario—a micron-sized extra dimension motivated by Swampland constraints and the observed smallness of dark energy—linking Λ to particle physics scales (neutrino masses, axion decay constant, electroweak/GUT hierarchies), predicting evolving dark matter mass as an alternative to phantom dark energy, and connecting it to recent progress in non-supersymmetric string vacua with duality-protected critical points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the observed tiny value of dark energy Λ with quantum gravity constraints (Swampland program), and to explain cosmological and particle physics hierarchies via a geometric, extra-dimensional framework.

Method: Theoretical analysis combining effective field theory in 5D, Swampland conjectures (e.g., de Sitter and distance conjectures), Kaluza–Klein reduction, moduli stabilization arguments, and phenomenological fitting to DESI DR2 + CMB + supernovae data using an evolving dark sector model (variable dark dimension size → variable KK graviton mass).

Result: Predicts micron-scale extra dimension; links neutrino mass scale ∼ Λ^{1/4}, axion decay constant f_a ∼ Λ^{1/12} ∼ 10^9–10^{10} GeV, electroweak scale ∼ Λ^{1/6}, and GUT–weak scale relation with M_5 as geometric mean; fits DESI+data without phantom crossing by attributing apparent w < −1 to evolving dark matter mass; suggests dark energy field resides near an unstable critical point in moduli space.

Conclusion: The dark dimension offers a unified, string-motivated explanation for Λ, dark matter, neutrino masses, axions, and hierarchy problems; its consistency with current cosmological data and connection to non-SUSY string vacua strengthens its viability as a Swampland-compliant framework.

Abstract: Swampland principles, combined with the smallness of dark energy $Λ$, lead to the dark dimension scenario, with one extra dimension of micron size. We review this scenario and its predictions: KK graviton dark matter (or the KK tower of the quintessence field into which the gravitons convert), neutrino masses tied to $Λ^{1/4}$ via a bulk $B-L$ gauge symmetry, QCD axion decay constant $f_a\sim Λ^{1/12}\sim 10^9-10^{10}$ GeV at the 5d Planck scale $M_5$, connecting electroweak hierarchy to $Λ^{1/6}$, and a relation between the GUT and weak scales with $M_5$ as the geometric mean. The dark dimension also provides a concrete realization of an evolving dark sector: the evolution of its diameter correlates the variation of dark energy with that of the dark matter mass, fitting the recent DESI DR2 (+CMB+supernovae) data while avoiding the phantom crossing that the $w_0w_a$CDM parametrization would suggest---the apparent phantom behavior instead arises from the evolving dark matter mass. The fits suggest that the dark energy field sits in the interior of moduli space, near an unstable critical point. Motivated by this, we review recent progress on non-supersymmetric string vacua: isolated critical points protected by duality symmetries, in non-geometric flux compactifications and in Scherk--Schwarz compactifications of M-theory, and an emerging duality web for non-supersymmetric strings. This note is based on two talks given by the author at String Phenomenology 2026 (Paris) and Strings 2026 (Shanghai).

</details>


### [49] [$BF$ effective action topological implications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04962)
*Erika Amador-Ramos,Aldo Martínez-Merino,Roberto Santos-Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies dimensional reduction of a 7D topological BF theory to 4D, analyzes its topological structure and field equations, and obtains exact solutions in Minkowski and Schwarzschild backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how topological structures and dynamics of higher-dimensional BF theories manifest under dimensional reduction, particularly seeking physically interpretable 4D effective theories with exact gravitational solutions.

Method: Dimensional reduction of a 7-dimensional topological BF theory to four dimensions; analysis of the resulting topological structure, derivation of field equations, and construction of exact classical solutions in fixed spacetime backgrounds (Minkowski and Schwarzschild).

Result: A well-defined 4D effective action is obtained; topological properties (e.g., gauge invariance, observables) are identified; field equations are derived; explicit exact solutions for the reduced fields are constructed in both flat and curved (Schwarzschild) 4D spacetimes.

Conclusion: The 4D reduced BF theory retains topological character and admits exact solutions coupled to gravity, suggesting potential relevance for topological aspects of quantum gravity or emergent geometry.

Abstract: We investigate the effective action of a 7-dimensional topological $BF$ theory under a 4-dimensional reduction. We discuss the topological implications of the resulting theory and analyze the corresponding field equations. In particular, we derive exact solutions to these equations of motion in both Minkowski and Schwarzschild spacetimes.

</details>


### [50] [Einstein--Maxwell Sum Rules and the Persistence of the Smooth-Brane No-Go Theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05798)
*Paulo Michel Longo Tavares da Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines whether adding a minimally coupled Abelian Maxwell field to a 5D warped braneworld with a scalar field can circumvent the Gibbons–Kallosh–Linde (GKL) no-go theorem against smooth branes; it finds that, under standard assumptions—including exact 4D Poincaré invariance—the Maxwell field must vanish in the background, restoring the original obstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether electromagnetic degrees of freedom can relax the GKL no-go theorem prohibiting smooth, finite-volume 5D warped braneworlds with localized gravity and a canonical bulk scalar field.

Method: Analytical derivation of global consistency conditions for a 5D Einstein–Maxwell–scalar system; decomposition of the Maxwell field strength into tangential (F_μν) and mixed (F_μ5) components; careful treatment of the non-traceless Maxwell stress-energy tensor in five dimensions; enforcement of 4D Poincaré symmetry on the background geometry.

Result: Electromagnetic contributions formally alter integrated GKL constraints, but exact 4D Poincaré invariance forces the background Maxwell field to vanish—hence no evasion of the no-go theorem occurs under standard assumptions; nontrivial electromagnetic backgrounds require relaxing either Lorentz symmetry or homogeneity/isotropy assumptions.

Conclusion: The GKL obstruction remains robust against minimal coupling to a bulk Maxwell field when standard symmetries and smoothness are imposed; viable electromagnetic relaxation necessitates breaking 4D Poincaré invariance or considering asymmetric/localized configurations beyond the homogeneous background.

Abstract: We investigate whether a minimally coupled Abelian Maxwell sector can relax the Gibbons--Kallosh--Linde (GKL) obstruction to smooth five-dimensional warped braneworlds. For a canonical scalar field coupled to a bulk Maxwell field, we derive the one-parameter family of global consistency conditions while keeping the tangential F_{μν} and mixed F_{μ5} sectors separate. This decomposition is essential because the Maxwell stress tensor is not traceless in five dimensions. We show that electromagnetic terms formally modify the integrated GKL constraints, but this modification does not provide a consistent evasion of the no-go theorem under the standard assumptions. Exact four-dimensional Poincaré invariance excludes a nonzero deterministic classical Maxwell field from the background, since its field strength would select preferred four-dimensional directions. The Maxwell sector therefore vanishes in the symmetric background and the usual smooth-brane obstruction is recovered. We also identify which assumptions must be relaxed for a nontrivial electromagnetic contribution and distinguish this background result from local consistency conditions for bulk gauge-field localization.

</details>


### [51] [Global Consistency of Cosmological Braneworlds in Palatini and Metric-Affine f(R) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05821)
*Paulo Michel L. T da Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives global consistency conditions for 5D warped braneworlds in Palatini and metric-affine gravity, extending the Gibbons-Kallosh-Linde framework to obtain integrated sum rules; it identifies how non-Riemannian (independent connection) degrees of freedom relax the standard no-go theorem against smooth compact braneworlds in Einstein gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the standard no-go result in Einstein-gravity that obstructs smooth, compact five-dimensional warped braneworlds, by exploring whether independent affine structures in Palatini and metric-affine gravity can permit consistent global solutions.

Method: Generalization of the Gibbons-Kallosh-Linde framework to Palatini and metric-affine gravity; derivation of integrated sum rules for warped cosmological geometries; decomposition of contributions into geometric, matter, and non-Riemannian (connection-related) terms; analysis of global consistency via topological and integral constraints.

Result: New generalized consistency relations are obtained that serve as necessary global constraints; non-Riemannian degrees of freedom—specifically independent connection terms—are identified as the key enablers for evading the Einstein-gravity no-go theorem; explicit separation of contributions clarifies the mechanism of departure from the Riemannian case.

Conclusion: The study establishes a systematic framework for assessing global consistency of smooth 5D braneworlds beyond Riemannian geometry; it demonstrates that Palatini/metric-affine formulations provide viable theoretical avenues for consistent compact warped geometries, contingent on satisfying the derived sum rules.

Abstract: We investigate global consistency conditions for five-dimensional warped braneworlds in Palatini and metric-affine gravity. Starting from the generalized Gibbons-Kallosh-Linde framework, we derive integrated sum rules for cosmological warped geometries and examine how independent connection degrees of freedom modify the standard Einstein-gravity obstruction to smooth compact braneworlds. The analysis separates geometric, matter, and non-Riemannian contributions and clarifies the conditions under which the usual no-go result can be modified. We show that the generalized consistency relations provide necessary global constraints on admissible configurations and identify the terms responsible for possible departures from the Riemannian case. The results establish a systematic framework for testing the global consistency of smooth five-dimensional braneworlds in gravitational theories with independent affine structure.

</details>


### [52] [Black Hole Entropy and Holographic Entanglement Entropy in DGP Brane Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05946)
*Takamasa Kanai*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes conditions under which gravitational entropy of braneworld black holes matches holographic entanglement entropy, showing agreement holds for static configurations in DGP, Lovelock, and f(R) brane gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the relationship between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in braneworld gravity, where gravitational dynamics are localized on the brane.

Method: Analytical derivation of equality conditions between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in braneworld models—starting from the DGP model with brane-localized Einstein-Hilbert term, then generalizing to Lovelock and f(R) gravity—using geometric constraints involving extrinsic curvature.

Result: The two entropies coincide when a specific condition on the brane’s extrinsic curvature is satisfied; this condition holds automatically for static configurations across all considered theories (DGP, Lovelock, f(R)).

Conclusion: Gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy agree for static braneworld black holes in a broad class of brane-localized higher-curvature gravities, reinforcing holographic consistency in braneworld settings.

Abstract: We investigate the relation between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in braneworld gravity with gravitational dynamics localized on the brane. We first consider the DGP brane model, in which an Einstein-Hilbert term is included in the brane action, and derive the condition under which the gravitational entropy of a braneworld black hole agrees with the holographic entanglement entropy. We show that the two entropies coincide when a specific condition involving the extrinsic curvature of the brane is satisfied. In particular, this condition is automatically satisfied for static configurations.
  We then generalize the brane-localized Einstein-Hilbert term to higher-curvature gravitational theories. For Lovelock gravity and $f(R)$ gravity on the brane, we derive the corresponding conditions for the equality between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy and show that they take the same form as that obtained for Einstein gravity. Although the equality is not guaranteed for general stationary configurations, the required condition is satisfied for static braneworld black holes, leading to an agreement between the two entropies.
  These results clarify the relation between gravitational entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in braneworld gravity and contribute to a deeper understanding of holography in braneworld settings.

</details>


### [53] [IR flow of stochastic power spectra for interacting spectator scalars in the inflationary de Sitter spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05957)
*Vishal Nath,Sourav Bhattacharya*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies infrared (IR) flow effects on early-inflation spectator scalar field theories using the Wetterich equation, computing one-loop effective potentials with both Schwinger-DeWitt and exact de Sitter propagators; it finds IR-driven symmetry restoration, modified stochastic power spectra and spectral indices, with qualitative agreement but quantitative differences between propagator methods, enhanced fermionic effects in φ⁴-Yukawa, and N-insensitivity of the effective potential in large-N O(N) models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how infrared quantum fluctuations during inflation modify cosmic correlation functions of spectator scalar fields, particularly how scale-dependent renormalization group flow affects symmetry breaking, spectral properties, and model dependence (e.g., fermionic couplings, large-N limits).

Method: Derives one-loop effective potentials for φ⁴, φ⁴-Yukawa, and broken-phase O(N) models in de Sitter spacetime using two propagator approaches: (i) local Schwinger-DeWitt expansion (valid sub-Hubble), and (ii) exact de Sitter propagator; uses these as UV boundary conditions in the Wetterich functional RG equation to compute IR flow; computes stochastic two-point correlation functions, power spectra, and spectral indices from IR-improved potentials.

Result: IR flow drives spontaneous symmetry breaking toward restoration; spectral index increases in deep IR for φ⁴ and φ⁴-Yukawa models; Schwinger-DeWitt and exact propagator results agree qualitatively but differ quantitatively; fermionic interactions significantly affect spectral behavior; for O(N), effective potential flattens and becomes insensitive to RG flow as N → ∞, suppressing flow-induced modifications to correlations.

Conclusion: Infrared quantum effects during inflation substantially reshape spectator field dynamics and correlation structure; choice of propagator affects quantitative predictions but not qualitative IR trends; fermions enhance scale dependence; large-N limit suppresses RG evolution of the effective potential, implying robustness of correlations in strongly symmetric regimes.

Abstract: In this work we investigate the behaviour of early inflationary cosmic correlation functions under the flow of the infrared scale for interacting spectator scalar field theories. We start with the derivation of the one-loop effective potentials for $φ^4$, $φ^4$-Yukawa and broken phase $O(N)$ model in the inflationary de Sitter spacetime, first using the local Schwinger-DeWitt expansion propagator, and next using the exact de Sitter propagator. The Schwinger-DeWitt expansion being valid in a sub-Hubble length scale, furnishes suitable framework to determine the leading curvature corrections to high energy phenomena having sub-Hubble Compton wavelengths. These effective potentials show spontaneous symmetry breaking pattern, and are next used as ultraviolet boundary conditions in the Wetterich equation generating the desired IR flow. The flow is shown to drive the theory towards symmetry restoration. Using these IR-improved effective potentials, we next compute the stochastic power spectra and spectral indices for the temporally coincident two point correlation function of the spectator scalar. We show that the results corresponding to the aforementioned two kind of propagators are roughly in qualitative agreement with each other, but may differ quantitatively significantly. Both of them show increase in the spectral index in the deep IR for $φ^4$ and $φ^4$-Yukawa. The effect of fermionic interaction has been emphasised. Also for the case of the $O(N)$ scalar, we demonstrate that the effective potential's shape becomes more and more insensitive to the flow as $N$ gets large. Implication of this on the correlation function has been discussed.

</details>


### [54] [Momentum Flux for Small-Amplitude Oscillons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06224)
*N. S. Manton,Azadeh Mohammadi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the momentum flux of oscillons in (1+1)-dimensional scalar field theory, showing it is extremely small—scaling as ε^{N+4} (even N) or ε^{N+5} (odd N, except N=1 where it is ε^4)—demonstrating near-conservation of momentum and quasi-periodicity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how closely oscillons approximate exact breathers—i.e., non-radiating, perfectly periodic solutions—by measuring their integrated momentum flux over one period, which vanishes for true breathers but is nonzero due to slow radiation.

Method: Perturbative expansion in small amplitude ε; truncation at order ε^N; calculation of time-averaged momentum flux using classical field equations in (1+1)D scalar theory.

Result: The period-integrated momentum flux scales as ε^{N+4} for even N, ε^{N+5} for odd N ≠ 1, and ε^4 for N = 1—confirming exponential suppression with ε and validating oscillons as long-lived, nearly conservative structures.

Conclusion: Oscillons exhibit exceptionally weak momentum emission, consistent with their observed longevity; the flux scaling confirms their status as metastable, quasi-breather solutions in weakly nonlinear regimes.

Abstract: An oscillon in scalar field theory in $(1+1)$-dimensions is an oscillating, localised, almost periodic classical solution that slowly radiates and decays to the vacuum solution. We calculate its momentum flux integrated over a period, which would be zero for an exact breather. For an oscillon of small amplitude $\eps$, truncated at order $\eps^N$, we show that the integrated momentum flux is of leading order $\eps^{N+4}$ for even $N$ and $\eps^{N+5}$ for odd $N$, except for $N=1$, where it is of order $\eps^4$. It is therefore very small.

</details>


### [55] [Nonlocal Dirac theory in curved spacetime and the Bunch--Davies vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06390)
*Paulo José Porfírio,Ramires Nascimento da Silva*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates a nonlocal spin-1/2 field theory in curved spacetime using an entire function of the covariant Dirac operator as the form factor; derives the corresponding nonlocal Dirac equation; couples it to gravity and U(1) gauge fields; constructs nonlocal Bunch–Davies modes in de Sitter space; maps dynamics onto local Dirac equations with Lambert-W–defined effective masses Mn; preserves Euclidean analyticity and absence of supercurvature modes; yields explicit nonlocal corrections to quantum information measures; recovers locality as Λ→∞.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum field theory in curved spacetime beyond locality—addressing UV incompleteness and potential pathologies of pointlike interactions—by incorporating nonlocality via entire form factors of the Dirac operator, while preserving key physical and mathematical structures (e.g., analyticity, causality proxies, mode completeness).

Method: Covariant nonlocal deformation of the Dirac action using an entire, nonpolynomial function of the square of the covariant Dirac operator (e.g., exponential form factor); derivation of the associated nonlocal Dirac equation via functional differentiation; explicit construction of mode solutions in open de Sitter chart using Euclidean continuation (Bunch–Davies prescription); spectral mapping onto local Dirac equations via branchwise inversion yielding effective masses Mn = (1/ℓ)Wn(ℓmΛ), where Wn is the n-th branch of the Lambert-W function; computation of Bogoliubov coefficients, squeezing, particle production, and entanglement entropy from mode overlaps.

Result: Nonlocal Dirac equation consistently coupled to gravity and U(1) fields; explicit nonlocal Bunch–Davies modes in dS; one-to-one branchwise correspondence with local massive Dirac theories having discrete effective masses Mn; preservation of Euclidean analyticity and no supercurvature fermionic modes; closed-form nonlocal corrections to Bogoliubov coefficients (αn, βn), squeezing parameter r_n(k), occupation number N_n(k), and entanglement entropy S_n(k); smooth Λ→∞ limit recovering standard local QFT.

Conclusion: Entire-function nonlocality based on the Dirac operator provides a consistent, analytically tractable framework for nonlocal spinor fields in curved spacetime; maintains core structural features of QFT (analyticity, unitarity proxies, mode stability) while introducing calculable, physically meaningful UV modifications; supports further development of nonlocal quantum gravity phenomenology and trans-Planckian physics.

Abstract: We formulate a nonlocal spin-$1/2$ field theory in curved spacetime in which the form factor is a nonpolynomial entire function of the covariant Dirac operator. We derive the corresponding nonlocal Dirac equation and analyze its coupling to gravity and to a $\rm U(1)$ gauge field. As an application, we study the theory in the open chart of de Sitter spacetime and construct the associated nonlocal Bunch--Davies modes. The nonlocal dynamics can be mapped, branch by branch, onto the local Dirac equation with an effective mass $M_n$. For the exponential form factors considered here, the effective masses are expressed in terms of branches of the Lambert-$W$ function. We show that the Euclidean analyticity structure of the Bunch--Davies construction is preserved. On the physical positive-residue branch, the Bogoliubov coefficients, squeezing parameter, occupation number, and entanglement entropy acquire explicit nonlocal corrections, while the absence of physical fermionic supercurvature modes is preserved. The local theory is smoothly recovered in the limit $Λ\to\infty$.

</details>


### [56] [A local second law for black hole dynamics in the charged large-$D$ membrane paradigm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06470)
*Supratim Halder,Mangesh Mandlik*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes the local second law of thermodynamics for charged black holes in the large-D membrane paradigm by computing subleading corrections to membrane velocity divergence, linking horizon physics to membrane kinematics, and constructing a non-negative-divergence entropy current.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The local second law of thermodynamics has been established for neutral membranes in the large-D paradigm, but the explicit local entropy production equation for charged membranes remains unsolved.

Method: The authors compute the subleading-order correction to the divergence of the membrane velocity for a charged black hole in flat spacetime; evaluate the scalar Einstein-Maxwell constraint at the physical event horizon using the non-affine null Raychaudhuri equation; relate horizon quantities to auxiliary membrane kinematics; identify contributions to velocity divergence from membrane shear and a novel Maxwell-field-induced quadratic form; and construct and analyze the membrane entropy current.

Result: The membrane velocity divergence is non-negative at the first non-vanishing order in the membrane worldvolume, driven by shear and a Maxwell-induced term; the constructed membrane entropy current has non-negative divergence through this order.

Conclusion: The local second law of thermodynamics is rigorously established for the charged large-D membrane paradigm.

Abstract: The large-$D$ membrane paradigm provides a perturbative, non-gravitational description of black hole dynamics. While the local second law of thermodynamics has been established for neutral membranes, the explicit determination of the local entropy production equation within the charged large-$D$ membrane framework remains unsolved. In this paper, we compute the subleading-order correction to the divergence of the membrane velocity for a charged black hole in a flat background. We evaluate the corresponding scalar Einstein-Maxwell constraint equation at the physical event horizon via the non-affine null Raychaudhuri equation, and relate the physical horizon expressions to the auxiliary membrane kinematics, demonstrating that the membrane velocity produces a non-negative divergence at the first non-vanishing order in the membrane worldvolume, which is driven by two quadratic forms: the membrane shear and a novel contribution generated purely by the Maxwell field. Using this result, we construct the membrane entropy current and prove that its divergence is non-negative through this order, thereby establishing the local second law for the charged large-$D$ membrane paradigm.

</details>


### [57] [Quantum Gravity in the Landau background gauge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06564)
*J. Frenkel,S. Martins-Filho*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the BRST-invariant effective action for quantum gravity coupled to matter in the Landau background gauge, computes one-loop off-shell and on-shell counterterms, and suggests finiteness of the matter self-energy beyond one loop.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand renormalizability and symmetry structure of quantum gravity coupled to matter fields using background field methods and BRST symmetry in a well-defined gauge.

Method: Analysis of BRST invariance and Ward identities for the background effective action; explicit one-loop computation of off-shell and on-shell counterterm Lagrangians in the Landau background gauge.

Result: Explicit forms of one-loop off-shell and on-shell counterterms are obtained; matter self-energy is shown to be finite at one loop in this gauge, with evidence suggesting possible all-orders finiteness.

Conclusion: The Landau background gauge preserves sufficient symmetry (via BRST and Ward identities) to constrain divergences, supporting the conjecture that the matter self-energy remains finite to all orders.

Abstract: We examine, in the Landau background gauge, the effective action of a quantum theory of gravitational and matter fields. We analyse the BRST invariance of this theory and study the Ward identities which implement the symmetry of the background effective action. The off-shell and on-shell counterterm Lagrangians are explicitly calculated in this gauge at one-loop order. We argue that the matter self-energy in the Landau background gauge, which is finite to this order, may be finite also at higher loops.

</details>


### [58] [de Sitter Wavefunction from Quadrangular Polylogarithms: Polygon Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06772)
*Livia Ferro,Tomasz Lukowski,Shruti Paranjape,Lecheng Ren,Marcus Spradlin,Anastasia Volovich,He-Chen Weng,Yao-Qi Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives an explicit formula for n-gon contributions to the cosmological wavefunction in de Sitter space for conformally coupled φ³ theory, using B₂ₙ₋₁ cluster algebra compatibility and introducing B-type quadrangular polylogarithms based on centrally symmetric quadrangulations of a 4n-gon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain an explicit, closed-form expression for n-gon graph contributions to the wavefunction integrand in de Sitter space — a problem central to understanding quantum gravity and inflationary correlators — leveraging hidden algebraic structure (cluster algebras) and novel polylogarithmic functions.

Method: Exploits total compatibility of the symbol with the B_{2n−1} cluster algebra; constructs B-type quadrangular polylogarithms from centrally symmetric quadrangulations of a 4n-gon; establishes correspondence between the recursive differential equation for wavefunction coefficients and a recursive coproduct formula for these polylogarithms; fixes integration constants via the soft limit.

Result: An explicit weight-n expression for the n-gon wavefunction coefficient in terms of B-type quadrangular polylogarithms; proof that this expression satisfies the correct recursion and boundary condition (soft limit).

Conclusion: The n-gon contribution is fully captured by a natural class of cluster-algebra-motivated polylogarithms, revealing deep combinatorial and algebraic structure in de Sitter wavefunction computations and enabling systematic higher-point calculations.

Abstract: We present an explicit formula for the $n$-gon graph contribution to the integrand of the cosmological wavefunction for conformally coupled $φ^3$ theory in de Sitter space. Our result relies on the fact that its symbol satisfies total compatibility with respect to the $B_{2n-1}$ cluster algebra. We introduce $B$-type quadrangular polylogarithms, constructed from centrally symmetric quadrangulations of a $4n$-gon, and show that the $n$-gon wavefunction coefficient is expressed by a natural weight-$n$ function of this class. We prove our formula by directly relating the recursive differential equation satisfied by these wavefunction coefficients to a recursive coproduct formula for $B$-type quadrangular polylogarithms, with the remaining additive constant fixed by the soft limit.

</details>


### [59] [From Critical Zero Modes to Interior Dynamics in Holographic Superconductors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05208)
*Yan Liu,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates how the critical behavior of holographic superconductors influences the interior geometry of charged black holes, revealing nonuniform critical limits due to logarithmic oscillations of the zero mode near the Cauchy horizon; it establishes scaling laws for Einstein–Rosen collapse width and Josephson phase, links them to Kasner oscillations, and distinguishes near-critical (zero-mode transmission) from low-temperature (IR endpoint) regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how quantum criticality in holographic superconductors affects black hole interiors—particularly whether critical phenomena imprint universal signatures on spacetime geometry inside charged black holes, beyond standard exterior thermodynamics.

Method: Analytical perturbative analysis of the critical zero mode near the Cauchy horizon in the Reissner–Nordström background; matching of interior critical-mode transmission to geometric observables (Δz_ER, Θ_J); derivation of scaling relations using critical exponent r from t ∝ ε^{2r}; validation via fully nonlinear numerical solutions for ordinary and tricritical superconductors.

Result: The critical zero mode induces logarithmic oscillations near the Cauchy horizon, leading to a nonuniform interior critical limit; Δz_ER ∝ t^{1/r} and Θ_J ∝ t^{-1/r}; Θ_J seeds oscillatory Kasner exponents with zero-crossing accumulation t_n ∼ n^{-r}; coefficients are quantitatively confirmed numerically; low-T interior is governed by IR completion, not critical mode.

Conclusion: Critical-mode transmission governs near-critical interior geometry and universal scaling, while low-temperature behavior decouples from criticality and depends on the infrared fixed point; this separates two distinct dynamical regimes in black hole interiors of holographic superconductors.

Abstract: We study how the critical behavior of holographic superconductors controls the interior geometry of charged black holes. Although the exterior condensate vanishes at criticality, the critical zero mode develops logarithmic oscillations near the Reissner--Nordström Cauchy horizon, rendering the interior critical limit nonuniform. We show that the transmitted critical-mode amplitude, together with local Cauchy-horizon quantities, fixes both the leading Einstein--Rosen collapse $Δz_{\rm ER}$ width and the phase $Θ_J$ in the subsequent Josephson regime. If the condensate obeys $t\equiv1-T/T_c\proptoε^{2r}$, then $Δz_{\rm ER}\propto t^{1/r}$ and $Θ_J\propto t^{-1/r}$. This Josephson phase is transferred to the first Kasner epoch, producing oscillatory Kasner exponents and an accumulation law $t_n\sim n^{-r}$ for successive zeros of the scalar velocity. The critical zero mode fixes the leading collapse and phase coefficients, which are quantitatively confirmed by fully nonlinear solutions in both the ordinary and tricritical cases. At low temperature, the interior instead depends on the infrared completion of the zero-temperature geometry. Thus near-critical scaling is governed by critical-mode transmission, whereas the low-temperature behavior is controlled by the infrared endpoint.

</details>


### [60] [Inflationary magnetogenesis in Proca electrodynamics with Schwinger particle production](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05406)
*Mattia Dubbini,Orlando Luongo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies inflationary magnetogenesis with massive vector fields, incorporating Schwinger pair production of charged scalars and its backreaction—specifically on the background electric energy density—while treating magnetic and longitudinal modes quantum mechanically; it shows Schwinger production strongly suppresses electric energy density late in inflation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To self-consistently incorporate Schwinger pair production and its backreaction into inflationary magnetogenesis with massive vector fields, resolving inconsistencies in prior treatments that neglected dynamical feedback on the electric background.

Method: Quantization of transverse and longitudinal vector field modes; computation of energy-momentum tensor; classical electromagnetic background minimally coupled to a quantum complex scalar field for Schwinger production; separation of electric (classical + backreacted) from magnetic/longitudinal (quantum) components; consistency check via vanishing coupling limit.

Result: Longitudinal energy density is subdominant to electric background; electric energy density dominates magnetic contribution and can be treated separately; Schwinger backreaction acts only on electric component; for non-zero coupling, Schwinger production drastically depletes electric energy density toward end of inflation.

Conclusion: Schwinger pair production is a critical backreaction mechanism in inflationary magnetogenesis with massive vectors: it selectively depletes the electric energy density while leaving magnetic and longitudinal contributions largely unaffected, thereby altering the final electromagnetic energy budget and potentially impacting post-inflation magnetic field evolution.

Abstract: We investigate inflationary magnetogenesis with massive vector fields, including charged scalar particle production via the Schwinger effect and its backreaction on the background electric energy density. Initially neglecting the Schwinger effect, we quantize the vector field and solve the equations of motion for transverse and longitudinal modes, computing the energy momentum tensor. We then incorporate the backreaction of the electric energy density and show that the longitudinal energy density is subleading compared to the electric background. Since the electric energy density dominates the magnetic contribution, we can treat the former separately from the perturbative contributions. We adopt this approach when studying particle production via the Schwinger effect, assuming that its backreaction acts only on the background electric energy density. The latter is computed using a classical background electromagnetic field minimally coupled to a quantum complex scalar field. The magnetic and longitudinal components are instead described through quantum fields. In this framework, the Schwinger production affects only the electric energy density, as long as it remains dominant compared to the other components. As a consistency check, we verify that for a null coupling constant we recover the same electric energy density as obtained within the fully quantum electromagnetic description. Finally, we show that for non-null coupling constants Schwinger particle production drastically reduces the electric energy density during the final stages of inflation.

</details>
