### [61] [Simultaneous measurement of the Higgs boson decay rates to WW and ZZ in fully hadronic final states at FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04397)
*Jan Eysermans,Mila Pandurovic,Christoph Paus,Michele Selvaggi,Aman Desai*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper studies Higgs boson decays to WW and ZZ in the Higgsstrahlung process at FCC-ee (240 GeV, 10.8 ab⁻¹), focusing on fully hadronic six-jet final states; it achieves simultaneous extraction of signal strengths with relative uncertainties of 1.48% (H→WW) and 8.20% (H→ZZ) on σ(ZH)×BR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise determination of Higgs couplings to W/Z bosons is essential for model-independent measurement of the Higgs total decay width — a key FCC-ee physics goal.

Method: Analyzes ZH → 6 jets (Z→qq̄, H→WW/ZZ→4q) using jet clustering and dedicated jet-pairing algorithms to reconstruct hadronic W/Z decays and disentangle overlapping WW/ZZ kinematics; performs simultaneous fit to both channels.

Result: Expected relative uncertainties: 1.48% on σ(ZH)×BR(H→WW) and 8.20% on σ(ZH)×BR(H→ZZ), assuming √s = 240 GeV and 10.8 ab⁻¹ luminosity.

Conclusion: Fully hadronic six-jet channel provides competitive precision for H→WW and feasible sensitivity for H→ZZ at FCC-ee, enabling simultaneous, model-independent constraints on Higgs gauge couplings and total width.

Abstract: The precise determination of the Higgs boson couplings to electroweak gauge bosons is a key objective of the FCC-ee physics program. In particular, measurements of the Higgs boson couplings to W and Z bosons play a central role in the model-independent determination of its total decay width. This paper presents a study of Higgs boson decays to H -> WW and H -> ZZ in the Higgsstrahlung production process. The analysis focuses on events in which the associated Z boson and the vector bosons from the Higgs decay all decay hadronically, resulting in six-jet final states. The large H -> WW branching fraction leads to high signal statistics in the fully hadronic channel, while for H -> ZZ the much smaller Higgs branching fraction is partly compensated by the large hadronic branching fractions of the Z bosons. Due to the substantial kinematic overlap between the WW and ZZ decay modes, their signal strengths are extracted simultaneously. Jet-clustering and dedicated jet-pairing techniques are employed to optimize the reconstruction of the hadronically decaying gauge bosons and enhance the separation of the two signal components. Assuming a center-of-mass energy of 240 GeV and an integrated luminosity of 10.8 ab^-1, expected relative uncertainties of 1.48% and 8.20% are obtained on the production cross section times branching fraction, sigma(ZH) x BR, for the H -> WW and H -> ZZ decay modes, respectively.

</details>


### [62] [Search for Higgs boson compositeness effects in high-mass dilepton final states in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 and 13.6 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04782)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports a CMS search for Higgs boson compositeness using high-mass dielectron and dimuon events at 13 and 13.6 TeV, setting stringent 95% CL limits on oblique parameters W (−2.1–1.6 × 10⁻⁴) and Y (−3.9–3.5 × 10⁻⁴), and deriving improved TeV-scale compositeness constraints assuming Z′ resonances—including novel large-width scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe Higgs boson compositeness—a signature of physics beyond the Standard Model—via distortions in dilepton mass spectra and potential new resonances, motivated by the absence of direct BSM signals and theoretical expectations of compositeness at the TeV scale.

Method: Analysis of 207 fb⁻¹ of CMS data at √s = 13 and 13.6 TeV; performed oblique parameter fits (W, Y) using an effective field theory framework; conducted resonance searches for Z′-like states, including first-time exploration of large-width scenarios within the visible dilepton mass range.

Result: 95% CL limits: −2.1 × 10⁻⁴ < W < 1.6 × 10⁻⁴ and −3.9 × 10⁻⁴ < Y < 3.5 × 10⁻⁴; tighter compositeness bounds derived under Z′ resonance hypotheses, especially for large decay widths.

Conclusion: No evidence for Higgs compositeness or new resonances is found; results significantly constrain compositeness scales and oblique parameter space, advancing sensitivity to TeV-scale BSM dynamics in the electroweak sector.

Abstract: A search for beyond the standard model effects in high-mass dielectron and dimuon final states with the CMS detector is performed, with a specific focus on Higgs boson compositeness. The search uses data collected at the LHC at 13 and 13.6 TeV center-of-mass energies, corresponding to a total integrated luminosity of 207 fb$^{-1}$. Compositeness would manifest as distortions in the observed dilepton mass spectrum and the presence of bound states, such as the Higgs boson and new massive vector boson resonances. Assuming the existence of new boson states with masses well above the TeV scale, an effective field theory approach provides stringent bounds on the oblique parameters $\mathcal{W}$ and $\mathcal{Y}$, which are zero in the standard model. One-dimensional fits, in which only one of the oblique parameters is allowed to deviate from zero, provide the following limits: $-$3.9 $\times$ 10$^{-4}$$\lt$ $\mathcal{Y}$ $\lt$ 3.5 $\times$ 10$^{-4}$ and $-$2.1 $\times$ 10$^{-4}$ $\lt$ $\mathcal{W}$ $\lt$ 1.6 $\times$ 10$^{-4}$ at 95% confidence level. More stringent compositeness limits at the TeV scale are set in several new physics scenarios under the assumption that a new Z' resonance is physically present within or slightly above the visible dilepton mass spectrum. In this context, cases with large resonance decay widths are explored for the first time.

</details>

Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First amplitude analysis of the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decay D⁺ → K⁺π⁺π⁻π⁰ reveals first observations of D⁺ → K*⁰ρ⁺ and D⁺ → K*⁺ρ⁰, with precise branching fractions and polarization measurements; also reports improved measurement of D⁺ → K⁺ω.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decays of charmed mesons, probe QCD dynamics in charm decays, test factorization hypotheses, and investigate vector-vector (VV) final-state interactions and polarization patterns in D → VV transitions.

Method: Amplitude analysis of e⁺e⁻ collision data (20.3 fb⁻¹ at √s = 3.773 GeV) collected by BESIII; full Dalitz plot analysis including interference effects; spin–parity–dependent parameterization of intermediate resonances (K*⁰, K*⁺, ρ⁺, ρ⁰, ω); simultaneous fit to determine complex amplitudes, branching fractions, and polarization fractions.

Result: First observation of D⁺ → K*⁰ρ⁺ [ℬ = (5.67 ± 0.41 ± 0.17) × 10⁻⁴] and D⁺ → K*⁺ρ⁰ [ℬ = (5.32 ± 0.57 ± 0.24) × 10⁻⁴]; both exhibit large transverse polarization (longitudinal fractions f_L = 0.111 ± 0.024 ± 0.008 and 0.263 ± 0.049 ± 0.015); updated ℬ(D⁺ → K⁺ω) = (4.76 ± 0.84 ± 0.13) × 10⁻⁵.

Conclusion: The observed dominance of transverse polarization in D⁺ → VV decays challenges naive factorization expectations and suggests significant nonfactorizable contributions or dynamical suppression of longitudinal amplitudes; results provide new constraints for theoretical models (e.g., QCD factorization, SCET, light-cone sum rules) and serve as benchmarks for future charm physics studies.

Abstract: By analyzing an $e^+e^-$ collision data sample with an integrated luminosity of 20.3 fb$^{-1}$ collected with the BESIII detector at the center-of-mass energy of 3.773 GeV, we perform the first amplitude analysis on the doubly Cabibbo-suppressed decay $D^+ \to K^+π^+π^-π^0$ and report the first observation of $D^+ \to K^{*0}ρ^+$ and $D^+\to K^{*+}ρ^0$. The corresponding branching fractions are $(5.67\pm0.41_{\rm stat}\pm0.17_{\rm syst})\times10^{-4}$ and $(5.32\pm0.57_{\rm stat}\pm0.24_{\rm syst})\times10^{-4}$, respectively. These two $D\to VV$ decay both have large transverse polarizations. The longitudinal polarization fractions are measured to be $0.111\pm0.024_{\rm stat}\pm0.008_{\rm syst}$ and $0.263\pm0.049_{\rm stat}\pm0.015_{\rm syst}$, respectively. The branching fraction of the decay $D^+\to K^+ω$ is measured to be $(4.76\pm0.84_{\rm stat}\pm0.13_{\rm syst})\times 10^{-5}$.

</details>


### [64] [Precision measurement of ocean tides using cosmic-ray muons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05294)
*Jiwon Seo,Jongseok Chung,Chang Hyon Ha,TongZhou Huang,Jinyoung Kim,Sooa Kim,Hani Kimku,Byoung-cheol Koh,Minsu Kwak,Sihyun Lee,Yujin Lee*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that cosmic-ray muons can be used to monitor ocean-tide-induced variations in seawater overburden within an underwater tunnel, achieving meter-scale resolution and quantitative agreement with tide-gauge data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a non-local, integrated measurement technique for time-dependent environmental mass variations—specifically tidal changes in seawater overburden—beyond the point-wise limitations of conventional tide gauges.

Method: Muon flux attenuation measurements inside an underwater tunnel were performed continuously and correlated with independent tide-gauge records; a spatial muography scan was conducted to validate the overburden model, including seawater contribution.

Result: Observed muon rate modulation matches tide-gauge data with attenuation coefficient k = 43.2 ± 1.0 counts/h/m; meter-scale overburden variations resolved; seawater contribution confirmed essential via muographic model validation.

Conclusion: Muography enables quantitative, integrated monitoring of dynamic environmental mass distributions, offering a novel geophysical tool for observing time-varying overburden such as tidal loading.

Abstract: Cosmic-ray muons provide a natural probe of the integrated mass along their trajectories. Here we show that cosmic-ray muons can monitor ocean-tide-induced variations in seawater overburden within an underwater tunnel. The observed modulation closely tracks independent tide-gauge measurements, yielding an attenuation response coefficient of $k=43.2\pm1.0$~counts$\rm /h/m$ and resolving meter-scale variations in seawater overburden. A spatial muography scan validates the overburden model, confirming that the seawater contribution is essential to reproduce the observed attenuation. Unlike conventional tide gauges that measure sea level locally, muography measures the integrated overburden above the detector. These results demonstrate that muography can quantitatively resolve small, time-dependent variations in environmental overburden, providing a new approach to monitoring dynamic natural systems.

</details>


### [65] [Search for Diffuse Supernova Neutrino Background of All Flavors with PandaX-4T](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05980)
*Yingxin Zhang,PandaX Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports the first search for the diffuse supernova neutrino background (DSNB) using coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering (CEνNS) in the PandaX-4T liquid xenon detector, yielding a competitive 90% CL upper limit on the DSNB ν_x flux above 19.3 MeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the diffuse supernova neutrino background—a cosmological neutrino flux from all past core-collapse supernovae—using CEνNS, a low-threshold, high-sensitivity channel previously unexploited for DSNB searches.

Method: Analysis of 1.54 tonne·year exposure from PandaX-4T’s commissioning and first science runs; background modeling and statistical inference to derive an upper limit on the DSNB ν_x flux via CEνNS signal expectation above 19.3 MeV.

Result: No significant excess observed; upper limit of (2.27–2.43) × 10³ cm⁻² s⁻¹ per flavor (ν_μ, ν̄_μ, ν_τ, ν̄_τ) at 90% CL for E_ν > 19.3 MeV.

Conclusion: The PandaX-4T experiment sets the first CEνNS-based constraint on the DSNB, achieving sensitivity comparable to the world’s best limits, demonstrating CEνNS as a viable channel for future DSNB detection.

Abstract: We present the first search for the diffuse supernova neutrino background utilizing coherent elastic neutrino-nucleus scattering with the PandaX-4T liquid xenon detector at the China Jinping Underground Laboratory. Based on a total exposure of $1.54~\mathrm{tonne \cdot year}$ from the commissioning and first science runs, we observe no significant event excess over the expected background. We therefore set an upper limit of $(2.27 - 2.43) \times 10^3~\mathrm{cm^{-2}s^{-1}}$ per flavor at 90\% confidence level on the $ν_x$ flux (each of $ν_μ, \barν_μ, ν_τ, \barν_τ$) of the diffuse supernova neutrino background for incident neutrino energies above 19.3~MeV. The result is comparable to the current world best limit.

</details>


### [66] [A graph neural network for deep learning-based full event interpretation at FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06054)
*Elena Graverini,Samuel Morand,Rafael Silva Coutinho,Felipe Luan Souza de Almeida*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a graph neural network (GNN) framework for full event interpretation in FCC-ee $e^+e^- \rightarrow Z \rightarrow b\bar{b}$ events, achieving >90% accuracy in reconstructing correct particle sets and >93% clustering efficiency for multi-generation $b$-hadron decays using IDEA detector simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable precise, end-to-end reconstruction of hierarchical $b$-hadron decay trees in high-luminosity $e^+e^-$ colliders like FCC-ee, where traditional sequential reconstruction methods face combinatorial and resolution challenges.

Method: A graph neural network operating on event-level graphs: charged particle tracks are nodes; pairwise kinematic and topological observables (e.g., angular separations, invariant mass proxies) serve as edge features; the GNN jointly performs clustering (grouping particles into decay chains) and hierarchical tree reconstruction (inferring parent–child relationships).

Result: On simulated $Z \rightarrow b\bar{b}$ events with IDEA detector response: 90% of reconstructed decay chains contain exactly the correct particle set (regardless of hierarchy); clustering efficiency exceeds 93% across benchmark $b$-hadron decays with up to three decay generations.

Conclusion: The proposed GNN-based full event interpretation framework demonstrates high fidelity in simultaneous particle clustering and decay tree inference, establishing a viable path toward automated, physics-informed reconstruction for future lepton colliders.

Abstract: This paper presents a deep learning-based full event interpretation prototype developed for the electron-positron Future Circular Collider (FCC-$ee$) using simulated $e^{+}e^{-}\rightarrow Z\rightarrow b\bar{b}$ events at $\sqrt{s} = 91 \ \mathrm{GeV}$. The study was carried out using the Innovative Detector for Electron positron Accelerators (IDEA) detector concept as case study for future application. The proposed graph neural network framework aims to simultaneously identify and reconstruct the hierarchical decay trees of $b$-hadrons in each event using information from reconstructed charged particles. Events are represented as graphs, where reconstructed charged particles form the nodes and pairwise observables are encoded as edge features describing the relations between particles. On a test sample, 90% of reconstructed decay chains contain exactly the correct set of particles, irrespective of their inferred decay hierarchy. Across a range of benchmark $b$-hadron decays, this clustering efficiency exceeds 93% for decay chains spanning up to three generations of decays.

</details>


### [67] [SysVar: Consistent and scalable treatment of systematic uncertainties in template-based analyses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06117)
*Ilias Tsaklidis,Agrim Aggarwal,Georgios Alexandris,Tristan Fillinger,Giacomo De Pietro,Daniel Ivanov,Melisa-Melek Akdag,Markus Prim,Florian Bernlochner*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: SysVar is a lightweight, open-source Python package that automates propagation of correlated systematic uncertainties from correction weights into histogrammed templates—preserving full covariance via orthogonal eigenvariations—thereby simplifying and robustifying high-precision template-based signal extraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: As analyses scale, propagating systematic uncertainties from correction weights into simulated templates while preserving correlations across signal extraction variables becomes increasingly complex and error-prone.

Method: SysVar applies correction weights, constructs systematic variations, and histograms nominal/varied templates; it preserves correlations by propagating the full covariance matrix and compressing it into a small set of orthogonal eigenvariations (each mapped to a standard shape nuisance parameter).

Result: SysVar streamlines error-prone workflows for high-precision measurements and enables consistent combination of independent pseudo-measurements via correlated template-level systematics.

Conclusion: SysVar provides an experiment-agnostic, pip-installable solution that significantly reduces bookkeeping overhead while maintaining statistical rigor in systematic uncertainty treatment for template-based analyses.

Abstract: Propagating systematic uncertainties from correction weights into templates generated from simulated data while preserving correlations in the signal extraction variables becomes increasingly challenging as analyses scale in size. SysVar's lightweight API hides most of the bookkeeping complexity of applying correction weights, building systematic variations, and histogramming nominal and varied templates. This streamlines workflows that are otherwise error-prone but essential for high-precision measurements. Correlations are preserved by propagating the full covariance across the analysis and compressing it into a small set of orthogonal eigenvariations, each defining an ordinary shape nuisance parameter. In this work, we demonstrate SysVar's workflow and impact by combining two independent pseudo-measurements that benefit from consistently correlated systematic uncertainties encoded in the template shapes. SysVar is open-source, pip-installable, and experiment-agnostic.

</details>


### [68] [FPGA-based muon shower identification and graph neural network tracking algorithms for HL-LHC triggers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06379)
*Daniel Estrada,Elena Aller,Andrea Cardini,Santiago Folgueras,Pelayo Leguina,Javier Prado*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces two FPGA-based hardware-accelerated strategies for the CMS Level-1 Trigger upgrade: (1) a low-latency muon shower identification algorithm using Drift Tube hit multiplicities in the barrel, and (2) a quantized GraphSAGE-based GNN for displaced muon track reconstruction in the barrel-endcap overlap region, achieving 20% DSP usage and 19-cycle deterministic latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To recover L1T efficiency losses caused by highly energetic radiating muons and unconventional signatures—particularly Long-Lived Particles (LLPs)—which are poorly captured by current trigger algorithms operating on higher-level objects rather than raw detector hits.

Method: First strategy: A custom, ultra-low-resource FPGA algorithm monitoring hit multiplicities in CMS Drift Tube chambers to generate Shower Primitives with sub-bunch-crossing latency. Second strategy: A hardware-software co-design of a GraphSAGE-inspired GNN for displaced muon tracking in the overlap region, employing INT8-PO2 quantization, data-driven bit-width optimization, and replacement of DSP-intensive fixed-point multiplications with compile-time bit-shifts.

Result: The shower identification algorithm successfully tags showered muons with minimal resource use and sub-bunch-crossing latency. The GNN implementation achieves 20% DSP utilization and deterministic 19-clock-cycle inference latency on FPGA, validating feasibility for real-time L1T deployment.

Conclusion: These two complementary, hardware-native approaches enable robust, low-latency triggering on non-standard muon signatures—including LLP decays—at Level-1, significantly enhancing CMS’s physics reach for beyond-Standard-Model phenomena.

Abstract: This work presents two hardware-accelerated strategies for the upgrade of the CMS Level-1 Trigger (L1T) to target efficiency losses due to highly energetic radiating muons and unconventional physics signatures, such as Long-Lived Particles (LLPs). Operating directly on low-level detector hits, the first strategy implements a dedicated muon shower identification algorithm in the CMS barrel region that monitors hit multiplicities in the Drift Tube chambers to generate Shower Primitives. Executing with sub-bunch-crossing latency and ultra-low resource consumption, this algorithm successfully tags showered muons and provides essential context for downstream track finders. The second approach targets the barrel-endcap transition (overlap) region by evaluating Graph Neural Networks to reconstruct displaced muon tracks. Following a divided methodology that first isolates hardware feasibility from evolving physics refinements, an initial proxy model based on a GraphSAGE architecture is evaluated to validate its viability on FPGAs within strict latency limits. This implementation utilizes an INT8-PO2 quantization technique coupled with a data-driven bit-width optimization. By replacing DSP-heavy fixed-point multiplications with fast compile-time arithmetic bit-shifts and allocating the minimum required bits per signal, this hardware-software co-design reduces DSP utilization to $20\%$ and achieves a deterministic inference latency of just $19$ clock cycles. Together, these developments pave the way for triggering on non-standard muon signatures, such as those from LLP decays, at L1T.

</details>


### [69] [Design optimization of the inter-electrode structures in DC-RSD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06676)
*N. Cartiglia,R. Arcidiacono,G. Bardelli,M. Boscardin,A. Cassese,M. Centis Vignali,T. Croci,M. Durando,M. Ferrero,A. Fondacci,S. Gaiero,O. Hammad Ali,L. Lanteri,A. Losana,L. Menzio,A. Morozzi,F. Moscatelli,D. Passeri,G. Paternoster,G. Sguazzoni,F. Siviero,V. Sola,L. Viliani*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper compares inter-electrode structures (trenches, resistive strips, no containment) in C-coupled Resistive Silicon Detectors (RSDs) using a custom 2D Kirchhoff-network simulator (DCRSD_SurfaceSimulator), validated against test-beam data; it finds resistive strips yield ~25% better spatial resolution and superior uniformity than trenches due to a stronger, more uniform charge-sharing gradient.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which inter-electrode structure — trench, resistive strip, or none — best optimizes spatial resolution and its uniformity in C-coupled RSDs.

Method: Developed DCRSD_SurfaceSimulator, a ROOT-based 2D Kirchhoff resistive network model of the n+ surface layer, parameterized by hit position, surface resistivity, and inter-electrode geometry; validated against test-beam data from FBK DC-RSD sensors (300–1000 μm pitch); performed comparative simulation across structures for resolution, uniformity, inter-electrode resistance, and signal leakage.

Result: Resistive strips improve position resolution by ~25% over trenches across 500–2000 Ω strip resistances and 0–100 Ω input impedances at 1000 Ω/□ surface resistivity; this gain stems from a stronger and more spatially uniform gradient in the charge-sharing fraction, especially away from pixel centers.

Conclusion: Resistive strips are the optimal inter-electrode structure for maximizing both spatial resolution and uniformity in C-coupled RSDs under the studied conditions, outperforming trenches and unstructured surfaces due to superior charge-sharing gradient characteristics.

Abstract: C-coupled Resistive Silicon Detectors (RSD) share charge among electrodes through a resistive n$^+$ surface layer, and the choice of inter-electrode structure --- a trench, a resistive strip, or no structure at all --- directly determines the achievable position resolution and its uniformity. This paper addresses the question of which structure best optimises the spatial resolution. To answer it quantitatively, DCRSD_SurfaceSimulator was developed, a ROOT-based tool that models the resistive surface as a two-dimensional Kirchhoff resistive network and solves for charge sharing as a function of hit position, surface resistivity, and inter-electrode structure. The simulator is validated against test-beam data from FBK DC-RSD sensors with 300, 500, and 1000~$μ$m pixel pitch, reproducing the position resolution, its spatial uniformity, and the signal-sharing templates correctly. Using the validated simulator, trenches, resistive strips, and no containment are then compared on position resolution and its spatial uniformity, inter-electrode resistance, and signal leakage to outer pixels. Resistive strips are found to outperform trenches in resolution by approximately 25\% for all tested strip resistances (500--2000~$Ω$) and input impedances (0--100~$Ω$), at a surface resistivity of 1000~$Ω/\square$, and trace the origin of this difference to the spatial gradient of the charge-sharing fraction: trenches produce a markedly weaker and less uniform gradient, particularly away from the pixel centre, than either a resistive strip or a bare resistive surface.

</details>


### [70] [How to scale your HEP ML models: A recipe for robust architecture comparisons at scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06784)
*Matthias Vigl,Nikita Pond,Jackson Barr,Alexander Froch,Dan Guest,Nicole Hartman,Michael Kagan,Lukas Heinrich*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper establishes robust scaling laws for machine learning in high-energy physics (HEP), validating them on toy problems and applying them to multi-task transformers on the large ATLAS JetSet2 dataset; it identifies optimal hyperparameters under data constraints, confirms √C scaling in compute-optimal regime, shows benefits of auxiliary objectives and low-level inputs, and emphasizes the necessity of large, high-fidelity datasets to observe meaningful power-law scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable systematic, reproducible scaling studies in high-energy physics—where recent empirical scaling behavior mirrors that in language modeling—but lacking standardized methodology for deriving robust scaling laws across relevant budget axes (compute, data, model size, training time).

Method: A systematic procedure for deriving scaling laws: (1) validation on controlled toy problems; (2) application to multi-task transformer models on the ~11B-jet ATLAS JetSet2 dataset; (3) separate analysis in data-constrained and compute-constrained regimes; (4) joint hyperparameter optimization (model size, training horizon, learning rate, batch size) under early stopping for data-limited case; (5) ablation of input representation granularity and auxiliary task inclusion.

Result: In the data-constrained regime: first prediction of jointly optimal hyperparameters under early stopping. In the compute-optimal regime: near-equal √C scaling of model and dataset size; auxiliary objectives reduce primary jet-classification loss at fixed compute; expanding inputs to lower-level features reduces loss without altering scaling exponent; power-law scaling only emerges above a critical dataset size threshold.

Conclusion: Robust scaling laws are attainable in HEP, but require large, high-quality full-simulation datasets to reliably observe asymptotic behavior; the proposed methodology enables principled foundation model development and resource-aware design of ML systems for particle physics.

Abstract: Much of the recent progress in machine learning domains such as language models has come from scaling laws that predict performance as a function of training effort. In high-energy physics (HEP) similar behavior has now been observed. To aid further study, we present a systematic procedure to derive robust scaling laws and compare design choices on the relevant budget axes for HEP tasks. We first validate the full scaling trajectory on toy problems and then apply the procedure to multi-task transformers on the ~11 billion-jet ATLAS JetSet2 dataset, in both the compute- and data-constrained regimes. For the latter, we predict, to the best of our knowledge for the first time, the jointly optimal model size, training horizon, learning rate and batch size under early stopping. At compute-optimal scaling, we recover a near-equal $\sqrt{C}$ dependence of model and dataset size, and find that auxiliary objectives lower the primary jet-classification loss at equal compute budget. Expanding the inputs toward lower-level data systematically lowers the loss while leaving the scaling exponent nearly unchanged. The onset of the power-law regime is itself set by scale: below a threshold in dataset size the loss carries little information about high-compute scaling, underscoring the value of large, high-quality full-simulation datasets as a foundation for scaling studies and the development of foundation models in HEP.

</details>


### [71] [Reconfigurable On-Chip AI for Particle Physics Detectors with Logic Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06816)
*Mila Bileska,Lino Gerlach,Jennet Dickinson,Lindsey Gray,Giuseppe Di Guglielmo,Peter Elmer,Isobel Ojalvo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper evaluates Logic Neural Networks (LNNs) for on-chip inference in next-generation particle physics pixel detectors, demonstrating their superior resource efficiency, radiation robustness, and—through novel reconfigurable designs—their ability to adapt post-deployment to radiation-induced detector degradation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Next-generation pixel detectors face extreme data rates, strict power/space constraints, and radiation-induced parameter drift, necessitating highly efficient, robust, and adaptable on-chip ML models—conventional and hard-wired solutions fail on one or more of these axes.

Method: The authors evaluate LNNs for on-chip inference; analyze their hardware efficiency and radiation tolerance; and introduce a novel reconfigurable LNN architecture supporting post-deployment model retraining and reconfiguration.

Result: LNNs enable more complex models within tight hardware budgets; exhibit increased robustness to radiation-induced bit flips; and—via the proposed reconfigurable variant—support adaptation to evolving detector response.

Conclusion: LNNs, especially in reconfigurable form, are a promising solution for adaptive, radiation-hard, ultra-efficient on-device ML in high-energy physics pixel detectors—and potentially other harsh-edge environments.

Abstract: On-device machine learning is increasingly important in applications where extreme data rates or resource constraints make centralized processing infeasible. Logic neural networks (LNNs), which directly learn Boolean logic instead of conventional arithmetic operations, have recently emerged as a state-of-the-art approach for efficient inference and are particularly well suited for deployment in custom silicon. However, hard-wired solutions are typically optimized for narrowly defined tasks and lack the ability to adapt to changing data distributions. Next-generation pixel detectors in particle physics present a particularly challenging use case: their high granularity and readout frequency generate data rates that cannot be centrally processed, while stringent space and power constraints necessitate highly efficient on-chip inference. Moreover, radiation damage progressively alters detector response, requiring models that can be re-trained and reconfigured after deployment. In this work, we evaluate LNNs for on-chip inference in pixel detectors. We show that their superior resource efficiency may enable a step change in model capability, making more complex architectures feasible within the available hardware budget. LNNs also provide increased robustness against radiation-induced random bit flips. Finally, we present novel work on reconfigurable LNNs, enabling models to adapt to changing operating conditions after deployment.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [72] [Time-dependent integrable quantum field theories with dynamical boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03870)
*Pradip Kattel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs time-dependent integrable chiral quantum field theories on a finite interval using scattering and reflection data from autonomous integrable models; spectral labels evolve affinely in time, linking bulk scattering and boundary reflections to boundary quantum Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations; the local Hamiltonian is reconstructed from factorized S- and R-matrices, and the wavefunction is obtained via characteristic pullback of the BqKZ solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct explicitly time-dependent integrable quantum field theories on bounded domains—beyond static or periodic driving—by leveraging the structural constraints of factorized scattering and boundary reflection in chiral models.

Method: Uses affine spectral coordinate maps to enforce time-locality of scattering events; derives boundary quantum Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov (BqKZ) equations from round-trip consistency of spectral translations across reflections; reconstructs time-dependent local Hamiltonians by solving one- and two-particle matching conditions from bulk S-matrix and boundary R-matrix; obtains many-body wavefunctions via characteristic pullback of BqKZ solutions.

Result: A unified framework where autonomous factorized scattering (Yang–Baxter) and reflection (boundary Yang–Baxter) data fully determine both the time-dependent driven Hamiltonian and the exact many-body wavefunction; explicit examples include rational SU(N) and O(3)-invariant models with dynamical boundary impurities realizing driven Kondo couplings.

Conclusion: Time-dependent integrability in chiral QFTs on intervals is governed by affine spectral evolution, with BqKZ flatness encoding global consistency; the same factorized data underlie both the transport problem and the local Hamiltonian, establishing a duality between boundary-driven dynamics and integrable deformation of autonomous models.

Abstract: We present a method for constructing time-dependent integrable chiral quantum field theories on a finite spatial interval from the scattering and reflection data of an autonomous integrable model. Each particle carries a spectral label that is constant along its free trajectory. Requiring a right--left scattering event at a fixed time to be independent of its position forces the map from characteristic coordinates to spectral labels to be affine, with the same slope for both chiralities. As a result, all bulk scattering events at a given time sample one common spectral argument, and the two boundary reflections sample the corresponding endpoint arguments. The bulk and boundary drives are not independent time-dependent deformations: their time dependence is fixed by the same affine spectral coordinate. Successive reflections at the two endpoints translate the spectral label by a finite amount. Following a particle through a complete round trip then produces a difference transport whose consistency gives the boundary quantum Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov equations. The Yang--Baxter and reflection equations ensure local compatibility, while BqKZ flatness ensures compatibility of complete round trips. We then reconstruct the local Hamiltonian by inverting the one- and two-particle matching problems. The bulk matrix fixes the contact interaction, while the reflection matrix fixes the corresponding boundary coupling. The same autonomous factorized data determine the boundary qKZ transport problem and the local driven Hamiltonian. Once the boundary qKZ system is solved, its characteristic pullback gives the corresponding time-dependent many-body wavefunction. We illustrate the construction with rational $SU(N)$ models and an $O(3)$-invariant model with dynamical boundary impurities, obtaining driven Kondo interactions whose couplings are fixed by the same affine spectral coordinate.

</details>


### [73] [Quenched Growth of Normal Matrices and Harmonic Measure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03912)
*Oleg Alekseev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the conditional distribution of additional eigenvalues in a large normal random matrix after freezing an initial N-eigenvalue configuration; it proves convergence of their spatial distribution to harmonic measure on the boundary of the spectral droplet under scaling constraints (M/log N → ∞, M/N → 0), and establishes an exact finite-rank identity linking polynomial moments of the conditional mean to spectral fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how newly sampled eigenvalues distribute conditionally on a frozen subset in normal random matrices, and to rigorously connect this conditional law to harmonic measure — a fundamental object in potential theory and random matrix theory — especially in the semiclassical regime with growing but sublinear perturbations.

Method: Uses tools from two-dimensional Coulomb gas theory, orthogonal polynomial ensembles, harmonic measure, and semiclassical analysis; employs projection onto vanishing polynomials, conditional probability arguments, and asymptotic analysis of polynomial ensembles under analytic droplet regularity assumptions.

Result: Proves that the empirical distribution of M new eigenvalues converges weakly to harmonic measure on the boundary of the original spectral droplet, almost surely with respect to the frozen spectrum (outside a vanishing-probability set); establishes an exact finite-rank identity relating conditional polynomial moments to fluctuations of the frozen spectrum, with error controlled by high-degree polynomial modes.

Conclusion: The conditional eigenvalue distribution in large normal random matrices is asymptotically governed by harmonic measure — not the equilibrium measure — when adding a sublinear number of eigenvalues relative to the matrix size, revealing a sharp geometric-potential-theoretic structure underlying conditional sampling in Coulomb gases.

Abstract: The eigenvalues of a large normal random matrix form a two-dimensional Coulomb gas. At fixed weight, the spectral droplet grows with the matrix rank, and its semiclassical density increment is harmonic measure. We freeze an $N$-eigenvalue configuration and sample $M$ further eigenvalues using the projection onto polynomials of degree less than $N+M$ that vanish at the frozen points. Their conditional law is an exact polynomial ensemble in the potential of the frozen charges. For a regular analytic droplet, we prove that the spatial distribution of the new eigenvalues converges to harmonic measure on the old boundary when $M/\log N\to\infty$ and $M/N\to0$. This convergence holds conditionally on the frozen spectrum, outside a set of frozen configurations whose probability tends to zero. The equilibrium process formed from orthogonal polynomials of degrees $N,\ldots,N+M-1$ has the same limit. An exact finite-rank identity relates polynomial moments of the conditional mean to fluctuations of the frozen spectrum, with a remainder from polynomial modes of degree $N+M$ and above.

</details>


### [74] [Branching Gaps, Coexistence, and Boundary Influence for the Ising Model on Spherically Symmetric Trees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03941)
*Farrukh Mukhamedov,Otabek Khakimov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes Gibbs uniqueness and phase coexistence for the ferromagnetic Ising model on spherically symmetric rooted trees with variable branching (1 or q children per level), linking structural properties—especially patterns of nonbranching (one-child) levels—to thermodynamic behavior under nonzero external field and finite temperature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how tree geometry—specifically the distribution and length of nonbranching (linear) stretches—affects Gibbs uniqueness, phase coexistence, and critical phenomena in the Ising model, beyond classical homogeneous or periodic settings.

Method: Analytical study of the recursive renormalization map (tree recurrence) for magnetization; fixed-point analysis of return maps for eventually periodic branching sequences; use of Schwarzian derivative to classify bifurcations; construction of explicit counterexamples via density-one trees with prescribed decay rates; comparison with periodic reference trees using monotonicity and coupling arguments.

Result: 1) Unbounded runs of one-child levels imply uniqueness at all β < ∞ and h ≠ 0; 2) For any vanishing sequence, there exists a density-one tree where boundary influence decays slower than that sequence along a subsequence; 3) Eventually bounded gaps admit optimal uniform comparison with the largest-gap periodic tree, yielding an explicit coexistence field interval; 4) For eventually periodic gaps, Gibbs uniqueness ⇔ fixed-point uniqueness of the full-period return map; 5) With negative Schwarzian derivative, the supercritical return map has exactly three fixed points: two nondegenerate folds at the endpoints of a closed coexistence interval and one repelling fixed point outside.

Conclusion: Tree geometry controls Ising phase behavior fundamentally: long linear stretches destroy coexistence, while bounded variability preserves it robustly; the return map’s dynamical properties—especially Schwarzian sign and fixed-point structure—fully characterize uniqueness and coexistence, enabling a rigorous classification of thermodynamic phases in inhomogeneous tree models.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the ferromagnetic nearest-neighbor Ising model on rooted spherically symmetric trees with one or \(q\) children per vertex, according to a prescribed sequence of branching levels. Unbounded runs of one-child levels force uniqueness at every nonzero homogeneous field and every finite inverse temperature. We quantify this effect through the locations of sufficiently long nonbranching stretches. Conversely, uniqueness need not entail any uniform rate: for each prescribed vanishing sequence, we construct a density-one tree whose finite-volume boundary influence decays more slowly along a subsequence. The tree retains branching number \(q\), although its nonzero-field coexistence region disappears. For eventually bounded gaps, we prove an optimal uniform comparison with the periodic tree having the largest allowed gap and obtain an explicit interval of coexistence fields. Thus bounded gaps are equivalent, within this family, to coexistence at some finite temperature and nonzero field. For eventually periodic gaps, Gibbs uniqueness is equivalent to fixed-point uniqueness of the full-period return map. A negative Schwarzian derivative yields a complete classification: in the supercritical regime the map has three fixed points inside a closed coexistence interval, two at its endpoints, and one outside. The nonzero endpoints are automatically nondegenerate folds, with square-root splitting of the merging branches.

</details>


### [75] [A Self-Generated Local Structural Prior for Resolving Target Merging in Electrical Impedance Tomography (EIT)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04394)
*Junkang Liu,Yizhuang Song*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a self-generated local structural prior to improve spatial separability in Electrical Impedance Tomography (EIT) reconstruction, preventing merging of neighboring conductivity targets by leveraging eigenvector-based structural information from an initial reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional EIT suffers from ill-posedness and spatially nonuniform resolution, causing merged reconstructions of adjacent conductivity targets—especially in low-sensitivity regions—thereby losing spatial separability.

Method: A two-stage method: (1) obtain an initial conductivity image via standard EIT reconstruction; (2) construct a Gaussian similarity matrix from pixel intensities, compute its covariance matrix, select an adaptive eigenvector to extract principal structural features, and generate a localized structural mask incorporated into a refined EIT reconstruction objective.

Result: Numerical simulations and water tank experiments show the method successfully separates previously merged neighboring conductivity targets, outperforming conventional EIT methods in preserving spatial separability.

Conclusion: The self-generated local structural prior, derived adaptively from the initial reconstruction, effectively enhances target delineation without requiring external anatomical or structural priors, offering a practical and data-driven improvement for EIT.

Abstract: Electrical impedance tomography (EIT) is an ill-posed inverse imaging technique with limited and spatially nonuniform resolution. Conductivity targets located in regions with degraded spatial sensitivity may be reconstructed as a single connected region, resulting in the loss of spatial separability between neighboring structures. In this work, we propose a self-generated local structural prior for suppressing target merging in EIT reconstruction. First, an initial conductivity image is obtained from the measured boundary data using an existing EIT reconstruction method. This reconstruction is subsequently used as a reference image to construct a Gaussian similarity matrix according to the intensity similarity between image pixels. The adaptively chosen eigenvector of the covariance matrix is then employed to extract the principal structural information of the reference image. A localized structural mask is constructed and incorporated into a subsequent EIT reconstruction to preserve the spatial separability of neighboring targets. Numerical and water tank experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method effectively separates neighboring conductivity targets that are merged by conventional EIT reconstruction methods.

</details>


### [76] [Supersymmetric semi--infinite cohomology and $N = 2$ superconformal structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04434)
*Thomas Creutzig,Uhi Rinn Suh,Sangwon Yoon*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology with N=2 superconformal structure via BRST reduction of an N=3/2 superconformal structure on a super-affine vertex algebra linked to a super Manin pair satisfying the Kazama–Suzuki condition, and realizes super coset models using relative supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous algebraic framework for supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology equipped with N=2 superconformal symmetry, motivated by applications in string theory and topological field theory—particularly in relation to Kazama–Suzuki coset constructions.

Method: BRST reduction applied to an N=3/2 superconformal structure on a super-affine vertex algebra associated with a super Manin pair; use of relative supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology to model super cosets.

Result: A construction of N=2 superconformal supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology; realization of super coset models as relative cohomologies within this framework.

Conclusion: The BRST reduction approach successfully yields an N=2 superconformal structure on supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology, unifying vertex-algebraic and geometric aspects of super coset models under the Kazama–Suzuki condition.

Abstract: We study supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology and its $N = 2$ superconformal structure via the BRST reduction of an $N = {\frac{3}{2}}$ superconformal structure on the super-affine vertex algebra associated to a super Manin pair arising from the Kazama--Suzuki $N = 2$ condition. Moreover, we realize super coset models in terms of relative supersymmetric semi-infinite cohomology.

</details>


### [77] [Covariant Euler-Poincaré Reduction for Field Theories on Centered Semidirect Products](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04628)
*Miguel Ángel Berbel,Leonardo Colombo,Álvaro Rodríguez Abella*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a geometric reduction framework for covariant field theories on principal bundles with structure group given by a centered semidirect product $G\Join V$, yielding decomposed Euler-Poincaré and Lie-Poisson field equations, multisymplectic reduction, and balance laws; it clarifies the role of centered variables via heart/diamond operators, and applies the theory to matrix microstructure models and jet-group field theories, including exact solutions and numerical validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize geometric field-theoretic reduction beyond standard semidirect products by incorporating commuting left/right representations—capturing two-sided symmetry data—and to resolve ambiguities in physical modeling (e.g., nematodynamics) through intrinsic decomposition of microstructural variables.

Method: Hamiltonian and Lagrangian reduction on principal bundles with centered semidirect-product structure group $G\Join V$; use of centered variables, heart ($\heartsuit$) and diamond ($\diamond$) operators to separate left/right contributions; multisymplectic formalism; application to matrix representation decomposition and jet-group geometry; analytical derivation of field equations and conservation laws; numerical reconstruction and consistency verification via mesh refinement and wave-map comparison.

Result: Reduced Euler-Poincaré and Lie-Poisson field equations split into $\mathfrak{g}$- and $V$-components; reduced multisymplectic form formula; Noether and energy-momentum balance laws; decomposition of $\operatorname{Mat}(3)$ under conjugation into scalar/vector/symmetric-traceless sectors clarifying director/alignment-tensor relations; for $G_r^2$ on $(1+1)$-Minkowski space, derivation of a semilinear symmetric-hyperbolic system, an exact traveling conversion front with explicit inter-sector energy-momentum exchange, conserved positive energy for linearized perturbations, and validated nonlinear evolution numerically.

Conclusion: The centered semidirect product framework provides a natural, geometrically faithful reduction scheme that preserves and disentangles two-sided symmetries, enabling precise physical interpretation of microstructural fields and robust computational modeling—demonstrated in both matrix and jet-group settings.

Abstract: Let $G$ be a Lie group equipped with commuting left and right linear representations on a vector space $V$, and let $G\Join V$ be the associated centered semidirect product. We develop a reduction scheme for covariant field theories on principal bundles with structure group $G\Join V$ from both Lagrangian and Hamiltonian perspectives, obtaining the reduced Euler-Poincaré and Lie-Poisson field equations decomposed into their $\mathfrak g$- and $V$-components, where $\mathfrak g$ is the Lie algebra of $G$. We also derive the reduced multisymplectic form formula, as well as the Noether and energy-momentum balance laws. Our reduction results are compared with those for principal bundles with standard semidirect-product structure groups, and the role of the centered variables is investigated: they retain the original two-sided geometric data and separate the left and right contributions via the heart and diamond operators. The framework is then applied to a two-sided matrix microstructural model and to the second-order jet group on $\mathbb R^r$, $G_r^2$. For the matrix model, the conjugation representation decomposes $\operatorname{Mat}(3)$ into scalar, vector, and symmetric-traceless sectors, clarifying its precise relation to director and alignment-tensor variables without identifying it with standard nematodynamics. For the one-dimensional jet group on a $(1+1)$-dimensional Minkowski base, we obtain a semilinear symmetric-hyperbolic system and an exact traveling conversion front with explicit energy-momentum exchange between the linear- and quadratic-jet sectors. Its hyperbolic-target interpretation yields a conserved positive energy for the linearized perturbations. A nonlinear numerical experiment illustrates the reduced evolution and reconstruction of the group-valued field, with consistency assessed through mesh refinement and an independent wave-map computation.

</details>


### [78] [Dynamical quasi-symmetry of the classical Kepler-Coulomb problem and the conservation of the Laplace-Runge-Lenz vector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04650)
*Radosław Szmytkowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies a new dynamical quasi-symmetry for the Kepler–Coulomb problem in ℝ^N (N ≥ 2) via a projective radius transformation and time rescaling, mapping trajectories between conics and linking energy conservation in the transformed system to Laplace–Runge–Lenz vector conservation in the original system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover novel dynamical symmetries beyond the known SO(N+1)/SO(N,1) Kepler–Coulomb symmetry, specifically exploring geometric transformations that preserve structural features of motion while altering the effective potential or metric.

Method: Applies an extended-configuration-space transformation: projective spatial map r → R = r/(1 + c·r) combined with differential time scaling dt → dT = dt/(1 + c·r)²; analyzes how Newton’s equation transforms under this map; performs analytical and graphical study of induced mappings between conic sections and velocity hodographs; derives implications for conserved quantities.

Result: The transformation yields a dynamical quasi-symmetry mapping the original Kepler–Coulomb equation into a modified form with sign-dependent effective force; establishes explicit trajectory correspondences between non-degenerate conics (ellipses, parabolas, hyperbolas); shows that energy conservation in the transformed system is equivalent to Laplace–Runge–Lenz vector conservation in the original system.

Conclusion: A previously unrecognized dynamical quasi-symmetry exists for the Kepler–Coulomb problem, independent yet complementary to the standard dynamical symmetry group; it provides a geometric unification of conic trajectories and reveals a deep equivalence between energy conservation in the transformed frame and hidden symmetry (LRL vector) in the original frame.

Abstract: The paper studies a classical non-relativistic particle moving in $\mathbb{R}^{N}$, $(N\geqslant2)$, in the presence of the Kepler-Coulomb force $\boldsymbol{F}(\boldsymbol{r})=α\boldsymbol{r}/r^{3}$, $(α\neq0)$. It is shown that the extended-configuration-space transformation involving the projective transformation of the radius vector $\boldsymbol{r}\to\boldsymbol{R}=\boldsymbol{r}/(1+\boldsymbol{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{r})$ and the differential time scaling $\mathrm{d}t\to\mathrm{d}T=\mathrm{d}t/(1+\boldsymbol{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{r})^{2}$, where $\boldsymbol{c}$ is a real vector-valued parameter with dimensions of inverse length, constitutes a dynamical quasi-symmetry in the sense that it maps Newton's equation of motion $m(\mathrm{d}^{2}\boldsymbol{r}/\mathrm{d}t^{2})=α\boldsymbol{r}/r^{3}$ into the equation $m(\mathrm{d}^{2}\boldsymbol{R}/\mathrm{d}T^{2})=(α\boldsymbol{R}/R^{3}) \operatorname{sgn}(1-\boldsymbol{c}\cdot\boldsymbol{R})$. This quasi-symmetry is independent of, yet complementary to, the well-known Kepler-Coulomb dynamical symmetry, which is SO($N+1$) for negative energies and SO($N,1$) for positive energies. A detailed analytical and graphical analysis of induced mappings between non-degenerate conic trajectories associated with the original and transformed systems is presented. Mappings between velocity hodographs are also briefly discussed. It is then proved that the conservation of energy in the transformed motion implies the conservation of the Laplace-Runge-Lenz vector in the original dynamics.

</details>


### [79] [Alternative Hamiltonian and Lagrangian Approaches for Constrained Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04692)
*M. A. de Andrade,C. Neves,E. V. Corrêa Silva*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a generalized Hamiltonian and Lagrangian framework for constrained classical systems using a revised Barcelos-Wotzasek symplectic algorithm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard Hamiltonian and Lagrangian methods fail directly for constrained classical systems, necessitating a consistent and constructive alternative.

Method: Revised Barcelos-Wotzasek (BW) symplectic algorithm is employed to systematically derive generalized Lagrangian and Hamiltonian formulations for constrained systems.

Result: A consistent and successful construction of standard Lagrangian and Hamiltonian descriptions for constrained systems is achieved.

Conclusion: The revised BW symplectic algorithm provides a robust, systematic pathway to formulate both Lagrangian and Hamiltonian structures for constrained classical mechanical systems.

Abstract: While standard Hamiltonian and Lagrangian methods are powerful for unconstrained classical systems, they are not directly suited to constrained ones, despite the existence of well-established treatments in the literature. In this paper, we present an alternative approach to deriving a generalized Hamiltonian and Lagrangian framework, based on the revised Barcelos-Wotzasek (BW) symplectic algorithm. This method enables a consistent and successful construction of the standard Lagrangian and Hamiltonian descriptions for constrained systems.

</details>


### [80] [A bulk--edge correspondence for two-dimensional self-adjoint boundary and interface problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05616)
*Guillaume Bal*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bulk-edge correspondence for quantized asymmetric transport in 2D topological insulators with boundaries or interfaces, showing that the edge/interface current — defined via a trace-class observable — equals a combination of bulk invariants and spectral asymmetry terms (eta invariants) depending on the self-adjoint realization; it proves stability, index-theoretic interpretation (Fredholm index, spectral flow, Maslov index), and extends results to general interface transmission conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously define and quantify edge/interface transport in finite or semi-infinite topological insulators, where boundary termination necessitates a choice of self-adjoint extension — unlike infinite Euclidean settings — and to establish a robust bulk-edge correspondence that accounts for that choice.

Method: Uses functional analysis and index theory: (i) Boutet de Monvel's calculus for elliptic Shapiro–Lopatinski realizations to prove trace-class property and Fredholm index interpretation; (ii) Spectral analysis for translation-invariant edge geometries to define the invariant as spectral flow and Maslov index; (iii) Eta invariant analysis to relate edge physics to bulk topology and boundary conditions.

Result: The edge/interface current σ_I[H_L] is shown to be well-defined and quantized; its 2π-multiple ℐ is proven to be a stable Fredholm index (for elliptic realizations) and also a spectral flow/Maslov index (in translation-invariant case); the main bulk-edge formula expresses ℐ as the difference of bulk Chern numbers (ℛ − ℛ_L) plus eta-invariant corrections encoding boundary condition dependence.

Conclusion: Quantized transport at edges/interfaces of 2D topological insulators is intrinsically tied to the choice of self-adjoint realization, and the bulk-edge correspondence must incorporate spectral asymmetry (via eta invariants) to fully capture the effect of boundary termination; the invariant ℐ is robust, topologically meaningful, and generalizes to arbitrary interface transmission conditions.

Abstract: This paper analyzes the quantized asymmetric transport along the boundary of a two-dimensional topological insulator occupying a half plane, or along the interface separating two insulators. In contrast with problems posed on the Euclidean plane, terminating a sample requires a choice of self-adjointrealization, $H_L$, and we show that a quantized transport depends on that choice.
  The physical observable modeling the asymmetric transport is the edge, or interface, current $σ_I[H_L]=\Tr\,i[H_L,P]\varphi'(H_L)$. It is shown to be well defined along two routes. For Shapiro--Lopatinski elliptic realizations, we use estimates from Boutet de Monvel's calculus to show that the current observable is a well-defined trace, that $\mathcal I=2πσ_I[H_L]$ is a Fredholm index, and that it is stable under deformations and compactly supported changes of the operator and of its termination. For translation invariant operators along the edge, a spectral analysis defines $\mathcal I$ when the edge spectrum escapes a bulk gap at large momenta. $\mathcal I$ is then also a spectral flow and a Maslov index.
  Our main theorem is a bulk--edge correspondence \[
  \mathcal I=\cR-\cR_L+η\bigl(L_\infty,\Lm^Φ(+)\bigr)-η\bigl(L_\infty,\Lm^Φ(-)\bigr), \] We determine the range of $\mathcal I$. We extend the formula to interfaces with arbitrary transmission conditions. At the transparent condition, the invariant equals the bulk-difference invariant of the Euclidean plane. Eight examples illustrate the theoretical findings.

</details>


### [81] [On the signaling dimension of the cross-polytope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05924)
*Tamás Bencze,Péter E. \ Frenkel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes upper and lower bounds on the signaling dimension of high-dimensional cross-polytopes, extending prior exact results for dimensions 3–5.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the minimal classical communication resources needed to perfectly simulate quantum or generalized probabilistic communication protocols, via the notion of signaling dimension—specifically for cross-polytope state spaces.

Method: Analytical derivation of upper and lower bounds on the signaling dimension using geometric and information-theoretic arguments tailored to the structure of cross-polytopes in arbitrary finite dimension.

Result: New nontrivial upper and lower bounds for the signaling dimension of d-dimensional cross-polytopes for d > 5; previously known exact value (3) for d = 3, 4, 5 is recovered as a special case.

Conclusion: The signaling dimension of high-dimensional cross-polytopes grows sublinearly with dimension but remains strictly greater than 2; the bounds reveal a gap between achievable and necessary classical simulation complexity.

Abstract: In the general probabilistic model of information theory, the signaling dimension of a given communication protocol (or, equivalently, of its state space) is the smallest possible complexity of a classical communication protocol capable of perfectly simulating the given one in terms of input-output correlations.
  The signaling dimensions of three-, four-, and five-dimensional cross-polytopes have been known to be 3. We give an upper and a lower bound for signaling dimensions of higher dimensional cross-polytopes.

</details>


### [82] [Asymptotically free solutions of the scalar flow equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06165)
*Christoph Kopper*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates the existence of UV asymptotically free solutions to the renormalisation group flow equations for n-point Schwinger functions in 4D Euclidean scalar field theory, under nonpolynomial boundary conditions, satisfying key physical and mathematical constraints (Bose symmetry, Euclidean invariance, analyticity, and Osterwalder-Schrader growth).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous existence of physically admissible, UV asymptotically free quantum field theories beyond standard polynomial (e.g., φ⁴) interactions, by exploring nonpolynomial boundary conditions in the renormalisation group framework.

Method: Mathematical analysis of renormalisation group flow equations for Schwinger functions; imposition and verification of symmetry, analyticity, and axiomatic (Osterwalder-Schrader) conditions under nonpolynomial UV boundary data.

Result: Existence proof of UV asymptotically free, Bose-symmetric, Euclidean-invariant, analytic (in flow parameter and momenta), and Osterwalder-Schrader-compliant solutions to the RG flow for scalar field theory in four Euclidean dimensions.

Conclusion: Nonpolynomial boundary conditions admit well-behaved, physically consistent asymptotically free solutions in 4D scalar QFT, expanding the landscape of rigorously constructible quantum field theories beyond conventional perturbative or polynomial settings.

Abstract: We analyse solutions of the flow equations of the renormalisation group for the n-point Schwinger functions of scalar field theory in four-dimensional Euclidean space with nonpolynomial boundary conditions. We show that there exist UV asymptotically free solutions of this type which are Bose symmetric, invariant under the Euclidean group, analytic in the flow parameter and the momenta, and satisfy the Osterwalder-Schrader growth condition.

</details>


### [83] [Entropy Contraction and Hypercontractivity for Gaussian Quantum Markov Semigroups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06201)
*Zhengwei Liu,Jincheng Wan,Jinsong Wu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes finite-mode Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups with a faithful invariant Gaussian state, providing algebraic characterizations of the complete modified logarithmic Sobolev inequality (MLSI), deriving its optimal constant under Hurwitz drift convergence, establishing hypercontractivity for all $1<p<q<\infty$ at large times, showing failure of standard reverse hypercontractivity from $t=0$ but recovery for $1/2<p<q<1$ at large times, and revealing negativity of the $p$-logarithmic Sobolev constant for $0<p<1/2$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand functional inequalities—particularly logarithmic Sobolev and hypercontractivity properties—for Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups, which are fundamental in quantum information theory, open quantum systems, and quantum statistical mechanics; specifically, to extend classical results to the noncommutative Gaussian setting and clarify the role of drift dynamics and fixed-point structure.

Method: Algebraic analysis of Gaussian quantum dynamical semigroups using symplectic formalism and covariance matrix techniques; derivation of necessary and sufficient conditions for the complete modified logarithmic Sobolev inequality relative to the fixed-point algebra; spectral analysis of the drift generator $\mathbf{Z}$ (Hurwitz condition); use of interpolation and $L^p$-norm estimates to prove hypercontractivity and reverse hypercontractivity; explicit computation and sign analysis of $p$-logarithmic Sobolev constants via Gaussian integral formulas.

Result: 1) Algebraic characterization of complete MLSI relative to the fixed-point algebra; 2) Optimal MLSI constant obtained under Hurwitz convergence of $e^{t\mathbf{Z}}$; 3) Hypercontractivity holds for all $1<p<q<\infty$ at sufficiently large times; 4) Standard reverse hypercontractivity fails from $t=0$, but reverse hypercontractivity for $1/2<p<q<1$ holds asymptotically; 5) $p$-logarithmic Sobolev constant is negative for $0<p<1/2$, indicating breakdown of standard convexity-based bounds.

Conclusion: The functional inequality landscape for Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups differs markedly from classical or finite-dimensional quantum settings: the interplay between drift stability (Hurwitz property), fixed-point algebra structure, and $L^p$-geometry leads to novel phenomena—including asymptotic rather than instantaneous hypercontractivity, restricted reverse hypercontractivity domains, and negative $p$-Sobolev constants—highlighting the necessity of refined noncommutative analytic tools.

Abstract: In this paper, we study finite-mode Gaussian quantum Markov semigroups with a faithful invariant Gaussian state. We present an algebraic characterization of the complete modified logarithmic Sobolev inequality relative to the fixed-point algebra, and we obtain the corresponding optimal constant under the assumption that the drift evolution $e^{t\mathbf{Z}}$ converges as $t\to\infty$. We establish hypercontractivity for such quantum Markov semigroups with Hurwitz drift, in the sense that every fixed pair $1<p<q<\infty$ is attained at sufficiently large times. We find that the usual reverse hypercontractive curves with a positive rate from time zero can fail in general, but every fixed pair $1/2 < p < q < 1$ is attained at sufficiently large times under the Hurwitz assumption. We also systematically investigate the $p$-logarithmic Sobolev inequality and show that the $p$-logarithmic Sobolev constant can be negative for $0 < p < 1/2$.

</details>


### [84] [Quantum Fluctuations in Bose--Einstein Condensates:Microscopic Correlations and Moment Bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06367)
*Mauris Chueng,Keheng Zhu,Hexiang Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes higher-order quantum fluctuations and microscopic correlation energy in the 3D Gross–Pitaevskii regime, establishing uniform bounds on spectral moments of depletion and collective fluctuations, identifying a scaling limit of the excitation-energy measure at scale N², proving strict positivity of the dressed contact, and providing error estimates for cubic approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously understand higher-order quantum fluctuations and correlation effects beyond mean-field (Gross–Pitaevskii) description in dilute Bose gases, particularly propagation of spectral moments and structure of excitation-energy measures in the thermodynamic limit.

Method: Functional-analytic and spectral-theoretic methods applied to many-body Schrödinger dynamics: propagation of moments of the correlated energy operator, analysis of norm-convergent pure/mixed state families, spectral decomposition via relative scattering operator (-2Δ + v), and energy-form domain estimates for cubic approximations.

Result: Uniform-in-N bounds on third moment of depletion and sixth moments of one-particle fluctuations; identification of N²-scaled excitation-energy measure as a spectral measure of -2Δ+v weighted by time-dependent dressed contact; strict positivity of contact for finite-kinetic-energy, finite-mean-excitation references; lower bounds O(N^{2p−2}) on p-th excitation-energy moments for p > 1; O(N^{-3/16}) and O(N^{-5/4}) norm errors for cubic approximation on respective domains.

Conclusion: The Gross–Pitaevskii regime supports a well-defined hierarchy of quantum fluctuations governed by spectral properties of the relative scattering operator, with the dressed contact serving as a dynamically evolving, strictly positive universal coefficient linking microscopic correlations to macroscopic observables.

Abstract: We study higher moments of quantum fluctuations and microscopic correlation energy in the three-dimensional Gross--Pitaevskii regime. For a smooth, compactly supported, nonnegative radial interaction and an $N$-independent normalized initial condensate wave function in $H^6$, we propagate the third spectral moment of a positive correlated energy along the exact many-body evolution. A uniform initial bound controls the third moment of the depletion number and sixth moments of collective one-particle fluctuations on every compact time interval, independently of $N$. For a nonzero interaction and a smooth, compactly supported initial condensate, we also identify the excitation-energy measure at scale $N^2$, weighted by the first energy. This limit holds for norm-convergent pure-state families with a uniform bound on the initial third correlated-energy moment. Its positive-energy part is a spectral measure of the relative scattering operator $-2Δ+v$, multiplied by a time-dependent dressed contact. We compute the radial density and prove strict positivity of the contact for nonzero references with finite kinetic energy and finite mean excitation number. The same evolving states have bounded physical fluctuation moments and excitation-energy moments of order $p>1$ bounded below by a positive multiple of $N^{2p-2}$, in the extended spectral sense. Inverse powers of the positive initial energy construct admissible pure and mixed families. For the norm-convergent pure-state class, we determine the scaled interaction-energy moments for $1\le p<2$ and bound the second moment by $O(N^2)$. A finite cubic approximation has norm error $O(N^{-3/16})$ on the energy form domain and $O(N^{-5/4})$ on a stronger initial domain of its generator.

</details>


### [85] [Electrostatic inequalities and stability of matter on asymptotically Euclidean manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06624)
*David Stuart*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves stability of nonrelativistic fermionic matter with fixed nuclei on asymptotically Euclidean Riemannian manifolds, using Lieb-Thirring and novel electrostatic inequalities derived via Green-function–based Voronoi decomposition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend stability-of-matter results to curved, non-Euclidean geometries—specifically asymptotically Euclidean Riemannian manifolds—without requiring curvature sign constraints, thereby generalizing foundational quantum mechanical stability theorems beyond flat space.

Method: Adapts the Lieb-Thirring approach; introduces a curvature-adapted electrostatic inequality proved via a Voronoi-type decomposition rooted in Green functions $G(\cdot,R_j)$ associated with nuclei positions $R_j$, instead of Euclidean distance.

Result: Establishes thermodynamic stability (i.e., energy bounded below by $-C(N+K)$) for nonrelativistic fermionic matter interacting with fixed nuclei on asymptotically Euclidean manifolds under bounded sectional curvature assumptions—no positivity or negativity required on curvature.

Conclusion: Stability of matter holds robustly in geometrically nontrivial settings where only asymptotic flatness and curvature bounds are assumed, demonstrating that Green-function geometry suffices to replace Euclidean distance in electrostatic estimates.

Abstract: We prove stability of nonrelativistic fermionic matter in the presence of fixed nuclei \(\{R_j\}\) on an
  asymptotically Euclidean Riemannian manifold \((M,g)\).
  Upper and lower bounds on sectional curvatures are assumed, but
  no specific sign conditions on the curvature are imposed.
  The proof follows the route of Lieb-Thirring and electrostatic inequalities, the latter being
  proved by means of a Voronoi decomposition type argument which is however based on the Green functions \(\{G(\,\cdot\,,R_j)\}\) rather than the distance function.

</details>


### [86] [A Cohomological Characterization of the Clifford Hierarchy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06699)
*Junaid Aftab*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides a cohomological characterization of the Clifford hierarchy using quantum derivatives, showing they form non-abelian 1-cocycles; it decomposes the third level into symplectic, affine, and phase components, and proves semi-Cliffordness for all two- and three-qudit gates at that level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The higher levels of the Clifford hierarchy are poorly understood despite their importance in fault-tolerant quantum computation; prior work using quantum higher-order Fourier analysis lacked explicit level-wise descriptions.

Method: The authors establish a bijection between unitary operators and non-abelian 1-cocycles via quantum derivatives, yielding a recursive cohomological characterization; they then specialize to the third level and decompose the cocycle into symplectic, affine, and phase components with cohomological interpretations.

Result: A full recursive cohomological characterization of the Clifford hierarchy is obtained; for the third level, the decomposition enables new proofs that all two-qudit and—first-time—three-qudit gates are semi-Clifford.

Conclusion: Cohomology provides a powerful structural framework for understanding the Clifford hierarchy; the third-level decomposition resolves long-standing questions about semi-Cliffordness, extending known results from two to three qudits.

Abstract: The Clifford hierarchy plays a central role in fault-tolerant quantum computation, but its higher levels remain only partially understood. Recent work on quantum higher order Fourier analysis characterizes membership in the Clifford hierarchy using quantum derivatives, but does not provide an explicit description of the individual levels. In this work, we address this problem by showing that the collection of quantum derivatives of a unitary operator forms a non-abelian \(1\)-cocycle, and conversely that every such \(1\)-cocycle arises from a unitary operator. This yields a recursive cohomological characterization of the Clifford hierarchy. We then specialize this framework to the third level and decompose the cocycle data into symplectic, affine, and phase components, each admitting a cohomological interpretation. As applications, we give a new proof that every two-qudit gate in the third level of the Clifford hierarchy is semi-Clifford, and prove for the first time that every three-qudit gate in the third level is semi-Clifford.

</details>

# hep-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [87] [Status and Progress of Hamiltonian Effective Field Theory in Light Hadron Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06075)
*Qi-Liang Xie,Qiu-Rui Wu,Zi-Jun Li,Fang-Chao Han,Yu Zhuge,Zhan-Wei Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review presents Hamiltonian effective field theory (HEFT) as a unified framework to connect hadron resonance dynamics with both experimental scattering data and lattice QCD spectra, enabling discrimination between competing structural interpretations (e.g., bare quark states vs. multihadron components) in light-hadron spectroscopy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in hadron resonance structure—particularly the relative roles of quark-model 'bare' states versus dynamically generated multihadron configurations—by jointly constraining theoretical models with both experimental scattering data and first-principles lattice QCD results.

Method: Uses infinite- and finite-volume formulations of Hamiltonian effective field theory (HEFT), where a model-space Hamiltonian couples discrete bare states to continuous hadronic scattering channels; parameters are constrained by fitting to experimental phase shifts and/or lattice QCD energy-level spectra in finite volume.

Result: HEFT enables quantitative comparison of competing resonance interpretations that fit scattering data equally well but differ in underlying composition; recent extensions accommodate coupled-channel systems, inelastic thresholds, and multi-hadron final states.

Conclusion: HEFT provides a rigorous, unified, and computationally tractable bridge between QCD-informed calculations (lattice) and phenomenology (scattering experiments), enhancing interpretability of hadron resonances beyond what either approach achieves alone.
