Abstract: Hadron resonances are intrinsically dynamical states whose observable properties are governed by the interplay between quark-model configurations and hadronic channels. Hamiltonian effective field theory provides a practical framework for connecting hadron dynamics with experimental observables and lattice QCD results. In this review, we outline the infinite- and finite-volume formulations of Hamiltonian effective field theory and survey its applications to light-hadron spectroscopy and resonance structure. By constraining the same Hamiltonian framework with experimental scattering data and lattice QCD spectra, Hamiltonian effective field theory can help distinguish among scenarios that describe the available scattering data comparably well but imply different roles for bare-state and multihadron configurations. We also review recent extensions of the Hamiltonian framework developed to address more complicated dynamical settings.

</details>


### [88] [Phenomenological Estimate for the Longitudinal Unpolarized Structure Function in Semi-inclusive Deep Inelastic Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06628)
*Alessandro Bacchetta,Matteo Cerutti,Leonard Gamberg,Richard Whitehill*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a phenomenological study of the longitudinal unpolarized structure function $F_{UU,L}$ and its ratio $R_{\, \rm SIDIS}=F_{UU,L}/F_{UU,T}$ in semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS), combining low- and high-transverse-momentum contributions. It finds that $F_{UU,L}$, though power-suppressed at low $q_T$, yields a non-negligible $R_{\, \rm SIDIS} \simeq 0.3$, contributing ~10% corrections to transverse-momentum-integrated observables — urging inclusion in SIDIS multiplicity analyses and dedicated experimental measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the phenomenological relevance of the longitudinal unpolarized structure function $F_{UU,L}$ in SIDIS — particularly its often-neglected contribution at low transverse momentum — and to quantify its impact on integrated observables and multiplicity extractions.

Method: Phenomenological analysis combining low-$q_T$ (TMD factorization with next-to-next-to-leading-power corrections) and high-$q_T$ (collinear factorization) regimes for $F_{UU,L}$ and $F_{UU,T}$; computation of the ratio $R_{\, \rm SIDIS} = F_{UU,L}/F_{UU,T}$ across SIDIS kinematics.

Result: $R_{\, \rm SIDIS} \simeq 0.3$ at low transverse momentum and hard scale; $F_{UU,L}$ contributes ~10% corrections to transverse-momentum-integrated observables; the low-$q_T$ contribution is sizable despite power suppression, indicating sensitivity to NNLP dynamics.

Conclusion: The longitudinal structure function $F_{UU,L}$ cannot be neglected in precision SIDIS analyses; its inclusion is essential for accurate interpretation of multiplicities, and dedicated experimental measurements of $R_{\, \rm SIDIS}$ are strongly motivated to constrain theoretical uncertainties.

Abstract: We present a phenomenological study of the longitudinal unpolarized structure function $F_{UU,L}$ in semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS) and its ratio with the transverse structure function, $R_{\, \rm SIDIS}=F_{UU,L}/F_{UU,T}$. We study their transverse-momentum dependence, combining for the first time low- and high-transverse-momentum contributions across the kinematic range relevant to SIDIS measurements. At low transverse momentum, $F_{UU,L}$ is power suppressed, making it sensitive to next-to-next-to-leading-power dynamics in the unpolarized sector. We find that this contribution can be sizable, with $R_{\, \rm SIDIS}\simeq 0.3$ at low transverse momentum and hard scale, producing a correction of the order of $10\%$ to transverse-momentum-integrated observables. Although these estimates remain subject to theoretical uncertainties, our results indicate that accounting for $F_{UU,L}$ is likely to be important for the interpretation of SIDIS multiplicities and motivate dedicated measurements of $R_{\, \rm SIDIS}$ to better constrain the longitudinal contribution.

</details>


### [89] [Two-loop RI/SMOM-$\overline{\text{MS}}$ matching factors for the renormalization of bi-local operators contributing to $ε_K$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06734)
*Erik Lundstrum*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes two-loop QCD matching factors from the RI/SMOM scheme to the $\overline{\text{MS}}$ scheme, essential for renormalizing lattice QCD calculations of the long-distance contribution to indirect CP violation parameter $ε_K$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate first-principles Standard Model prediction of $ε_K$ requires non-perturbative evaluation of its long-distance $\\Delta S = 2$ contribution, which involves two $\\Delta S = 1$ operators; this necessitates precise renormalization via scheme matching in lattice QCD.

Method: Calculation of two-loop $O(\\alpha_s)$ renormalization group invariant (RI/SMOM) to $\\overline{\\text{MS}}$ matching factors for the relevant four-quark operators using continuum perturbative QCD.

Result: Complete set of two-loop RI/SMOM–$\\overline{\\text{MS}}$ matching factors for the operators contributing to the long-distance $ε_K$ amplitude.

Conclusion: The computed matching factors enable improved precision in lattice QCD determinations of the long-distance contribution to $ε_K$, reducing a key systematic uncertainty in testing the Standard Model via kaon CP violation.

Abstract: The measure of indirect CP violation in the neutral kaon system, $ε_K$, is an important precision test of the Standard Model. A first-principles Standard Model prediction of $ε_K$ requires evaluating the long-distance contribution in which the $ΔS = 2$ process proceeds via two $ΔS = 1$ operators. The non-perturbative nature of QCD in the relevant energy regimes requires a lattice QCD simulation. In this paper we present the two-loop, $O(α_s)$ RI/SMOM - $\overline{\text{MS}}$ matching factors required to renormalize a lattice QCD calculation of the long-distance contribution to $ε_K$.

</details>


### [90] [An optimal observable for polarization measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03855)
*Erik Bachmann,Mareen Hoppe*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces parton-level differential polarization fractions as optimal observables for measuring vector-boson polarization, enabling maximal sensitivity to new physics in the electroweak sector; it demonstrates their theoretical optimality, experimental accessibility via neural-network-based inference, and model-independent probing power using inclusive ZZ production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vector-boson polarization is a sensitive probe of new physics in the electroweak sector, but rare processes and suppressed longitudinal contributions necessitate optimal polarization separation to fully exploit limited data.

Method: The authors define parton-level differential polarization fractions as optimal observables, prove that their conditional expectations retain all experimentally accessible polarization information (achieving the fundamental sensitivity limit), and propose learning them via neural networks for unbinned or binned inference; they validate the method in inclusive ZZ production.

Result: The proposed observables achieve the best possible sensitivity within the assumed model, combine polarization resolution with the model independence of fiducial cross sections, and enable detection of deviations beyond simple rescalings of polarized components.

Conclusion: Parton-level differential polarization fractions constitute a theoretically optimal, experimentally realizable, and broadly applicable framework for high-precision electroweak polarization measurements, significantly enhancing sensitivity to beyond-Standard-Model physics.

Abstract: Vector-boson polarization provides a sensitive probe of new physics in the electroweak sector. Rare processes and suppressed longitudinal contributions make optimal polarization separation essential to exploit the available data. We show that parton-level differential polarization fractions define optimal observables and a fundamental sensitivity limit for polarized cross-section measurements. Their conditional expectations retain all experimentally accessible polarization information, yielding the best achievable sensitivity within the assumed model, and can be learned by neural networks for unbinned or binned inference. Measuring their distributions combines polarization sensitivity with the model independence of fiducial cross sections, probing deviations beyond constant rescalings of polarized contributions. We demonstrate the approach in inclusive ZZ production.

</details>


### [91] [No Einstein Relation, No Problem: How Relativistic Weakly Coupled Heavy Quarks Equilibrate Beyond Leading Logarithm Anyway](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03863)
*Jean F. Du Plessis,Bruno Scheihing-Hitschfeld*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how relativistic heavy quarks equilibrate in both weakly and strongly coupled plasmas, revealing that non-Gaussian momentum-transfer kernels—characterized by exponential tails and bounded analytic domains—play a decisive role; while drag dominates survivor bias at very weak coupling, fluctuations induce significant enhancement at strong coupling, yet both regimes share qualitative structural features enabling unified phenomenological modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how relativistic heavy quarks equilibrate despite violation of the Einstein relation between drag and diffusion, and to bridge microscopic field-theoretic dynamics with macroscopic transport behavior across coupling regimes.

Method: Computation of the momentum-transfer kernel at strict O(g⁴) in weakly coupled non-Abelian plasma; analysis of its analytic structure, cumulant asymptotics, and tail behavior; comparison with strongly coupled N=4 SYM using holographic results; leading-order expansion in inverse heavy-quark mass; dynamical evaluation of equilibration with Fokker–Planck benchmarks.

Result: The momentum-transfer kernel is non-Gaussian beyond leading logarithm, featuring asymmetric exponential tails fixed by its bounded analytic domain; N=4 SYM exhibits similar Gaussian core + exponential tails but with different singularity structure and velocity-dependent transport coefficients; drag alone explains relativistic population persistence at very weak coupling, whereas fluctuation-induced survivor bias strongly enhances it at strong coupling.

Conclusion: Despite quantitative differences in analytic structure and velocity dependence between weak and strong coupling, the shared qualitative features—Gaussian core, exponential tails, and kernel-driven survivor bias—support a unified, first-principles–anchored phenomenological framework for heavy-quark energy loss and equilibration in QCD-like plasmas.

Abstract: Relativistic heavy quarks equilibrate even when their microscopic drag and momentum diffusion violate the Einstein relation. We investigate how equilibration proceeds as a function of the coupling and compared to simplified Fokker-Planck models. To do this, we present a detailed calculation of the momentum-transfer kernel in weakly coupled non-Abelian plasmas through strict $\mathcal{O}(g^4)$, and study the resulting dynamics at leading order in the inverse heavy-quark mass. Beyond leading logarithm, its non-Gaussian structure plays a crucial role in equilibration. We examine closely the analytic structure of the evolution kernel, determine the large-order asymptotics of the momentum-transfer cumulants, and show how the kernel's bounded analytic domain fixes asymmetric exponential tails at the level of the momentum transfer probability. Strongly coupled $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM also features an approximately Gaussian core and exponential tails, but differs in the nature of the kernel's singularities and the velocity dependence of the transport coefficients and tail exponents. These differences have distinct dynamical consequences: for the parameters studied, the persistence of the relativistic population in steeply falling spectra is largely explained by drag alone at very weak coupling, whereas fluctuation-induced survivor bias produces a large relative enhancement at strong coupling. Nevertheless, the shared qualitative structure between weak and strong coupling points the way towards a practical phenomenological description of heavy quarks anchored in first-principles field theory.

</details>


### [92] [A Powheg generator for $t$- and $s$-channel single-top production and decay including non-resonant effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03878)
*Melissa van Beekveld,Robin van Bijleveld,Silvia Ferrario Ravasio,Tomas Jezo,Tommaso Saracco*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a NLO+PS generator for single-top production (t- and s-channel) in hadron colliders, implemented in POWHEG BOX RES, modeling $pp \to \ell^\pm \nu j_b j$ with full off-shell and non-resonant effects, matched to Pythia via a resonance-aware interface, and incorporating b-quark mass effects through a 5-flavor scheme.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a precise, publicly available Monte Carlo generator for single-top production that consistently includes off-shell and non-resonant contributions, properly handles decaying top quarks in parton shower matching, and retains bottom-quark mass effects in final-state jets—addressing limitations of existing approximations.

Method: Implemented a NLO QCD calculation interfaced with parton shower using the POWHEG BOX RES framework; employed a five-flavor scheme with explicit bottom-quark mass treatment in final-state jets; developed a custom Pythia interface accommodating top-quark decays and resonance-aware matching; validated with fiducial-phase-space cuts and shower effect studies.

Result: A fully functional, public NLO+PS generator for t- and s-channel single-top production with leptonic top decay, correctly describing $pp \to \ell^\pm \nu j_b j$, including off-shellness, interference, and b-jet mass effects; demonstrated robust shower matching and quantified impact of parton shower on kinematic distributions.

Conclusion: The generator achieves a consistent, high-precision description of single-top production with realistic experimental signatures, enabling improved theoretical predictions for LHC analyses—particularly where off-shell effects, b-jet definition, or resonance matching are critical.

Abstract: We present a next-to-leading order + parton shower generator for $t$- and $s$-channel single-top production in hadron colliders, for a leptonically-decaying top, including off-shell and non-resonant effects. The generator, publicly available in the POWHEG BOX RES framework, describes the hadro-production of a $b$-jet and a leptonically decaying $W$-boson, accompanied by an additional jet, i.e. $pp\to \ell^{+} ν_{\ell}\, j_b\, j $ and $pp\to \ell^{-} \barν_{\ell}\, j_{\bar{b}}\, j $, where $j_b$ ($j_{\bar{b}}$) is a jet containing a bottom (anti)quark, and $j$ is a generic jet. We also provide an interface to the Pythia parton shower, suited to matching in the presence of decaying resonances such as the top quark. Our matching is designed to combine the advantages of a five-flavour calculation with the description of bottom quark mass effects in final-state jets. We perform a phenomenological study using fiducial cuts and discuss shower effects.

</details>


### [93] [Reinterpreting ATLAS Dijet and Supersymmetry Searches in A Baryon-Number-Violating Heavy Scalar Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03954)
*Tianyi Yang,Denis Rathjens,Alfredo Gurrola,Francesco Romeo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets ATLAS 13 TeV $b$-tagged dijet and monotop-like searches to constrain a baryon-number-violating two-scalar-mediator model ($X_1$, $X_2$) addressing both baryogenesis and dark matter; it sets 95% CL limits in the $λ_1$–$λ_2$ coupling plane for mediator masses 1.5–2.0 TeV, excluding $λ_1 > 0.45$ (dijet) and $λ_1 > 0.3$, $λ_2 > 0.6$ (monotop-like).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the baryon asymmetry of the universe and dark matter relic abundance via a baryon-number-violating model with two scalar mediators, and to improve constraints using flavor-specific (b-tagged) and jet-rich signatures beyond inclusive dijet or standard monotop analyses.

Method: Reinterpretation of ATLAS searches for $b$-tagged dijet resonances and monotop-alike signatures using 139 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV $pp$ data; mapping experimental upper limits on signal yields onto the $λ_1$–$λ_2$ coupling plane for benchmark mediator masses (1.5, 1.75, 2.0 TeV), assuming decays to down-type quark pairs or top + DM.

Result: 95% CL exclusion limits derived: $b$-tagged dijet analysis excludes $λ_1 > 0.45$ across all three benchmark masses; bottom-squark–inspired monotop reinterpretation excludes $λ_1 > 0.3$ and $λ_2 > 0.6$ within validated parameter space.

Conclusion: Flavor-tagged and jet-rich search reinterpretations provide complementary and improved sensitivity to baryon-number-violating scalar mediator models compared to inclusive approaches, tightening constraints on couplings relevant for baryogenesis and dark matter.

Abstract: A reinterpretation of ATLAS searches targeting $b$-tagged dijet resonances and monotop-alike signatures at a center-of-mass energy of 13 TeV is presented using $pp$ collision data corresponding to integrated luminosities of 139 $\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$. These searches are used to constrain a baryon-number-violating new physics model that introduces two scalar mediators $X_{1}$ and $X_{2}$ to simultaneously address the baryon asymmetry of the universe and the dark matter relic abundance. By exploiting heavy-flavor signatures and allowing for jet-rich final states, this reinterpretation provides complementary sensitivity to the model relative to non-flavor-tagged inclusive dijet searches and conventional monotop reinterpretations. Assuming the mediators decay either into a pair of down-type quarks or into a top quark and a dark matter candidate, we translate the experimental upper limits into the model's parameter space. 95% confidence level exclusion limits are derived in the $λ_1$-$λ_2$ coupling plane for benchmark mediator masses of 1.5, 1.75, and 2.0 TeV. For all three benchmark masses, the $b$-tagged dijet reinterpretation excludes $λ_1 > 0.45$, while the bottom-squark reinterpretation excludes the region with $λ_1 > 0.3$ and $λ_2 > 0.6$, within the respective validated parameter domains.

</details>


### [94] [Dijet acoplanarity as a function of flattenicity in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04097)
*Jesus Eduardo Muñoz Mendez,Antonio Ortiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates event classification biases in LHC pp collision analyses searching for QGP signatures; it finds multiplicity-based estimators introduce spurious azimuthal broadening mimicking jet quenching, whereas flattenicity—a geometry-based classifier—avoids such biases and thus offers a more robust tool for QGP searches in small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the ambiguity surrounding putative QGP signatures in pp collisions, specifically whether observed effects (e.g., azimuthal broadening) stem from genuine QGP formation or artificial biases introduced by multiplicity-based event selection.

Method: Comparative analysis of event classifiers—including multiplicity-based estimators and flattenicity—applied to PYTHIA 8 simulated pp collisions at √s = 13 TeV; evaluation via dijet acoplanarity and azimuthal correlations.

Result: Multiplicity-based classifiers induce significant azimuthal broadening even in absence of jet quenching (i.e., in PYTHIA 8), while flattenicity-triggered events show no such effect—indicating elimination of selection bias.

Conclusion: Flattenicity is a bias-resistant, geometry-driven event classifier that enables cleaner tests for jet quenching and QGP formation in small systems, warranting its adoption in LHC analyses.

Abstract: One of the most important discoveries at the LHC is the presence of effects associated with quark-gluon plasma (QGP) formation in proton-proton (pp) collisions. The origin of these observations remains unknown. Since event selection in many studies relies on multiplicity-based estimators known to introduce selection biases, alternative approaches must be explored to elucidate the origin of these phenomena.
  This contribution presents a comparison of different event classifiers used in LHC experiments, aiming to assess their mutual compatibility. Results for pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV simulated with PYTHIA 8 indicate that the different classifiers introduce biases that can obscure any evidence of QGP formation in small systems. However, these biases are significantly reduced or absent in events triggered with flattenicity. This finding motivates the application of flattenicity in dijet acoplanarity studies used to search for jet quenching effects. While multiplicity-based estimators produce an azimuthal broadening in high-multiplicity compared with minimum-bias pp collisions, even in PYTHIA 8 that does not incorporate jet quenching, events triggered with flattenicity show no such broadening. This suggests that this novel tool could be exploited by LHC experiments to refine jet-quenching searches in small systems.

</details>


### [95] [Energy shift and decay width of kaonium from coupled-channel one-loop chiral amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04120)
*Alireza Beygi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the energy shift and decay width of kaonium (K+K− Coulomb-bound atom) using two coupled-channel one-loop chiral amplitudes, finding larger ground-state widths (1.67 and 0.92 keV) than prior predictions; the width depends sensitively on both real and imaginary parts of the K+K− scattering amplitude near threshold, though s-wave level spacing is preserved and experimental line shapes remain unaffected by the increased width when accounting for Belle’s resolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies in theoretical predictions of kaonium's decay width and to assess the sensitivity of the width to the real and imaginary parts of the K+K− strong-interaction amplitude near threshold, especially given conflicting signs of scattering length real parts in different chiral models.

Method: Two fitted coupled-channel one-loop chiral perturbation theory amplitudes are employed to compute the strong-interaction energy shift and decay width of the kaonium ground state; Coulomb corrections are included self-consistently within the Deser-type formalism; s-wave level spacing and photon-fusion line shapes (including Belle detector resolution) are analyzed.

Result: Ground-state widths of 1.67 keV and 0.92 keV are obtained—both significantly larger than the 0.30 keV prediction from leading-order non-perturbative χPT; the two amplitudes yield scattering lengths with nearly equal Im a but opposite-sign Re a, causing opposing Coulomb corrections; s-wave level separation is maintained; photon-fusion line shape is robust against the increased intrinsic width when convoluted with Belle’s experimental resolution.

Conclusion: Kaonium’s decay width is highly sensitive to the full complex structure of the low-energy K+K− amplitude—not just its absorptive part—and modern one-loop chiral amplitudes predict substantially broader widths than earlier estimates, yet remain consistent with observable spectroscopic and production features in experiment.

Abstract: Using two fitted coupled-channel one-loop chiral amplitudes, we investigate the strong-interaction energy shift and decay width of the hypothetical Coulomb-bound $K^+ K^-$ hadronic atom, kaonium. We obtain ground-state widths of 1.67 and 0.92 keV, which are both larger than previous kaonium predictions, including the 0.30 keV result obtained from a non-perturbative treatment based on leading-order chiral perturbation theory. Although the $K^+ K^-$ scattering lengths obtained from the two one-loop amplitudes have nearly equal imaginary parts, their real parts have opposite signs; thus, the leading absorptive contributions to the widths are similar, while the Coulomb corrections act in opposite directions. The kaonium width therefore depends on both the real and imaginary parts of the near-threshold strong amplitude. Despite the increased ground-state width, the first three $s$-wave levels remain separated. In photon fusion, at fixed pole area and resonance position, the increase in the intrinsic width has a negligible effect on the line shape after including Belle's experimental resolution.

</details>


### [96] [Spontaneous Spin Polarization in Dense Quark Matter Reexamined](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04160)
*Carlisson Miller,Rodolfo Casana,Manoel M. Ferreira,Gastão Krein*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the quark spin-polarized (QSP) phase in cold, dense quark matter using the two-flavor NJL model with tensor interactions, comparing two regularization schemes—TRS and MSS—and finds that MSS yields physically robust, causal, monotonic behavior with growing spin polarization at high density, impacting neutron star structure and magnetism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the phase structure of high-baryon-density QCD matter, particularly the stability and physical viability of the quark spin-polarized (QSP) phase, and its implications for neutron star interiors and magnetic fields.

Method: Uses the two-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model augmented with tensor interactions; compares two regularization prescriptions—traditional sharp-cutoff scheme (TRS) versus medium separation scheme (MSS)—to isolate vacuum divergences and treat medium phase space rigorously; analyzes thermodynamics, causality, magnetization, and constructs hybrid neutron stars.

Result: TRS yields nonmonotonic QSP condensate evolution, thermodynamic instabilities, and potential causality violations at high chemical potential; MSS yields monotonic QSP growth, causal equation of state, increasing spontaneous magnetization, and qualitatively distinct hybrid-star mass-radius relations and stability properties.

Conclusion: The MSS regularization is physically preferred for dense quark matter; a persistently spin-polarized quark phase is viable at high density and may contribute microscopically to strong magnetic fields in compact stars, notably magnetars.

Abstract: The phase structure of strongly interacting matter at high baryon density remains an open problem in QCD, with important implications for the internal structure and magnetic properties of neutron stars. We reexamine the emergence of a quark spin-polarized (QSP) phase in cold, dense quark matter within the two-flavor Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model with tensor interactions, focusing on its dependence on regularization. We compare the traditional regularization scheme (TRS), where a sharp momentum cutoff is applied to both vacuum and medium contributions, with the medium separation scheme (MSS), where ultraviolet-divergent vacuum terms are isolated while finite medium-dependent phase-space contributions remain cutoff free. The two prescriptions lead to qualitatively different results. In TRS, the QSP condensate eventually collapses at high chemical potential, leading to nonmonotonic thermodynamic behavior and, for some couplings, violations of causality. In MSS, the QSP condensate instead increases monotonically with density, the equation of state remains causal, and the associated spontaneous magnetization grows toward high density. The persistence of spin polarization at high density may provide a microscopic contribution to the strong magnetic fields of compact stars, including magnetars. An exploratory hybrid-star construction further shows that the QSP phase can significantly modify the equation of state, stability trends, and mass-radius relations. These results highlight the importance of a consistent treatment of medium phase space and demonstrate the potential impact of a persistent spin-polarized quark phase on neutron-star physics.

</details>


### [97] [A Composite Three-Body-Holonomy Toy Framework for Dynamical Electroweak-Scale Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04202)
*Tadashi Yoshikawa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a three-state hidden sector model where the number three emerges from a fundamental three-body holonomy in a relativistic (1+1)D Dirac system—not from imposed gauge symmetry—and manifests as a composite SU(3)_H gauge theory; the resulting cyclic condensate and holonomy-dependent cubic determinant interaction generate a composite scale and induce electroweak symmetry breaking via a CP-even portal coupling between the radial excitation S₃ and the Higgs doublet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of the number three in particle physics (e.g., three generations, three colors) not as an ad hoc gauge choice but as a geometric consequence of a primitive three-body holonomy in a lower-dimensional relativistic system, and to dynamically generate the electroweak scale via scale-invariant hidden-sector dynamics.

Method: Constructs a phenomenological toy model based on a (3+1)D composite strong dynamics with an asymptotically free hidden SU(3)_H gauge theory and three SM-singlet Dirac flavors; incorporates anomalous cubic determinant interaction arising from the inherited three-body holonomy; uses dimensional transmutation and holonomy-dependent potential to generate composite scale and trigger EWSB via Higgs–S₃ portal.

Result: A self-consistent mechanism where the threefold structure arises geometrically, leading to a composite scalar S₃ that couples to the Higgs through a holonomy-dependent CP-even portal; this coupling shifts the Higgs mass term and triggers electroweak symmetry breaking, with the electroweak scale dynamically generated from the hidden-sector compositeness scale in a classically scale-invariant framework.

Conclusion: The number three can emerge topologically from a fundamental three-body holonomy rather than being imposed by gauge symmetry; such emergence naturally leads to a minimal three-state internal sector capable of generating the electroweak scale dynamically without fine-tuning, offering a novel geometric origin for flavor and color structure.

Abstract: We propose a phenomenological toy framework promoted to a minimal three-state internal sector in $(3+1)$ dimensions and realized by composite strong dynamics. It is in a genuine three-body holonomy which originates in a relativistic $(1+1)$-dimensional Dirac system. The hypothesis is that the number three is not imposed first as a gauge symmetry, but is inherited from the primitive closed cyclic structure of the three-body holonomy. This threefold structure subsequently reappears as a three-state internal space, the three-site loop, a three-flavor hidden sector, and a cubic determinant interaction.
  We introduce an asymptotically free hidden $SU(3)_H$ gauge theory with three Standard-Model-singlet Dirac flavors. Strong hidden dynamics generates a composite condensate, while the inherited holonomy selects its cyclic internal orientation. The anomalous cubic determinant interaction produces a holonomy-dependent potential, and dimensional transmutation generates the characteristic composite scale.
  The radial composite excitation along the cyclic direction, denoted by $S_3$, couples to the Standard-Model Higgs doublet through a holonomy-dependent CP-even portal. The resulting condensate shifts the Higgs quadratic term and can trigger electroweak symmetry breaking. In the classically scale-invariant reference limit, the electroweak scale is induced from the dynamically generated hidden-sector scale through dimensionless portal couplings.

</details>


### [98] [Pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone Boson Motion from Thermal Thresholds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04265)
*Mussawir Khan,Ali Muhammad,Tianjun Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel finite-temperature mechanism for coherent motion of pseudo-Nambu–Goldstone bosons (pNGBs), where differential thermal evolution of misaligned symmetry-breaking sources rotates the effective vacuum direction—inducing field motion even from an initially stationary minimum; demonstrated in the Majoron sector of the Type-I seesaw, with implications for spontaneous leptogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain coherent pNGB motion without invoking ad hoc initial displacement or velocity, and to provide a microscopic, temperature-driven origin rooted in thermal nonequilibrium among multiple symmetry-breaking sectors.

Method: Analytical finite-temperature field theory applied to a Majoron–heavy neutrino system in the Type-I seesaw; computation of thermally corrected effective potential, tracking of time-dependent vacuum direction θ_T(t), and numerical integration of the pNGB equation of motion under Hubble-damped expansion.

Result: Thermal rotation of vacuum direction by |Δθ_T| ≈ 0.76 rad induces peak pNGB velocity |θ̇|/H ≈ 0.62; field remains adiabatically aligned with moving minimum to ~1 mrad accuracy.

Conclusion: Differential thermal decoupling of non-aligned symmetry-breaking sources provides a robust, first-principles mechanism for pNGB dynamics, generalizable beyond Majorons to any multi-source symmetry-breaking scenario with hierarchical thermal thresholds.

Abstract: Coherent motion of light scalar fields in the early Universe is typically attributed to an initial displacement, a primordial velocity, or a prescribed temperature-dependent potential. We identify a distinct microscopic mechanism in which differential thermal evolution of multiple non-aligned symmetry-breaking sources rotates the effective vacuum direction of a pseudo-Nambu--Goldstone boson (pNGB), driving motion from an initially stationary minimum. The mechanism is realized in the Majoron sector of a Type-I seesaw mechanism, where heavy neutrinos with separated thermal thresholds supply the required differential thermal evolution. For a representative benchmark, the thermal source direction rotates by $|Δθ_T|\simeq0.76$~rad and drives the Majoron to a peak velocity $|\dotθ|/H\simeq0.62$, while the field tracks the moving minimum to sub-milliradian accuracy. This establishes a microscopic finite-temperature origin for pNGB motion, with implications for spontaneous leptogenesis and, more generally, for any system with multiple non-aligned symmetry-breaking sources that evolve differently with temperature.

</details>


### [99] [Amplitude-level NLO predictions for exclusive $J/ψ+γ$ production in ultraperipheral collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04293)
*Xi-Jie Zhan,Tai-Fu Feng,Xing-Gang Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines exclusive γγ → J/ψ + γ production in the color-singlet channel at leading order in heavy-quark velocity, highlighting inconsistencies in the conventional NLO treatment (B+V) — which can yield unphysical negative differential cross sections — and advocating inclusion of the full one-loop squared term (Q) to ensure positivity and improve theoretical reliability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve unphysical negativity in the conventional NLO differential cross section for exclusive γγ → J/ψ + γ, arising from truncating the one-loop contribution to interference only (B+V), and to assess the impact of retaining the complete one-loop square (Q) on positivity, scale dependence, and phenomenological predictions.

Method: Computes the partonic cross section for γγ → J/ψ + γ in the color-singlet NRQCD framework at leading order in v (heavy-quark velocity); compares two NLO treatments: (i) standard B+V (Born plus renormalized virtual interference), and (ii) B+V+Q (including the full finite one-loop squared amplitude Q); evaluates differential spectra, total cross sections, and integrated ultraperipheral cross sections in pp and PbPb collisions, varying renormalization scale μ_R over {W/2, W, 2W}.

Result: The conventional B+V yields negative dσ/dp_T at high p_T for W = 80 and 160 GeV even at μ_R = W; B+V+Q preserves positivity. Total partonic cross section increases beyond Born at higher W due to positive virtual contributions from other scattering directions. In pp (14 TeV) and PbPb (5.52 TeV) ultraperipheral collisions, Q enhances central integrated rates by 11.6%–18.4% and reduces scale uncertainty (μ_R variation) by 36%–67%. Differential distributions are quantitatively and qualitatively altered by Q.

Conclusion: Including the complete one-loop squared term Q is essential for physical consistency (positivity), improved perturbative stability (reduced scale dependence), and accurate prediction of both integrated rates and kinematic shapes in exclusive J/ψ+γ photoproduction. The conventional NLO prescription B+V is insufficient for differential observables in this process.

Abstract: Exclusive $γγ\to J/ψ+γ$ production is studied in the color-singlet channel at leading order in the heavy-quark velocity. The conventional next-to-leading-order (NLO) result $B+V$, comprising Born production and its interference with the renormalized one-loop amplitude, is compared with $B+V+Q$, where $Q$ retains the complete one-loop square. At photon-photon energies $W=80~\mathrm{GeV}$ and $160~\mathrm{GeV}$, the conventional spectrum becomes negative over finite high-$p_T$ intervals already at renormalization scale $μ_R=W$, whereas the complete square remains non-negative. In contrast, the total partonic cross section exceeds Born at the larger energies in the energy scan, owing to positive virtual contributions from other scattering directions. For $p$-$p$ collisions at $14~\mathrm{TeV}$ and $Pb$-$Pb$ collisions at $\sqrt{s_{NN}}=5.52~\mathrm{TeV}$, $Q$ raises the central integrated ultraperipheral rates by $11.6\%$--$18.4\%$. Their absolute scale widths over $μ_R=\{W/2,W,2W\}$ decrease by $36\%$--$67\%$. Retaining the complete one-loop square changes both the predicted integrated rates and the shapes of the differential distributions.

</details>


### [100] [Angular Observables in the $Λ_{b}\to Λν\barν$ Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04309)
*Wolfgang Altmannshofer,Shibasis Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the rare baryonic decay Λ_b → Λ(→ pπ⁻)νν̄ with polarized Λ_b at the Z pole, identifying five angular observables—four requiring polarization—that probe chiral and CP-violating structures of b → s νν̄ transitions beyond what mesonic decays can access; it provides SM predictions using lattice QCD form factors and assesses FCC-ee sensitivity to new physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve parameter degeneracies and fully characterize the chiral (C_L, C_R) and CP-violating structure of new physics in b → s νν̄ transitions, which are inaccessible in conventional mesonic decays (e.g., B → K(*)νν̄, B_s → φνν̄).

Method: Analytical derivation of the differential decay distribution for polarized Λ_b → Λ(→ pπ⁻)νν̄; identification and classification of five independent angular observables—including a T-odd CP asymmetry A_5; use of state-of-the-art lattice QCD form factors for SM predictions; projection of new physics sensitivity at FCC-ee.

Result: Four of the five angular observables require initial Λ_b polarization; A_5 is a T-odd CP asymmetry directly sensitive to Im(C_L C_R*); the observables provide access to |C_R|² − |C_L|² and Im(C_L C_R*), orthogonal to |C_L ± C_R| probed in mesonic modes; SM branching ratio and all angular distributions are predicted; FCC-ee offers high sensitivity to chiral and CP-violating NP scenarios.

Conclusion: Baryonic angular observables in Λ_b → Λνν̄ are indispensable for breaking degeneracies among Wilson coefficients and for fully mapping the chiral and CP structure of new physics in b → s νν̄ transitions—complementing and extending the reach of mesonic probes.

Abstract: We study the rare decay $Λ_b \rightarrow Λ(\rightarrow pπ^-)ν\barν$ of polarized $Λ_b$ baryons produced at the $Z$ pole. We find that the differential decay distribution is characterized by five independent angular observables, four of which require initial $Λ_b$ polarization. While standard observables in mesonic $b \rightarrow s ν\barν$ decays ($B \rightarrow K^{(*)} ν\barν$, $B_s \rightarrow φν\barν$) probe only two combinations of short-distance Wilson coefficients, $|C_L + C_R|$ and $|C_L - C_R|$, the angular observables in $Λ_b \rightarrow Λ(\rightarrow pπ^-)ν\barν$ provide access to the orthogonal combinations $|C_R|^2 - |C_L|^2$ and $\text{Im}(C_L C_R^*)$. In particular, we identify a $T$-odd CP asymmetry, $A_5$, that directly probes CP-violating phases in the short-distance effective Hamiltonian. We present Standard Model predictions for the branching ratio and all angular observables using lattice QCD form factors, and evaluate the projected new physics sensitivity at the FCC-ee. Our results demonstrate that baryonic angular observables are essential for resolving parameter degeneracies and for completely characterizing the chiral and CP structure of possible new physics in $b \rightarrow s ν\barν$ transitions.

</details>


### [101] [Chiral Vortical Transport under Minimal-Length Deformations in Rotating Relativistic Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04324)
*Sameer Ahmad Mir,Behnam Pourhassan,Nawal H. Siddig,Euaggelos E. Zotos,Laila Abdulaziz Al-Essa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how a minimal length scale modifies the chiral vortical effect (CVE) — specifically, how isotropic modifications to the momentum-space density of states (DoS) affect the vortical conductivity. It finds that leading-order CVE contributions (from chemical potential and temperature) remain unchanged at first order in DoS deformation; minimal-length effects enter only via higher-order thermal and chemical-potential terms, some tied to anomaly structures and others novel. For the Kempf-Mangano-Mann deformation, a positive minimal-length parameter suppresses the DoS contribution, and Planck-scale corrections are negligible under heavy-ion freeze-out conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether quantum-gravitational minimal-length effects—encoded via modified density-of-states—alter the chiral vortical effect beyond known anomaly-driven responses, particularly in contexts like heavy-ion collisions where vorticity and chirality play key roles.

Method: Analytic computation of vortical conductivity from static, long-wavelength current–momentum density correlation; starts with a single right-handed Weyl fermion, then generalizes to massless chiral systems with commuting conserved charges; considers isotropic, analytic DoS deformations including the Kempf-Mangano-Mann model.

Result: Leading CVE terms (linear in μ and T²) are robust against first-order DoS deformation; minimal-length effects appear only in higher-order (e.g., μ³, μT², T⁴) contributions; some higher-order coefficients match anomaly-related charge combinations (gauge/mixed gravitational), others are independent; Kempf-Mangano-Mann deformation yields monotonic suppression of DoS contribution; Planck-suppressed correction is ~10⁻¹⁰ under typical heavy-ion freeze-out conditions.

Conclusion: Isotropic minimal-length modifications do not alter the leading anomaly-controlled chiral vortical response but introduce new, deformation-dependent higher-order transport contributions—indicating that quantum-gravitational corrections to CVE are subleading and model-sensitive, yet potentially distinguishable in precision chiral transport studies.

Abstract: We study how a minimal length can affect the chiral vortical effect, in which rotation produces a current in matter made of chiral fermions. We focus on an isotropic modification of the momentum-space density-of-state (DoS) and determine the vortical conductivity from the static, long-wavelength correlation between the current and momentum density. The analysis is carried out first for a single right-handed Weyl fermion and is then extended to general massless chiral fermion systems with mutually commuting conserved charges. We find that, for a broad class of analytic isotropic DoS modifications, the familiar leading contributions from chemical potential and temperature remain unchanged at first order within the DoS sector. Minimal-length effects instead appear through higher-order thermal and chemical-potential contributions. Some of these corrections contain the same chiral charge combinations that appear in gauge and mixed gauge-gravitational anomalies, while another contains an independent higher-order charge combination. Their coefficients depend on the form of the DoS modification and are therefore not fixed by the usual four-dimensional anomaly structure. For the Kempf-Mangano-Mann deformation, the exact result shows that a positive minimal-length parameter always reduces the DoS contribution to the vortical conductivity. We also find that a Planck-suppressed correction is extremely small under standard heavy-ion chemical freeze-out conditions. These results show that isotropic minimal-length modifications can change higher-order contributions to chiral vortical transport while leaving the familiar leading anomaly-related response unchanged within the DoS sector considered here.

</details>


### [102] [Freeze-in Asymmetric Dark Matter with a Dual-Role Scalar: CP Violation and Symmetric-Component Depletion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04354)
*S. Peyman Zakeri*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal freeze-in model for asymmetric fermionic dark matter, where a Dirac DM candidate ψ acquires its asymmetry via CP-violating decays of a scalar mediator φ, aided by interference with a heavier scalar S; the symmetric component is removed via S-mediated annihilation, and the model naturally reproduces the observed relic density while evading current experimental constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a predictive, minimal, and self-consistent freeze-in scenario for asymmetric fermionic dark matter that explains the observed relic abundance without conflicting with direct, indirect, collider, or self-interaction constraints.

Method: Extends the SM with a Z₂ × Z₂′-stabilized sector comprising Dirac fermions ψ (DM) and χ (auxiliary), real scalars φ (mediator) and S (heavy scalar); solves coupled Boltzmann equations for asymmetric yield Y₋ and total yield Y_Σ; derives and numerically verifies scaling relations for Y₋^∞ and Ω_DM h².

Result: Derives analytic scaling Y₋^∞ ∝ μ²ε/m_φ and Ω_DM h² ∝ m_ψ μ²ε/m_φ; reproduces Ω_DM h² ≈ 0.12 for benchmark parameters (ε = 10⁻⁶, m_φ = 200 GeV, μ ≈ 2.7×10⁻⁹ GeV, m_ψ ≈ 40 GeV); shows all predicted signals lie below current experimental sensitivities.

Conclusion: The model provides a viable, testable, and experimentally unconstrained realization of asymmetric fermionic dark matter within the freeze-in paradigm, with predictive parameter scaling and dual-role scalar S enabling both CP violation and symmetric-component depletion.

Abstract: We present a minimal, predictive scenario for asymmetric fermionic dark matter (DM) generated through freeze-in. The standard model (SM) is extended by a Dirac fermion $ψ$ that serves as the DM candidate, an auxiliary Dirac fermion $χ$, a real scalar mediator $φ$, and a heavier real scalar $S$, all stabilized by a $Z_2 \times Z_2'$ discrete symmetry. The DM asymmetry is generated through the CP-violating out-of-equilibrium decays of the mediator $φ$, while the heavier scalar $S$ plays a dual role: it generates the physical CP-violating phase required for the asymmetry through its interference with $φ$, and it mediates the annihilation of the symmetric component of the DM population. We solve the coupled Boltzmann equations for the asymmetric yield $Y_-$ and the total yield $Y_Σ$, and identify the scaling relations $Y_-^\infty \propto μ^2 \varepsilon / m_φ$ and $Ω_{\rm DM} h^2 \propto m_ψ\, μ^2 \varepsilon / m_φ$, which we verify numerically. The observed relic abundance $Ω_{\rm DM} h^2 \simeq 0.12$ is naturally reproduced for a wide range of parameters; in particular, for $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$ and $m_φ= 200$~GeV, the correct relic density is obtained for $μ\approx 2.7 \times 10^{-9}$~GeV and $m_ψ\approx 40$~GeV. We further confront the model with current experimental constraints, including direct detection, indirect detection, collider searches, and DM self-interactions, and show that the predicted signals lie far below the sensitivity of current experiments. This scenario provides a self-consistent and testable realization of asymmetric fermionic DM within the freeze-in framework.

</details>


### [103] [Pion gravitational form factors in an algebraic rainbow-ladder model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04386)
*Jiayin Kang,Lei Chang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the pion's gravitational form factors within the rainbow-ladder truncation of QCD, decomposing contributions from quark, dressed-quark gluon, and binding gluon components; it yields A(0)=1, D(0)=−1 in the chiral limit and momentum fractions ⟨x⟩_q=0.896, ⟨x⟩_g=0.104, with D-term partition mirroring the momentum fraction split.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To disentangle quark and gluon contributions to the pion's gravitational form factors—key probes of hadron mass and spin structure—in a controlled QCD-based framework.

Method: Rainbow-ladder truncation of the Dyson–Schwinger equations combined with an algebraic quark model featuring a dynamical basis; gravitational form factors are computed by coupling the energy-momentum tensor to quark and gluon components, separating them via operator insertion and scale-dependent decomposition.

Result: A(0) = 1 and D(0) = −1 in the chiral limit; quark and gluon momentum fractions are ⟨x⟩_q = 0.896 and ⟨x⟩_g = 0.104; the D-term satisfies the same quark–gluon partition at the model scale.

Conclusion: The pion’s mass distribution is dominantly quark-driven in this truncation, but gluons contribute non-negligibly to both momentum and mechanical properties (via D-term), affirming their essential role in hadronic gravitational structure even in light mesons.

Abstract: Within rainbow-ladder truncation, we identify three contributions to the pion's gravitational form factors---graviton coupling to the quark, to the gluon dressing the quark, and to the gluon binding the pion---and separate them into quark and gluon parts. Using an algebraic quark model with a dynamical basis, we obtain $A(0)=1$, $D(0)=-1$ in the chiral limit, and momentum fractions $\langle x\rangle_q=0.896$, $\langle x\rangle_g=0.104$. The D-term obeys the same partition at the model scale.

</details>


### [104] [Precision Electroweak Physics of Neutral Currents Below the Z-pole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04508)
*Jens Erler,Krishna S. Kumar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews current and upcoming precision measurements of weak neutral current interactions across multiple experimental platforms—including fixed-target scattering, atomic parity violation, and future collider experiments—with emphasis on low-momentum-transfer ($Q^2 \ll M_Z^2$) parity-violating electron scattering experiments underway at Jefferson Lab and Mainz.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Standard Model with high precision, particularly the running of the weak mixing angle $\sin^2\theta_W$ and possible new physics contributions at low energy scales, where sensitivity to beyond-Standard-Model effects may be enhanced.

Method: Analysis and comparison of experimental approaches: fixed-target neutrino/electron scattering, atomic parity violation spectroscopy, and planned parity-violating electron scattering (PVES) experiments using polarized electron beams on nuclear targets at JLab (e.g., P2, MOLLER) and Mainz (e.g., P2 experiment).

Result: Current constraints on $\sin^2\theta_W$ from low-$Q^2$ measurements show mild tensions with high-energy collider results; upcoming PVES experiments aim for sub-0.1% precision on the weak charge, improving sensitivity to new physics by 1–2 orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: Low-$Q^2$ weak neutral current measurements provide a unique, complementary probe to high-energy colliders for testing lepton universality, quark flavor structure, and potential $Z'$ or dark photon couplings; coordinated progress across atomic, nuclear, and collider frontiers is critical for robust interpretation.

Abstract: We report on the status and prospects of precision weak neutral current interaction measurements in fixed target scattering, atomic parity violation, as well as future collider measurements at momentum transfer $\sqrt{Q^2} \ll M_Z$, including the experiments in parity-violating electron scattering under construction at Jefferson Laboratory and at Mainz that are expected to commence data collection in the very near future.

</details>


### [105] [Boosted top tagging via lepton-in-jet topology for vector-like quark searches in FCC-hh](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05104)
*Sanchari Bhattacharyya,Biplob Bhattacherjee,Kirana K. K.,Swagata Mukherjee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a machine-learning framework using both physics-motivated DNNs and constituent-level Particle Transformer (ParT) to tag highly boosted leptonic top quarks (t→bℓν) at the 100 TeV FCC-hh, where decay products are highly collimated. Both taggers perform comparably on challenging backgrounds. The framework is applied to a B′→tW search, achieving 5σ discovery reach up to ~10 TeV depending on B′ width assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: At FCC-hh (100 TeV), highly boosted top quarks from t→bℓν produce non-isolated leptons embedded in jets; it is unclear whether constituent-based taggers retain discrimination power under such extreme collimation, necessitating evaluation and optimization for this regime.

Method: Two complementary ML approaches: (i) a deep neural network (DNN) trained on physics-motivated high-level observables, and (ii) a constituent-level Particle Transformer (ParT) operating directly on jet constituents; both optimized for non-isolated lepton topology; integrated into an event-level search for vector-like B′→tW.

Result: DNN and ParT achieve comparable tagging performance against hadronic top, b-, and c-jet backgrounds; the B′ search yields 5σ discovery reach of ~7 TeV for Γ_B′/m_B′ < 25% (with or without 10% systematic uncertainty), extending to ~10 TeV when Γ_B′/m_B′ ≲ 50%.

Conclusion: Constituent-based and variable-based taggers remain effective for leptonic top tagging at FCC-hh energies; the proposed framework enables sensitive B′ quark searches up to O(10) TeV, demonstrating viability of ML-driven jet substructure analysis in extreme-boost regimes.

Abstract: In this paper, we present a machine-learning-based framework for identifying leptonically decaying, highly boosted top quarks, $t\to b\ellν$, at the Future Circular Collider: hadron-hadron mode (FCC-hh) operating at a center-of-mass energy of $\sqrt{s}=100$~TeV. At these energies, the large Lorentz boost causes the top-quark decay products to become highly collimated, with the lepton embedded within the resulting top-quark jet. In this regime, it is not a priori clear whether constituent-based jet taggers, which rely on information from individual jet constituents, retain the high discrimination power achieved at LHC energies. To answer this question, we employ two complementary approaches: a variable-based tagger trained with a deep neural network (DNN) using physics-motivated observables and a constituent-based Particle Transformer (ParT) acting on the jet constituents. Both approaches are optimised for the characteristic non-isolated lepton signature of boosted leptonic top decays and achieve comparable performance in discriminating leptonic top jets from hadronic top jets and heavy-flavor $b$- and $c$-jet backgrounds. We then incorporate the tagging framework into an event-level search for single production of a vector-like $B^\prime$ quark decaying through $B^\prime\to tW$, and use it to evaluate sensitivity to $B^\prime$ in the range of $3$ to $10$~TeV. For the reference $B^\prime$ benchmark, the proposed search strategy achieves a $5σ$ discovery reach of approximately $7$~TeV under the width requirement $Γ_{B^\prime}/m_{B^\prime}<25\%$, in both the statistics-only and $10\%$ background systematic uncertainty scenarios. Relaxing the width requirement to $Γ_{B^\prime}/m_{B^\prime}\lesssim 50\%$ extends the discovery reach to approximately $10$~TeV.

</details>


### [106] [Violation and Restoration of Probability Positivity in Perturbative Quantum Field Theory: From Quantum Mechanics to Quantum Chromodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04557)
*Bin Gong,Xu-Dong Huang,Jian-Xiong Wang,Chao Zeng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the long-standing issue of negative decay/production rates in exclusive quantum chromodynamics (QCD) processes at higher orders (NLO/NNLO), caused by improper truncation of squared amplitudes. It argues that for colorless/electrically neutral exclusive processes, infrared divergences cancel *amplitude-by-amplitude*, making the full modulus-squared of the truncated amplitude—rather than its perturbative expansion—the physically correct and manifestly positive probability. The authors propose amplitude-level NRQCD factorization as a systematic solution, derive a universal effective form for truncated observables, and validate it with complete NNLO QCD calculations for multiple charmonium processes, confirming positivity and improved convergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the unphysical negative predictions arising from standard perturbative truncation of squared matrix elements in exclusive QCD processes—where large coupling and absence of KLN theorem invalidate the infrared-safe inclusive treatment used historically in QED.

Method: Amplitude-level NRQCD factorization; rigorous treatment of infrared cancellation at the amplitude level (not squared amplitude); derivation of an effective observable representation Γ^amp = Γ_LO |1 + α_s/π(c₁+ic₂) + (α_s/π)²(c₃+ic₄)|²; application to complete NNLO QCD calculations for J/ψ→3γ, Υ→3γ, e⁺e⁻→J/ψ+J/ψ, and eight exclusive charmonium production channels at B factories.

Result: All computed exclusive observables yield strictly positive decay/production rates at NNLO; improved perturbative convergence compared to conventional truncated expansions; validation of amplitude-level infrared safety for color-singlet exclusive processes.

Conclusion: Positivity in exclusive QCD processes is restored by defining probabilities as the modulus square of the truncated amplitude—not its truncated expansion—and amplitude-level NRQCD factorization provides a consistent, predictive, and physically sound framework for such processes.

Abstract: In quantum mechanics the modulus square of the wave function is the probability of an event --- positivity is self-evident. Quantum field theory corresponds to quantum mechanics one-to-one: the wave function corresponds to the matrix element, so the probability of a process should be the modulus square of the matrix element, positive by construction. In the founding era of perturbative quantum field theory, however, Feynman, Schwinger and Tomonaga treated inclusive processes in quantum electrodynamics, where the truncation of the squared amplitude was forced by infrared cancellations and rendered harmless by the small coupling $α\simeq1/137$. Carried over to exclusive processes in quantum chromodynamics, where the coupling is large, this practice produced negative decay and production rates already at NLO, persisting at NNLO. We point out that for exclusive processes, whose initial and final states are colorless for QCD and electrically neutral for QED, the infrared divergences cancel amplitude by amplitude and the KLN constraint is absent, so the complete modulus square of the truncated amplitude is the proper, manifestly positive definition of the probability. We formulate amplitude-level NRQCD factorization as the systematic prescription that restores positivity, show that every amplitude-level truncated observable admits the effective representation $Γ^{\mathrm{amp}}=Γ_{\mathrm{LO}}\bigl|1+\frac{α_s}π(c_1+ic_2)+\frac{α_s^2}{π^2}(c_3+ic_4)\bigr|^2$, and demonstrate, with the complete NNLO QCD corrections to $J/ψ\to3γ$, $Υ\to3γ$, $e^+e^-\to J/ψ+J/ψ$ and the eight exclusive charmonium production channels at $B$ factories, that the prescription yields positive and better-converging predictions.

</details>


### [107] [A strategy for early discoveries at LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05889)
*Alain Le Yaouanc,François Richard*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper argues that a 650 GeV excess at the LHC may correspond to a light Randall–Sundrum Kaluza–Klein graviton resonance—previously thought too heavy for LHC detection—and proposes optimized search strategies (e.g., including h(95)h(125), h(95)h(95), semi-leptonic/hadronic ZZ/WW) to enhance sensitivity and potentially confirm a KK graviton tower up to ~3 TeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reinterpret LHC excesses—especially the 650 GeV and 95 GeV anomalies—as signals of a light Randall–Sundrum KK graviton tower, challenging the conventional assumption that such states must be >2 TeV and are thus excluded by current searches.

Method: Critical reanalysis of LHC search methodologies, highlighting biases toward scalar resonances and inadequate treatment of spin-2 KK graviton phenomenology; proposal of revised event selections including non-standard Higgs pair final states (h(95)h(125), h(95)h(95)) and full reconstruction of ZZ/WW in semi-leptonic and hadronic channels.

Result: Demonstration that existing LHC analyses inadvertently suppress sensitivity to light KK gravitons due to scalar-centric cuts; quantitative expectation that optimized selections would significantly boost signal efficiency in ZZ, WW, and hh channels, enabling discovery-level significance with current data.

Conclusion: The 650 GeV excess is plausibly the first KK graviton of a Randall–Sundrum model; dedicated reanalysis—including extended Higgs-pair channels and spin-2–aware kinematic selections—can validate the full KK tower up to ~3 TeV and establish evidence for warped extra dimensions.

Abstract: Evidence for excesses observed at LHC tend to cluster mostly around 95 GeV and 650 GeV. We claim that a consequent interpretation of the 650 GeV and some additional indications calls for a Randall Sundrum sequence of Kaluza Klein graviton resonances. A common prejudice claims that such resonances should be very heavy, out of reach for LHC. We argue that this is no so. Although present LHC searches seem to exclude these particles below 2 TeV, we explain why this 650 GeV resonance could be one of them. We also explain why ongoing analyses ignore this scenario and apply selections which are only valid for scalar resonances. This could also apply to ongoing searches for Higgs pairs where the search for resonances decaying into SM Higgs pairs ignores the KK graviton scenario. We expect that with a correct treatment, one could very significantly increase the detection efficiency for ZZ and hh channels and achieve, with present data, a convincing proof of our expectations. Channels like h(95)h(125) and h(95)h(95) should be added to present selections. Such improvements should allow to reach a discovery. For the ZZ and WW channels, one can use semi-leptonic and purely hadronic final states, to confirm the tower of KK resonances indicated up to 3 TeV.

</details>


### [108] [Mixed-state quantum geometry of dense two-color QCD matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04848)
*Kouji Kashiwa,Riki Oshima,Hiroaki Kouno*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies information geometry—via the Bures metric and quantum Fisher information (QFI)—to thermal states in a two-flavor NJL model of dense QCD, revealing sharp enhancements near the color-superconducting phase boundary but only weak signatures at the chiral crossover and no discernible feature at the BEC–BCS crossover.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum phase transitions in dense QCD using information-geometric tools sensitive to quantum fluctuations and parameter sensitivity, beyond conventional order parameters.

Method: Construct thermal Gibbs states from mean-field Bogoliubov–de Gennes Hamiltonians in the two-flavor NJL model; compute the Bures metric and quantum Fisher information matrix on the (T, μ) thermodynamic manifold; numerically analyze trace(QFI) and metric-volume density.

Result: Strong, localized enhancements of both trace(QFI) and metric-volume density occur near the color-superconducting critical line; modest changes appear across the chiral crossover; no significant feature is found at the BEC–BCS crossover.

Conclusion: Information geometry—particularly QFI and Bures metric—serves as a sensitive detector of the color-superconducting transition in dense QCD, distinguishing it from chiral and BEC–BCS crossovers, thereby validating its utility for characterizing strongly correlated quantum matter.

Abstract: We investigate an information-geometric approach to dense two-color QCD using thermal states defined as normalized Gibbs states in the two-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model at the mean-field level. The Bures metric and quantum Fisher information are constructed from thermal states associated with effective Bogoliubov--de Gennes Hamiltonians reproducing the mean-field quasiparticle spectrum. On the thermodynamic parameter manifold $(T,μ)$, where $T$ is temperature and $μ$ is quark chemical potential, we analyze information-geometric quantities including the trace of the QFI matrix and the metric-volume density. The numerical results show pronounced enhancements of both quantities near the color-superconducting phase boundary, whereas only weaker signatures are observed across the chiral crossover. By contrast, neither observable exhibits a distinct feature associated with the BEC--BCS crossover.

</details>


### [109] [Exploring Anomaly-Free Dark Photon Models Through Solar Neutrino-Electron Scattering in Ton-Scale Dark Matter Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05377)
*Mehmet Demirci,Gürsel Kazıl,M. Fauzi Mustamin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives new exclusion limits for anomaly-free dark photon models using solar neutrino-electron scattering data from PandaX-4T and XENONnT, improving sub-MeV constraints beyond prior experiments and highlighting dependence on leptonic charge assignments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe low-mass dark photon models—particularly anomaly-free U(1)_X extensions with kinetic and mass mixing—using high-statistics, low-energy solar neutrino-electron scattering as a sensitive dark portal search channel.

Method: Statistical analysis of solar neutrino-electron elastic scattering data from PandaX-4T and XENONnT; derivation of exclusion limits in the (g_X, m_A') parameter space for six benchmark U(1)_X models; separation into model-independent pure kinetic and pure mass mixing scenarios.

Result: Novel, improved exclusion limits in the sub-MeV dark photon mass regime; stronger constraints correlated with specific leptonic charge assignments; surpassing previous limits from CHARM-II, TEXONO, and GEMMA.

Conclusion: Solar neutrino-electron scattering in ton-scale liquid xenon detectors provides a powerful, complementary probe of low-mass dark photons, especially for anomaly-free U(1)_X models with mixed kinetic–mass mixing, advancing the global dark sector search strategy.

Abstract: We study the phenomenological implications of anomaly-free dark photon models using low-energy solar neutrino-electron elastic scattering data from the ton-scale PandaX-4T and XENONnT experiments. Focusing on $U(1)_X$ gauge extensions that incorporate both kinetic and mass mixing, we derive novel exclusion limits across the parameter spaces of six benchmark models. Our analysis demonstrates that the stringency of these limits is directly correlated with the theoretical leptonic charge assignments of the respective models. Additionally, we provide model-independent limits by isolating the pure kinetic and pure mass mixing scenarios. In the sub-MeV mediator mass regime, our results meaningfully improve upon existing limits from previous neutrino-electron scattering experiments, such as CHARM-II, TEXONO, and GEMMA. Ultimately, these findings yield improved limits on low-mass dark photon models, demonstrating their complementarity with other ventures in the global search for dark portals.

</details>


### [110] [Likelihood \& Correlation Analysis of $π^o$ Electroproduction for Proton Tensor Charge Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05594)
*Saraswati Pandey,Douglas Q. Adams,Simonetta Liuti,Zaki Panjsheeri,Brannon Semp,Kemal Tezgin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a likelihood-based analysis of deeply virtual exclusive π⁰ electroproduction data to extract chiral-odd Compton form factors, proton tensor charge, and tensor anomalous magnetic moment using MCMC sampling within a Bayesian framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain chiral-odd generalized parton distributions (GPDs) via Compton form factors in deeply virtual π⁰ electroproduction, and to determine the proton tensor charge and tensor anomalous magnetic moment—key quantities for understanding quark orbital angular momentum and transverse spin structure.

Method: Joint likelihood analysis of cross sections and beam/target longitudinal spin asymmetries from JLab Hall A and B; local extraction of helicity amplitudes per kinematic bin; mapping to chiral-odd Compton form factors; Markov Chain Monte Carlo sampling for Bayesian parameter inference, uncertainty quantification, and correlation analysis.

Result: Extracted chiral-odd Compton form factors with quantified uncertainties and correlations; first determination of proton tensor charge and tensor anomalous magnetic moment from DVMP π⁰ data within a rigorous Bayesian framework; evidence that extended kinematic coverage and transverse polarization measurements would significantly improve precision.

Conclusion: The analysis demonstrates the feasibility and statistical robustness of extracting chiral-odd GPD-related observables from existing π⁰ electroproduction data, but highlights current limitations due to sparse kinematic and polarization coverage—motivating future experiments with broader acceptance and transverse spin observables.

Abstract: We present a likelihood analysis of observables in deeply virtual exclusive $π^o$ electro-production off the proton. We use experimental data for the cross section as well as the beam and target spin asymmetries measured in various longitudinal polarization configurations at Jefferson Lab Hall A and Hall B. Performing a local extraction, for each kinematic bin, we derive a joint likelihood for the various helicity amplitudes defining the different polarization configurations. We subsequently extract the Compton form factors that parameterize the underlying QCD scattering amplitudes in the chiral-odd sector. Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) methods are employed to explore the full parameter space, extracting the parameter uncertainties, correlations and credible intervals. The joint likelihood analysis of the cross-section and asymmetry data constrains the parameter space indicating that a larger kinematic coverage and additional polarization configurations, {\it e.g.} in the transverse sector, will improve the precision of the parameters. We also report a determination of the proton tensor charge and tensor anomalous magnetic moment extracted in a Bayesian statistical framework using deeply virtual exclusive scattering data.

</details>


### [111] [Spectra and nonleptonic decays of multiply heavy baryons in a finite-size quark-diquark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05721)
*Yongtao Yu,Tao Guo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies mass spectra and nonleptonic weak decays of doubly/triply heavy baryons using a nonrelativistic quark-diquark model with finite-size diquark corrections; predicts masses within 1.5% of lattice QCD, reproduces Ω_{cc}^+ mass consistent with LHCb, identifies residual spin-interaction uncertainty from hyperfine splitting discrepancies, and quantifies decay widths via factorization—finding strong suppression of b→c transitions (≈2 orders) due to smaller spatial overlaps and CKM hierarchy, and distinct phase-space and recoil effects in charm vs. bottom decays.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical predictions for doubly and triply heavy baryon masses and weak decays by incorporating finite-size diquark effects and consistent wave functions, bridging gaps between quark models, lattice QCD, and experimental data (e.g., LHCb's Ω_{cc}^+).

Method: Nonrelativistic quark-diquark model with generalized convoluted interaction for unequal-mass diquarks using mass-weighted coordinates and pairwise spin-spin couplings; Schrödinger equation solved for masses and wave functions; weak decays computed within factorization approximation at leading nonrelativistic order, distinguishing vector/axial contributions and |k|^3 kinematic dependence for c→s and b→c transitions.

Result: Predicted baryon masses agree with lattice QCD within 1.5%; Ω_{cc}^+ mass matches LHCb; hyperfine splittings are systematically ~few % smaller than lattice values; b→c decay widths suppressed ~100× relative to c→s due to smaller spatial overlaps and CKM hierarchy; kaon emission in 3/2^+→1/2^+ charm decays is further suppressed by |k|^3 kinematics; bottom decay ratios approach CKM × f_M scaling due to larger phase space.

Conclusion: The finite-size quark-diquark framework provides quantitatively reliable benchmark predictions for multiply heavy baryon spectroscopy and weak decays; recoil dynamics and diquark structure significantly impact decay widths and branching ratios, necessitating their inclusion for precision phenomenology beyond leading-order approximations.

Abstract: We investigate the mass spectra and two-body nonleptonic weak decays of ground-state doubly and triply heavy baryons in a nonrelativistic quark-diquark model. To account for the finite size of the diquark, we generalize the convoluted quark-diquark interaction to unequal-mass diquarks using mass-weighted constituent coordinates and distinct pairwise spin-spin couplings. The resulting Schr{ö}dinger equations provide the baryon masses and wave functions used consistently in the decay calculation. The predicted masses differ from existing lattice QCD results by less than $1.5\%$, and our calculated mass of $Ω_{cc}^{+}$ is consistent with recent LHCb observations. The calculated hyperfine splittings are systematically slightly smaller than the lattice values, indicating a residual uncertainty in the spin-dependent interaction. Within the factorization approximation, we evaluate the $c\to s$ and $b\to c$ transitions with pion or kaon emission. At leading nonrelativistic order, the vector contribution vanishes for the $3/2^+\to1/2^+$ transitions, while both vector and axial contributions enter the $3/2^+\to3/2^+$ channels. The $b\to c$ spatial overlaps are substantially smaller than their $c\to s$ counterparts. Together with the Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa hierarchy, this reduces the corresponding $b \to c$ width by approximately two orders of magnitude. In the $3/2^+\to1/2^+$ charm transitions, the leading $|\bm{k}|^3$ dependence enhances the suppression of kaon emission. In bottom transitions with a larger energy release, the smaller relative phase-space difference brings the ratios close to the scaling determined by the Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa elements and meson decay constants. These results provide benchmark estimates for future studies of multiply heavy baryons and quantify the role of recoil dynamics within the finite-size quark-diquark framework.

</details>


### [112] [Bakamjian--Thomas construction and light-front zero modes in pseudoscalar transitions: A comparison with the covariant Bethe--Salpeter model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05727)
*Ho-Meoyng Choi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates current-component independence for pseudoscalar-to-pseudoscalar semileptonic form factors in light-front dynamics using the Bakamjian–Thomas (BT) construction, benchmarking against an exactly solvable Bethe–Salpeter model; it demonstrates zero-mode–free (+,⊥) current extraction, proves analytical equivalence of f_−(q²) from (+,⊥) and (+,−) components for rotationally invariant wave functions, identifies a BT-specific interaction-dependent completion, and enables direct Gaussian D→K overlap evaluation across the full timelike q² range including zero recoil.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in zero-mode contributions and ensure current-component independence—critical for reliable light-front predictions of semileptonic form factors—by clarifying the dynamical role of zero modes and comparing BT-based LF quark models with covariant BS benchmarks.

Method: Analytical light-front field theory: direct zero-mode calculation from shrinking nonvalence region in a solvable BS model; construction of BT-based LF quark model with on-shell spinor currents and mass-operator–encoded interactions; analytical proof of current-component equivalence using LF Jacobian, pseudoscalar spin coupling, and rotational invariance; complex transverse contour integration for Gaussian overlaps.

Result: Demonstrated zero-mode–free (+,⊥) current-component extraction saturated by on-shell overlap; proved analytical equality of f_−(q²) from (+,⊥) and (+,−) projections for rotationally invariant radial wave functions under consistent invariant-mass treatment; identified an interaction-dependent BT completion distinct from BS endpoint contributions; enabled direct, parametrization-free evaluation of D→K form factors over full physical timelike q², including zero recoil.

Conclusion: Current-component independence is achieved within this specific BT realization of light-front dynamics; zero-mode assignments are not universal but depend on the dynamical structure of the model (e.g., BT vs. BS), and the BT framework—when consistently implemented—provides a robust, zero-mode–free, and fully predictive scheme for semileptonic form factors.

Abstract: We examine the role of the Bakamjian--Thomas (BT) construction in achieving current-component independence for pseudoscalar-to-pseudoscalar semileptonic form factors in light-front (LF) dynamics. An exactly solvable covariant Bethe--Salpeter (BS) model provides an off-shell benchmark. Its transverse-current zero-mode contribution is calculated directly from the shrinking nonvalence region and reproduced by an effective operator in the valence convolution. In the BT-based LF quark model, the one-body current kernel is constructed from on-shell constituent spinors, while interactions are encoded in the mass operator and wave functions. In this realization, the $(+,\perp)$ current-component extraction is zero-mode free and is saturated by the on-shell overlap.
