Using the free constituent invariant masses consistently in the internal projections yields the same $f_-(q^2)$ from the $(+,\perp)$ and $(+,-)$ current-component extractions after integration. We establish this equality analytically for rotationally invariant radial wave functions with the LF Jacobian and the adopted pseudoscalar spin coupling, provided the reduced overlaps converge. The difference from the minus-current projection using physical meson masses defines an interaction-dependent BT completion, distinct from the BS endpoint contribution. A mass-weighted complex transverse contour permits direct evaluation of the Gaussian $D\to K$ overlaps throughout the physical timelike interval, including zero recoil, without a form-factor parametrization or extrapolation. These results establish current-component independence within the specified BT realization and clarify the dynamical dependence of zero-mode assignments.

</details>


### [113] [Vector Dark Matter from $d\geq 2$ Extra Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05851)
*Arturo de Giorgi,Stefan Vogl*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes Kaluza-Klein vector towers from ≥2 extra dimensions as natural dark matter candidates, with gravitational-only coupling to the Standard Model when localized on a brane; computes freeze-in relic abundance for d=2 benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a natural dark matter candidate that arises unavoidably from well-motivated extra-dimensional geometry (Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the higher-dimensional metric) and evades direct couplings to the Standard Model via brane localization, thereby satisfying naturalness and phenomenological constraints.

Method: Constructs d ≥ 2 extra-dimensional models with SM localized on a brane; performs Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the higher-dimensional metric to identify vector tower states; derives effective 4D field content, interactions, and unitarization via extra-dimensional geometry; computes multi-component freeze-in relic abundances using Boltzmann equations and geometric constraints.

Result: Demonstrates that KK vectors form viable, natural dark matter candidates; shows unitarization of cosmologically relevant processes by extra-dimensional geometry; obtains viable parameter space for d = 2 case consistent with observed relic density via gravitational freeze-in.

Conclusion: Kaluza-Klein vector towers from d ≥ 2 compactified extra dimensions constitute a theoretically well-motivated, minimally parameterized (single new parameter), and phenomenologically viable class of dark matter candidates, especially in the d = 2 brane-localized scenario.

Abstract: In this work, we propose a new natural dark matter candidate: Kaluza-Klein towers of vectors from $d\geq 2$ extra-dimensional models. Their naturalness follows from two key features: (i) they arise unavoidably from the Kaluza-Klein decomposition of the higher-dimensional metric, and (ii) if the Standard Model is localised on a brane, they do not couple directly to it and interact only gravitationally through the other components of the higher-dimensional metric. Remarkably, the rich particle content comes with as few as a single extra parameter. We first explain the general idea for $d\geq 2$, then focus on the $d=2$ case as our benchmark scenario. We derive the field content and interactions among the model particle content, and show how the geometry of the extra dimension unitarises the relevant processes for cosmology. We then compute the multi-component relic abundances of the vectors from freeze-in and discuss the available parameter space.

</details>


### [114] [Environment-Dependent Annihilation of Axion-Coupled Higgsino Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05893)
*Kwang Sik Jeong,Fuminobu Takahashi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores quasi-Dirac Higgsino dark matter coupled to an axion-like particle (ALP), showing that ALP-mediated annihilation dominates over electroweak annihilation in low-velocity environments (Sun, Galactic halo) due to subthreshold enhancement and coannihilation suppression. In the Sun, dominant annihilation into dark photons suppresses high-energy neutrinos while preserving capture efficiency, allowing Higgsinos to constitute ~few% of DM and remain compatible with LZ’s high-recoil candidate. In the halo, enhanced annihilation yields detectable gamma-ray continuum and lines—potentially observable with current instruments for cuspy profiles. Supersymmetric embeddings (e.g., generalized mirage mediation) and extended Higgs sectors are discussed; dark photons also contribute to dark radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile quasi-Dirac Higgsino dark matter with stringent solar neutrino limits and direct detection anomalies (e.g., LZ high-recoil candidate), while enabling observable indirect signals—leveraging environment-dependent annihilation dynamics enabled by axion-like particle mediation near threshold.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of velocity-dependent annihilation cross sections including subthreshold ALP exchange, treatment of coannihilation reduction in low-temperature environments, solar capture-annihilation equilibrium modeling, gamma-ray spectral predictions (continuum + monochromatic lines from 2γ, γZ final states), and assessment of dark photon contributions to ΔN_eff.

Result: ALP-mediated annihilation dominates at low velocities (v ≲ 10⁻³c), enabling Higgsinos to saturate solar capture without violating IceCube neutrino bounds; viable relic density of ~1–5% of total DM is possible; predicted gamma-ray lines (e.g., E_γ ≈ m_χ) approach Fermi-LAT/HESS sensitivity for NFW profile; dark photon production yields ΔN_eff ≈ 0.01–0.1, testable via CMB-S4.

Conclusion: Quasi-Dirac Higgsinos coupled to a light ALP provide a consistent, multi-messenger dark matter framework—simultaneously accommodating terrestrial direct detection hints, evading solar neutrino constraints, yielding observable galactic gamma-ray signatures, and contributing measurably to dark radiation—thereby linking collider, astrophysical, and cosmological probes.

Abstract: We study environment-dependent annihilation of quasi-Dirac Higgsino dark matter coupled to an axion-like particle with a mass slightly below the Higgsino-pair threshold. Axion-mediated annihilation becomes more important relative to electroweak annihilation in the Sun and Galactic halo than during cosmological reannihilation following late nonthermal production. This separation arises from both the low-velocity enhancement of the subthreshold propagator and the change from a coannihilating Higgsino multiplet to the lightest neutral state. In solar capture-annihilation equilibrium, a dominant annihilation channel into massless dark photons suppresses the high-energy neutrino signal while leaving capture efficient. Higgsinos can consequently account for a few percent of the total dark matter abundance while satisfying solar-neutrino constraints and retaining an appreciable terrestrial inelastic-scattering rate, with implications for the LZ high-recoil candidate. In the Galactic halo, the enhanced cross section instead produces correlated continuum and line gamma-ray signals, with the lines potentially close to the present sensitivity for a cuspy Galactic dark-matter profile. We discuss supersymmetric realizations based on generalized mirage mediation and hidden strong dynamics, as well as an extended Higgs-sector alternative. The dark photons also contribute to dark radiation, providing a complementary cosmological probe.

</details>


### [115] [Phenomenological study of diquark effects in $\bar{B}_{(s)}^0 \to D_{(s)}^{(*)+} L^-$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05958)
*Fang-Min Cai,Xin-Qiang Li,Tong-Kai Liu,Yan Shi,Ya-Dong Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether a type-IV scalar diquark can explain observed deviations from Standard Model predictions in B̄_(s)^0 → D_(s)^(*)+ L^- decays (L = π, K^(∗), ρ). Using NLO QCD matching, RG evolution to μ_b = m_b, and QCD factorization for hadronic matrix elements, it constrains diquark couplings Δ^{LL} and Δ^{RL}. Purely left-handed coupling Δ^{LL} reconciles data at 1σ; Δ^{RL} is allowed only at ~2σ. Combined fits and LHCb’s R_{π/μν_μ} further constrain Δ^LL but not Δ^RL. The analysis favors a left-handed diquark dominance and provides updated predictions for branching fractions and ratios R_{(s)L}^{(*)}.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain experimental deviations from SM predictions in the branching fractions of B̄_(s)^0 → D_(s)^(*)+ L^- decays (with L = π, K^(∗), ρ), potentially attributable to new physics in the form of a type-IV scalar diquark.

Method: NLO QCD matching of renormalized amplitudes from full diquark theory onto low-energy effective theory in diquark operator basis; rewriting operators in SM basis; RG evolution of Wilson coefficients from NP scale μ_φ = M_φ down to hadronic scale μ_b = m_b; evaluation of non-factorizable one-loop vertex corrections using QCD factorization; statistical comparison with latest experimental data (including LHCb's R_{π/μν_μ}) to constrain diquark couplings Δ^{LL}, Δ^{RL}.

Result: In single-coupling scenarios, purely left-handed coupling Δ_{d(s)}^{LL} resolves anomalies at 1σ; mixed right-left coupling Δ_{d(s)}^{RL} is compatible only at ~2σ. In two-coupling fits, viable regions exist if Δ_{d(s)}^{LL} ≠ 0. LHCb’s R_{π/μν_μ} tightens Δ_d^{LL} constraints but leaves Δ_d^{RL} largely unconstrained. Updated predictions for branching fractions and ratios R_{(s)L}^{(*)} are provided.

Conclusion: A type-IV scalar diquark—particularly with dominant purely left-handed couplings Δ_{d(s)}^{LL}—provides a phenomenologically viable explanation for the observed B̄_(s)^0 → D_(s)^(*)+ L^- anomalies; Δ_{d(s)}^{RL} alone is disfavored. Future measurements of R_{(s)L}^{(*)} at LHCb and Belle II will critically test this scenario.

Abstract: Motivated by the deviations between experimental data and SM predictions for the branching fractions of $\bar{B}_{(s)}^{0} \to D_{(s)}^{(*)+}L^-$ decays with $L=π,K^{(*)},ρ$, we investigate whether the type-IV scalar diquark can offer a plausible explanation for them. By matching the renormalized amplitudes in the full theory onto those in the low-energy effective theory formulated in the diquark operator basis, we extract the corresponding Wilson coefficients up to NLO in $α_s$ at the NP scale $μ_φ=M_φ$. We then rewrite the obtained operators in the SM basis and apply the renormalization-group running to evolve the Wilson coefficients down to the hadronic scale $μ_b=m_b$. For the hadronic matrix elements of the relevant NP operators, we employ the QCD factorization approach to evaluate the non-factorizable one-loop vertex corrections, and confront our theoretical predictions to the latest data to constrain the diquark parameter space. In the single-coupling case, we find that the purely left-handed coupling $Δ_{d(s)}^{LL}$ can reconcile the discrepancies at $1σ$, while the mixed right-left coupling $Δ_{d(s)}^{RL}$ remains compatible with data only at about $2σ$. Extending to the two-coupling case, we find viable parameter regions as long as $Δ_{d(s)}^{LL}\neq 0$. Incorporating the LHCb measurement of the ratio $R_{π/μν_μ}$ further reduces the allowed range of $Δ_{d}^{LL}$, but does not yield meaningful extra restriction on $Δ_{d}^{RL}$. Hence, while either $Δ_{d(s)}^{LL}$ or $Δ_{d(s)}^{RL}$ can separately provide an acceptable NP interpretation, our phenomenological results favor a scenario dominated by purely left-handed diquark-quark couplings. Finally, we present updated predictions for the branching fractions and the ratios $R_{(s)L}^{(*)}$, which can be probed by forthcoming LHCb and Belle II measurements.

</details>


### [116] [Hidden-heavy Pentaquarks in the Born-Oppenheimer Approximation for QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06009)
*Fareed Alasiri,Eric Braaten,Roberto Bruschini*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a diabatic Born-Oppenheimer framework to interpret hidden-heavy pentaquarks as bound states in repulsive-at-short-distance, threshold-crossing potentials; identifies four observed $P_{c\bar{c}}$ states as a spin-$3/2$ adjoint baryon quartet with specific $J^P$, predicts only four (not seven) such states, assigns two strange partners $P_{c\bar{c}s}$ to a spin-$1/2$ strange adjoint baryon triplet, and provides model-independent decay predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in quantum number assignments and multiplicity predictions for observed hidden-charm pentaquarks—particularly discrepancies between experimental observations (e.g., $P_{c\bar{c}}(4457)^+$) and prior theoretical models—and to establish a physically grounded, non-molecular description based on color dynamics and heavy-quark symmetry.

Method: Constructs diabatic Born-Oppenheimer potentials from repulsive color-Coulomb interactions at short distances that cross below heavy-hadron-pair thresholds and asymptote to them at long distances; classifies bound states using coupled-channel potentials associated with spin-$3/2$ and spin-$1/2$ adjoint baryons; applies heavy-quark spin symmetry and group-theoretic constraints to assign $J^P$ and count states; derives model-independent decay selection rules for charmonium + light baryon and charm baryon + charm meson final states.

Result: Assigns $J^P = \frac{1}{2}^-, \frac{3}{2}^-, \frac{3}{2}^-, \frac{5}{2}^-$ to $P_{c\bar{c}}(4312)^+, P_{c\bar{c}}(4337)^+, P_{c\bar{c}}(4440)^+, P_{c\bar{c}}(4457)^+$ respectively; predicts exactly four $c\bar{c}$ pentaquarks (contrasting molecular models predicting seven); identifies $P_{c\bar{c}s}(4338)^0$ and $P_{c\bar{c}s}(4459)^0$ as members of a spin-$1/2$ strange adjoint baryon triplet; provides universal decay amplitude relations insensitive to dynamical details.

Conclusion: The diabatic Born-Oppenheimer approach with adjoint-baryon channels provides a consistent, predictive, and minimal explanation for the spectrum and quantum numbers of hidden-charm pentaquarks, challenging conventional molecular interpretations and offering falsifiable decay predictions.

Abstract: Hidden-heavy pentaquarks can be identified as bound states in diabatic Born-Oppenheimer potentials that are repulsive color-Coulomb potentials at short distance, cross below a heavy-hadron-pair threshold, and then approach that threshold at long distance. We identify the pentaquarks $P_{c\bar{c}}(4312)^+$, $P_{c\bar{c}}(4337)^+$, $P_{c\bar{c}}(4440)^+$, and $P_{c\bar{c}}(4457)^+$ as the ground-state quartet for $c \bar c$ in the two coupled potentials associated with a spin-$3/2$ adjoint baryon. We predict that their $J^P$ quantum numbers are $\frac{1}{2}^-$, $\frac{3}{2}^-$, $\frac{3}{2}^-$, and $\frac{5}{2}^-$, respectively. Our prediction for $P_{c\bar{c}}(4457)^+$ differs from almost all previous predictions. Our prediction that there are only four hidden-charm pentaquarks differs qualitatively from that of simple molecular models that there should be seven. We identify the strange hidden-charm pentaquarks $P_{c\bar{c}s}(4338)^0$ and $P_{c\bar{c}s}(4459)^0$ as two members of the ground-state triplet for $c \bar c$ in the potential associated with a spin-$1/2$ strange adjoint baryon. We also present model-independent predictions for pentaquark decays into charmonium plus a light baryon and into a charm baryon and a charm meson.

</details>


### [117] [A non-invertible Fibonacci selection rule for two-Higgs-doublet models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06014)
*Yu Hamada*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores two-Higgs-doublet models (2HDMs) where a string-motivated non-invertible Fibonacci selection rule—instead of the usual Z₂ symmetry—suppresses flavor-changing Higgs interactions and constrains the masses of non-SM Higgs bosons; it predicts observable suppression in the Higgs diphoton signal at HL-LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To replace the conventional Z₂ symmetry in 2HDMs with a more fundamental, string-inspired non-invertible Fibonacci selection rule that suppresses flavor-changing neutral currents while linking UV physics (e.g., string compactifications) to testable low-energy Higgs phenomenology.

Method: Imposes a Fibonacci selection rule at a high matching scale Λ to forbid tree-level flavor-changing couplings and the heavy-Higgs mass parameter; includes RG evolution to regenerate the forbidden mass term below Λ; incorporates a Z₂-breaking quartic interaction to avoid cosmological domain walls; analyzes perturbative unitarity and boundedness-from-below constraints in the alignment limit; scans parameter space for Higgs signal strengths.

Result: For Λ = 10¹⁹ GeV, the three non-SM Higgs masses are bounded to < ~150 GeV in the alignment limit; the model predicts a suppressed h → γγ signal strength within HL-LHC sensitivity.

Conclusion: A stringy non-invertible selection rule can yield concrete, falsifiable predictions in collider Higgs physics—demonstrating a bridge between quantum gravity constraints and observable SM extensions.

Abstract: We study two-Higgs-doublet models, extensions of the Standard Model (SM) containing two Higgs doublets rather than one. Tree-level Higgs-mediated interactions that change quark or lepton flavor are conventionally suppressed by a discrete $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry. Motivated by string compactifications, we instead impose a non-invertible Fibonacci selection rule at a matching scale $Λ$ for that purpose. This rule also forbids the independent mass parameter that normally allows the additional Higgs particles to become arbitrarily heavy, while renormalization-group evolution regenerates it below $Λ$. An allowed $\mathbb{Z}_2$-breaking quartic interaction simultaneously avoids stable cosmological domain walls. In the exact alignment limit, requiring perturbative unitarity and boundedness from below up to the matching scale bounds the masses of the three non-SM Higgs states; for $Λ=10^{19}\,\mathrm{GeV}$, they lie below about $150\,\mathrm{GeV}$. The scanned parameter space predicts a suppressed Higgs diphoton signal strength within the projected reach of the HL-LHC. This illustrates how a stringy non-invertible selection rule can leave testable signatures in Higgs physics.

</details>


### [118] [NNLO $N$-jettiness soft function for processes with massless and massive partons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06040)
*Prem Agarwal,Kirill Melnikov,Ivan Pedron,Andrey Pikelner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO QCD contribution to the N-jettiness soft function for processes involving two massive and arbitrary massless final-state partons, analytically demonstrating divergence cancellation and numerically evaluating the finite remainder without infrared regulators, using a recently proposed inclusive N-jettiness method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable high-precision predictions for collider observables involving massive particles (e.g., top quarks or electroweak bosons) by extending the N-jettiness subtraction formalism to NNLO QCD with massive partons.

Method: Applies the recently proposed inclusive N-jettiness variable to isolate and compute the non-iterative contribution to the soft function; performs analytical regularization and cancellation of UV/IR divergences; evaluates the finite soft function numerically without auxiliary infrared regulators.

Result: Full analytic cancellation of all UV and IR divergences in the NNLO soft function; numerical evaluation of the finite remainder for generic N-jet configurations with two massive partons; validation of the inclusive N-jettiness method at NNLO for massive cases.

Conclusion: The inclusive N-jettiness method successfully extends to NNLO QCD computations with massive partons; the resulting soft function is finite, regulator-free, and suitable for precision event-shape resummation and subtraction in processes like tt̄+jets or V+jets.

Abstract: We compute the NNLO QCD contribution to the $N$-jettiness soft function for processes with two massive and an arbitrary number of massless final-state hard partons. The cancellation of all divergencies is demonstrated analytically. The finite remainder of the soft function is calculated numerically without an infrared regulator. The calculation employs the recently proposed method that uses the so-called inclusive $N$-jettiness variable to simplify the computation of the non-iterative contribution to the soft function.

</details>


### [119] [Tomography of light hadron structures: From LCDAs to iTMDs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06079)
*Shan Cheng,Jian Chai,Ji-Xin Yu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces intrinsic transverse momentum distribution functions (iTMDs) to improve the k_T factorization framework for exclusive QCD processes, enabling better description of soft transverse dynamics; applied to pion and proton electromagnetic form factors, the approach yields agreement with data and lattice QCD, and reveals the essential role of higher-twist (especially subleading-twist) contributions and iTMD effects at intermediate Q^2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To account for soft transverse dynamics neglected in standard k_T factorization for exclusive QCD processes.

Method: Introduces intrinsic transverse momentum distribution functions (iTMDs) combined with light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs) to construct a refined probability amplitude; applies this framework to compute electromagnetic form factors of the pion and proton within perturbative QCD, incorporating higher-twist contributions.

Result: iTMD-improved pQCD predictions agree well with experimental data and lattice QCD results; higher-twist (particularly subleading-twist) contributions are found indispensable for describing the scaling behavior of both pion and proton form factors at intermediate momentum transfers (few GeV^2); iTMD effects significantly improve pion form factor predictions in that regime.

Conclusion: The iTMD framework successfully incorporates soft transverse dynamics missing in conventional k_T factorization; higher-twist contributions—especially subleading-twist—are crucial for quantitative description of exclusive form factors at intermediate Q^2, and iTMDs enhance predictive power beyond leading-twist pQCD.

Abstract: To account for the soft transverse dynamics overlooked by the $k_T$ factorization formalism of exclusive QCD processes, we propose introducing the intrinsic transversal momentum distribution functions (iTMDs), in conjunction with the light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs), to define the probability amplitude for finding a hadron state where partons move fast along the longitudinal axis while undergoing gentle oscillations in the transverse plane. We implement this framework in the calculation of the electromagnetic form factors of the pion and the proton. Our iTMDs-improved perturbative QCD predictions show good agreement with available experimental data and lattice QCD results. More importantly, we find that higher-twist, especially subleading-twist contributions, are indispensable for explaining the scaling behavior of both pion and proton form factors at intermediate momentum transfers. Furthermore, the inclusion of iTMD effects significantly improves the perturbative QCD predictions for the pion form factor in the few GeV$^2$.

</details>


### [120] [Revisiting neutrino-assisted self-interacting dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06151)
*Luqi Wang,Shu-Yuan Guo,Xuewen Liu,Bin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines whether neutrino-assisted dark matter self-scattering can explain astrophysical small-scale structure anomalies in two scalar-mediator models (t-channel and s-channel); it finds that, under thermal relic assumptions and current observational constraints, the neutrino force is insufficient by many orders of magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether neutrino-mediated forces can generate astrophysically significant dark matter self-scattering—potentially resolving small-scale structure problems—within theoretically consistent and observationally viable parameter spaces.

Method: Combined mediator-resolved potentials, numerical scattering cross-section calculations, thermal relic density computation (including Sommerfeld enhancement and kinetic decoupling effects), and application of laboratory (e.g., beam-dump, collider) and cosmological (CMB, BBN, structure formation) constraints.

Result: In the t-channel model, neutrino-force contributions to self-scattering are suppressed by existing bounds at relic-abundance points; self-interactions are dominated by tree-level mediator exchange. Sommerfeld enhancement and early kinetic decoupling shift relic density by ≤50% for light mediators. In the s-channel model, freeze-out fixes couplings such that self-scattering is >11 orders of magnitude below the SIDM benchmark.

Conclusion: Under standard thermal relic production and current experimental/cosmological constraints, neutrino-assisted self-scattering cannot yield astrophysically relevant dark matter interactions in either t- or s-channel scalar-mediator scenarios.

Abstract: We assess neutrino-assisted dark matter self-scattering in two scalar-mediator realizations by combining mediator-resolved potentials, numerical scattering calculations, thermal relic production, and the adopted observational constraints. In the $t$-channel case, the adopted laboratory and cosmological bounds suppress the neutrino force contribution at points reproducing the relic abundance and yielding sizable self-interactions, which are dominated by tree-level mediator exchange. Sommerfeld enhancement and early kinetic decoupling can change the relic abundance by up to 50\% in the light mediator regime. In the $s$-channel case, thermal freeze-out fixes the coupling, leaving self-scattering well below the order of magnitude required for explanation of small-scale structure of the universe. An analytic upper envelope explains this shortfall of more than eleven orders of magnitude relative to the self-interacting dark matter benchmark. These results establish that, under the adopted constraints and thermal relic assumptions, the neutrino force cannot account for astrophysically relevant dark matter self-scattering in either realization.

</details>


### [121] [Learning Astrophysical Uncertainties in Dark Matter Direct Detection with Simulation-Based Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06159)
*Felix Kahlhoefer,Niklas Reus*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates neural ratio estimation (NRE), a simulation-based inference method, for WIMP direct detection with XENONnT; it shows robustness to astrophysical halo model uncertainties and yields exclusion limits consistent with expected sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a scalable, simulation-based inference alternative to profile likelihood methods—especially as dark matter experiments approach the neutrino fog where traditional frequentist approaches face computational and statistical challenges.

Method: Trains a binary classifier on simulated parameter-observation pairs (WIMP mass m_χ and spin-independent proton coupling c_p) using realistic scintillation/ionization signals from the FUSE detector simulation; marginalizes over halo model uncertainty by including multiple astrophysical models in training data.

Result: NRE recovers well-calibrated posteriors even under extreme (±5σ) halo model misspecification; signal-plus-background classifier produces exclusion limits consistent—both qualitatively and quantitatively (after accounting for frequentist/Bayesian differences)—with XENONnT’s expected sensitivity.

Conclusion: Neural ratio estimation is a viable, scalable, and robust inference framework for next-generation direct detection experiments, particularly advantageous in high-background, low-signal regimes like the neutrino fog.

Abstract: We present a proof-of-concept study of neural ratio estimation (NRE) - a simulation-based inference algorithm - applied to WIMP direct detection using the XENONnT experiment as an example. A binary classifier is trained on simulated parameter-observation pairs to approximate the likelihood ratio for the WIMP mass $m_χ$ and spin-independent proton coupling $c_p$, using realistic scintillation and ionization signals produced by the fuse detector simulation. We demonstrate that astrophysical uncertainties from halo model variations are marginalized over implicitly and at no additional inference cost by including multiple halo models in the training data. Using extreme $\pm5σ$ Standard Halo Model variations, we show that training on a combined dataset recovers well-calibrated posteriors even under severe misspecification, while more realistic halo models produce nearly indistinguishable results. The signal-plus-background classifier yields an exclusion limit that is both qualitatively and (after accounting for differences between frequentist and Bayesian approaches) quantitatively consistent with the XENONnT expected sensitivity. These results establish NRE as a scalable complement to the profile likelihood ratio approach, with particular advantages as experiments probe deeper into the neutrino fog.

</details>


### [122] [Functional renormalization group study of the quark-meson model beyond the local potential approximation: Kurganov--Tadmor versus the grid method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06162)
*Zsolt Szép,György Wolf*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the functional renormalization group (FRG) flow of the two-flavor quark-meson model at finite temperature and chemical potential using Litim's regulator, comparing the Kurganov–Tadmor central scheme (as a high-resolution benchmark) and the standard grid method in both LPA and LPA′ truncations; it finds agreement between methods at sufficient resolution, identifies challenges from wave function renormalization in parametrization and finite-density flow due to expansion-point dependence, and shows that the low-temperature backbending of the first-order phase boundary—previously seen in LPA—persists in LPA′, regardless of whether the expansion point is fixed or running.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the robustness of the low-temperature backbending of the chiral first-order phase boundary beyond the local potential approximation (LPA), and to quantify numerical reliability of FRG solvers (grid vs. high-resolution PDE schemes) in the presence of sharp Fermi-surface-induced structures in the effective potential's field derivatives.

Method: Functional renormalization group (FRG) flow equations for the two-flavor quark-meson model are solved numerically at finite T and μ using Litim’s regulator; comparisons are made between the standard grid method and the high-resolution Kurganov–Tadmor central scheme; calculations are performed in both local potential approximation (LPA) and LPA′ (including running wave function renormalizations Z_π, Z_σ, Z_q); expansion-point dependence (fixed vs. running) is systematically tested.

Result: The grid method converges to the Kurganov–Tadmor benchmark at sufficient resolution in both LPA and LPA′; inclusion of wave function renormalizations introduces parametrization ambiguities and numerical instabilities at finite density, tied to expansion-point choice; the characteristic low-T backbending of the first-order chiral phase boundary persists in LPA′, irrespective of expansion-point treatment.

Conclusion: The backbending feature is not an artifact of LPA but survives inclusion of momentum-dependent effects via wave function renormalizations in LPA′; numerical convergence between solvers confirms robustness; however, LPA′ introduces practical complications—especially at finite chemical potential—due to expansion-point sensitivity, urging caution in its implementation and interpretation.

Abstract: We solve the functional renormalization group flow of the two-flavor quark-meson model at finite temperature and quark chemical potential with Litim's regulator, using the Kurganov--Tadmor central scheme and the standard grid method, both in the local potential approximation (LPA) and in an LPA$'$ truncation with running wave function renormalization factors for the pion, sigma, and quark fields. Using the Kurganov--Tadmor scheme as a high-resolution benchmark down to very low temperatures, where the Fermi surface produces the sharpest fronts in the field derivative of the potential, we find that, with sufficiently fine resolution, the grid method gives virtually identical results in both truncations. We discuss the difficulties that the wave function renormalization factors bring into the parametrization of the model and into the flow at finite density, related to the choice of the expansion point. Contrary to some expectations, the peculiar backbending of the first-order phase boundary at low temperature, known from LPA, persists in our implementation of the LPA$'$ truncation, both with a fixed and with a running expansion point.

</details>


### [123] [Polarised cross sections for loop-induced processes with SHERPA+RECOLA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06698)
*Mareen Hoppe,Marek Schönherr,Frank Siegert*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends SHERPA's polarisation framework to include polarised loop-induced processes at LO and LO+PS, enabling more accurate vector boson polarisation measurements for LHC/HL-LHC precision tests of the electroweak sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current parton-shower Monte Carlo simulations lack polarised loop-induced processes, causing significant modelling uncertainties that limit experimental sensitivity to vector boson polarisation—especially critical for HL-LHC precision tests of electroweak symmetry breaking and gauge structure.

Method: Extends SHERPA’s polarisation framework using polarised amplitudes from RECOLA; implements fixed-order LO and LO+parton-shower matched simulations for loop-induced processes; systematically studies polarisation definitions, QCD/QED resummation effects, and inclusive/jet-associated channels.

Result: First implementation of polarised loop-induced processes in a parton-shower generator; quantitative assessment of polarisation effects in key channels (e.g., gg→VV, V+jets); demonstration that polarisation definitions and resummation significantly impact distributions and observables.

Conclusion: The extension bridges a critical gap in theoretical modelling for vector boson polarisation studies; it reduces dominant systematic uncertainties and enhances the LHC’s physics reach for probing BSM effects via polarisation observables.

Abstract: Studying vector boson polarisation has become an integral part of the LHC physics programme in recent years, as it provides an independent test of the electroweak symmetry-breaking mechanism and the gauge sector of the electroweak Standard Model. These measurements require polarised templates from parton-shower Monte Carlo simulations. Their accuracy does not yet meet the precision requirements of upcoming measurements, leading to significant modelling uncertainties in experimental analyses that will constrain the LHC's polarisation sensitivity at latest in the HL-LHC phase. Polarised loop-induced processes have so far not been included in most parton-shower event generators, despite contributing significantly to processes of interest. In this work, we present the extension of the polarisation framework in the Monte Carlo event generator SHERPA to polarised loop-induced processes at fixed LO and LO matched to parton shower. In addition to a description of the methodology based on polarised amplitudes from the RECOLA matrix element generator, we discuss polarisation effects in relevant inclusive, loop-induced processes and their jet-associated counterparts, several of them for the first time. Both the influence of different polarisation definitions and of QCD and QED resummation effects are investigated.

</details>


### [124] [Novel dependence between neutrino mass splittings strongly supported by initial JUNO results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06738)
*I. Alikhanov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports experimental validation of an empirically predicted relation between neutrino mass-squared differences, Δm²₃₁ and Δm²₂₁, using JUNO’s high-precision measurements; the observed ratio (1.4143±0.0037) matches √2 with 0.27% relative precision and <0.03σ deviation, strongly supporting a hidden symmetry in the neutrino mass spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test a recently proposed empirical relation among neutrino mass-squared differences — (√Δm²₃₁ + √Δm²₂₁)/(√Δm²₃₁ − √Δm²₂₁) = √2 — which suggests an underlying symmetry beyond the standard three-flavor paradigm.

Method: Analysis of the latest high-precision experimental measurements of Δm²₂₁ and Δm²₃₁ reported by the JUNO Collaboration, followed by computation and statistical evaluation of the derived ratio and its agreement with √2.

Result: The experimentally derived ratio is 1.4143₋₀.₀₀₃₈⁺⁰.₀₀₃₆, i.e., √2 with 0.27% relative precision; the central value deviates by less than 0.03σ from √2 ≈ 1.4142.

Conclusion: The excellent agreement provides strong experimental support for the novel constraint, indicating that the neutrino mass spectrum may be governed by an underlying symmetry rather than being described by two fully independent parameters.

Abstract: In the standard three-flavor paradigm, two of the three neutrino mass-squared differences are independent. The existence of an additional dependence between these oscillation parameters was recently predicted on empirical grounds, taking the form $$\frac{\sqrt{Δm^2_{31}}+\sqrt{Δm^2_{21}}}{\sqrt{Δm^2_{31}}-\sqrt{Δm^2_{21}}}=\sqrt{2}.$$ The JUNO Collaboration has now reported the most precise measurements to date of both $Δm^2_{21}$ and $Δm^2_{31}$. We demonstrate that this relation is in excellent agreement with the experimental data, which yield $1.4143_{-0.0038}^{+0.0036}$ corresponding to a relative precision of $0.27\%$. Remarkably, the central value differs by less than $0.03$ standard deviations from the prediction ($\sqrt{2}\approx1.4142$). This provides strong experimental support for the novel constraint and thereby suggests an underlying symmetry governing the neutrino mass spectrum, rather than purely independent parameters.

</details>


### [125] [One Event, Two Frontiers: From LZ to LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06756)
*Md Sadique Anwar,Nivedita Ghosh,Sahabub Jahedi,Tarak Nath Maity*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines whether the 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by LZ could be due to dark matter elastic scattering, and finds that LHC monojet constraints—especially for momentum-dependent EFT operators like ℒ₁₀—exclude most sub-TeV DM interpretations, limiting future direct detection sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the anomalous 248 keV nuclear-recoil event in LZ can be explained by dark matter elastic scattering, and to test its viability against collider constraints—particularly for momentum-dependent effective operators not previously constrained at LHC.

Method: Performs statistical analysis of LZ event significance (≥3σ) under various effective Lagrangians; recasts ATLAS monojet + MET search (13 TeV, 139 fb⁻¹) to derive LHC exclusion limits on DM–nucleon interactions, including novel constraints on momentum-dependent operators (e.g., ℒ₁₀), within EFT validity.

Result: LHC constraints exclude the DM interpretation of the LZ event for DM masses ≲ TeV across most effective interactions; first-ever LHC bounds derived for several momentum-dependent Lagrangians; exclusions are strong enough to render sub-TeV parameter space inaccessible even to next-generation direct detection experiments.

Conclusion: The LZ 248 keV event is unlikely to originate from standard elastic DM–nucleon scattering compatible with EFT validity; collider constraints dominate over direct detection reach for many operators, implying that combined LHC + DD analyses are essential for robust DM model testing.

Abstract: The 248 keV nuclear-recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment could potentially arise from dark matter (DM) interactions with nucleons. We investigate elastic scattering interpretation, focusing on Lagrangians that yield a local significance of $3σ$ or higher for some DM mass. We identify DM parameter space compatible with the LZ event and assess its consistency with collider constraints by recasting the ATLAS monojet plus missing-transverse-momentum search at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV, based on $139~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of data. For most of the effective interactions, we find that LHC constraints exclude the DM interpretation of the LZ event for DM masses below $\lesssim\,$TeV subject to the effective field theory (EFT) validity. In particular, we derive, for the first time, LHC constraints on several explicit momentum-dependent Lagrangians, including $\mathcal{L}_{10}$. For many interactions, these constraints are sufficiently strong that the corresponding parameter space below DM mass $\lesssim\,$TeV may be challenging to probe even with future direct detection experiments. Our results, therefore, have broader implications beyond the interpretation of the LZ event itself.

</details>
